# EOS Quark Java Engine — VERIFIED Validation Issue List (Phase 0 ground-truth reconciled)
> Every issue below was re-checked against the **actual .NET source** and the **actual Java working copy** by an independent reconciliation agent; all CRITICAL/HIGH items additionally passed an adversarial skeptic re-check. Each carries a verbatim Java quote and .NET quote so it is auditable.

## Status of the original 95
- **93 confirmed real** (8 CRITICAL · 20 HIGH · 29 MEDIUM · 36 LOW)
  - **64 SOUND** — bug real AND the originally-proposed fix is correct.
  - **29 FIX_ADJUSTED** — bug real but the original fix was wrong/incomplete; a corrected fix is given (marked ⚠️).
- **2 WITHDRAWN** — Java already matches .NET; NOT a real defect (listed at the end with the reason).

---

## CRITICAL

### [#0] Degrade mode skips uploading the gabarit to the QXPS pool, so the degraded Render has no document to render  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:158-164`
- **Area:** pipeline (ProcessRunServiceImpl / Run lifecycle)
- **Scenario:** Gabarit data length exceeds engine.gabarit.size-limit-before-fail-soft. prepareGabarit() sets modeDegrade=true and returns early BEFORE calling filePoolPort.addFile(...). runProcessor then skips steps 3-6 and calls qxpsCallerService.render(run, ...), which uses run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath() as the QXPS DocumentName. Since the gabarit was never uploaded to the pool, the render call references a document that does not exist in the pool → render fails / produces no JPG/PDF/QXP, so a degraded run yields no output at all.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:159-167 — `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { log.warn(...); this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); return; // Return early — skip file pool operations and DID parsing in degraded mode }` then below `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());`. Render path: QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197 `String documentName = run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath();`. Orchestrator ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:69 `if (!run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade()) {...}` gates steps 3-6 but line 94 `qxpsCallerService.render(run, ...)` runs unconditionally outside the guard.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run.cs:92,94,97-100; Run_Base.cs:110-124,915-926` — Run.cs:89-100 — `this.Gabarit = __doc_gabarit; QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data); this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity(); if (!this.Mode_Degrade){ Trace_Helper.Log_Message(...); }` — Addfile and Evaluate_Document_Identity are UNCONDITIONAL; no degrade branch/early-return exists. Run_Base.cs:110-124 — `Launch_Load(); if (!this.Mode_Degrade){ Launch_Prepare(); Launch_Process(); Launch_Process_Steps(); Launch_Check(); } Launch_Render();` — Load (which uploads) and Render always run. Run_Base.cs:915-926 — Mode_Degrade is a lazily-computed property (Gabarit.Mode_Degrade), never assigned as a side effect.
- **Fix:** In Run.prepareGabarit, perform the pool upload unconditionally before the degrade short-circuit so the degraded render has a pooled document, matching .NET where Addfile (and Evaluate_Document_Identity) run unconditionally. Concretely: move `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData())` ABOVE the size-limit check, then for the size-limit branch set modeDegrade=true and return (skipping only the DID fetch/parse steps). Example:
```java
if (this.gabarit == null) { return; }
// Always upload the gabarit to the QXPS pool — required even in degrade mode (parity: .NET Addfile is unconditional)
filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());
if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) {
    log.warn(...);
    this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true);
    return; // degrade: skip DID fetch/parse only
}
String xmlContent = documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFileName(), "DID");
String didValue = documentIdentityPort.getElementValueByIdName(xmlContent, "DID");
this.gabarit.setDocumentIdentity(documentIdentityPort.parseDocumentIdentity(didValue));
```
Note: .NET also calls Evaluate_Document_Identity() unconditionally inside PrepareGabarit (only the trace log is degrade-guarded); skipping the DID fetch/parse in degrade mode is a defensible deviation since the DID *task* is skipped in degrade anyway, but the strictest-parity option is to run the DID fetch/parse unconditionally too. The load-bearing fix is the unconditional pool upload. Also verify render robustness: render reads gabarit.getFilePoolPath() as the doc name — ensure fileName used for addFile and filePoolPath used by render resolve to the same pool key.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java prepareGabarit early-returns on the size-limit branch BEFORE filePoolPort.addFile, while the orchestrator still runs render unconditionally; render uses gabarit.getFilePoolPath() as the QXPS doc name, so a degraded run renders against a never-uploaded pool document → no output. .NET runs Addfile (Run.cs:92) and Evaluate_Document_Identity (94) unconditionally; only the trace log is degrade-guarded, and Mode_Degrade is a computed property with no PrepareGabarit branch. The proposed fix moves addFile above the size check and only short-circuits the DID fetch/parse in degrade — this restores the unconditional pool upload and matches .NET render semantics. It compiles (getFileName/getData are existing Lombok getters, already used at line 167). Skipping DID fetch/parse in degrade is a defensible deviation (DID task is skipped in degrade in .NET too); the load-bearing upload is correct. Severity CRITICAL confirmed: degraded run yields zero output.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java Run.java:159-163 sets modeDegrade=true and returns BEFORE filePoolPort.addFile at line 167; ProcessRunServiceImpl gates steps 3-6 on !isModeDegrade (line 69) but always runs render (lines 93-95). Render uses run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath() as doc name (QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197), so a degraded run renders an un-uploaded document. .NET truly differs: Run.cs:92,94 run Addfile and Evaluate_Document_Identity UNCONDITIONALLY, only the trace log is degrade-guarded (97); no early return. Mode_Degrade (Run_Base.cs:915-926) is a computed property, never assigned; Run_Base.cs:110-124 always runs Launch_Load and Launch_Render. The fix (move addFile above the size check) compiles—all symbols exist—and is local to load(), breaking no call sites. The fix note already flags the getFileName vs getFilePoolPath pool-key concern.

### [#1] Combined store type SQL|DOCUMENT (0x03) collects NOTHING — StoreDataType modeled as a single-value enum, not bit flags  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/impl/CheckServiceImpl.java:61-71`
- **Area:** CheckServiceImpl — overflow detection, re-processing, SQL/document data collection, gabarit XML refresh timing
- **Scenario:** A run configured with store type = 3 (both SQL and DOCUMENT, a valid [Flags] combination in the original). check() does storeType.hasFlag(SQL) and storeType.hasFlag(DOCUMENT).
- **Java is (verified):** StoreDataType.java:30-37: `public static StoreDataType fromCode(int code) { for (StoreDataType type : values()) { if (type.code == code) { return type; } } return NONE; }` (no constant has code==3, so returns NONE). RunPropertiesMapper.java:84-86: `intVal = rs.getInt("store_data_type"); props.setStoreDataType(rs.wasNull() ? StoreDataType.NONE : StoreDataType.fromCode(intVal));`. CheckServiceImpl.java:62 `if (storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.SQL))` and :67 `if (storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.DOCUMENT))` — both false when storeType==NONE since hasFlag is `(0 & flag)==flag`. Same pattern in EndRunBusiness.java:138,150.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Enums/Store_Data_Type.cs:14-33; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:677,699; Proxy_Run.cs:151-153` — Store_Data_Type.cs:14 `[Flags] public enum Store_Data_Type { NONE=0x00, SQL=0x01, DOCUMENT=0x02, ALL=0xFF }`. Run_Base.cs:677 `if ((this.Properties.Store_Type & Store_Data_Type.SQL) == Store_Data_Type.SQL)` and :699 `if ((this.Properties.Store_Type & Store_Data_Type.DOCUMENT) == Store_Data_Type.DOCUMENT)` — two independent bit tests, both true for value 0x03. Proxy_Run.cs:151-153 `int __id_Store_Type = __odr["Store_Data_Type"]; ... __run_Properties.Store_Type = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToEnum<Store_Data_Type>(__id_Store_Type);` — converting int 3 to a [Flags] enum preserves the raw value 3.
- **Fix:** Stop collapsing non-enumerated bit combinations to NONE. The bitwise hasFlag logic is already correct; only the int->enum conversion is wrong. Minimal fix: change RunPropertiesMapper to not depend on a named constant for the combined value. Two clean options: (1) Carry the raw int. Store store_data_type as an int on RunProperties and have CheckServiceImpl/EndRunBusiness test `(storeTypeCode & StoreDataType.SQL.getCode()) == StoreDataType.SQL.getCode()` (and DOCUMENT) directly against the raw value — exact .NET parity. (2) Keep the enum-style API but make it bit-aware: replace fromCode's exact-match loop so that for any value where no single constant matches, it still preserves the bits. Cleanest is to model the run's store type as an EnumSet<StoreDataType> (or a small flags wrapper) decomposed from the int: `if ((code & SQL.code)==SQL.code) set.add(SQL); if ((code & DOCUMENT.code)==DOCUMENT.code) set.add(DOCUMENT);` then check `set.contains(SQL)` / `set.contains(DOCUMENT)`. Whichever approach, fromCode() must NOT silently return NONE for a non-zero, non-enumerated value — at minimum it should be removed from the SQL/DOCUMENT decision path. Apply the same fix to BOTH call sites (CheckServiceImpl.check lines 61-71 and EndRunBusiness.insertDataStorage lines 135-156) since they share the broken collapsed value. Add a unit test asserting that store_data_type=3 enables both SQL and DOCUMENT collection.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple fully verified from source. .NET is a [Flags] enum doing two independent bit tests at Run_Base.cs:677/699; value 0x03 passes both. Java fromCode(3) collapses to NONE, so both SQL and DOCUMENT collection AND persistence are silently skipped — total silent data loss for a valid combined config (ALL=0xFF still works, only the discrete 3 is broken). Root cause (fromCode collapsing non-enumerated combos to NONE) is correctly identified and severity CRITICAL is justified. Marked SOUND: the finding prescribes at least two fully correct, implementable approaches. adjustedFix only clarifies that the literal option-(2) wording ("fromCode preserves bits and returns a StoreDataType") is not realizable on a plain enum; pick the raw-int or EnumSet path.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java StoreDataType.java:30-37 fromCode loops named constants and returns NONE for 3; RunPropertiesMapper.java:85-86 feeds DB value 3 to fromCode, yielding NONE; CheckServiceImpl.java:62,67 and EndRunBusiness.java:138,150 then both hasFlag tests fail. .NET Store_Data_Type.cs:14-33 is [Flags] (SQL=0x01,DOCUMENT=0x02); Run_Properties.cs:366 types Store_Type as the enum, so Proxy_Run.cs:153 ToEnum(3) preserves raw bits and Run_Base.cs:677,699 two independent (&)==tests both pass. Reconciler fix (raw int or EnumSet at both cited sites) compiles and matches .NET. Note: two further sites exist beyond finding scope — ProcessSqlBusiness.java:63 (also broken by NONE) and DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:252-254 (uses == equality, independently broken for value 3); option (a) raw-int fix would also repair these.

### [#2] Anchor resolution is a stub returning empty DBlocInfo -> every INCORPORATE render throws 'Anchor not found'  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/task/TaskAnchor.java:45-47 (getBlocInfo stub); triggered via 63-76 getStartAnchor/getEndAnchor and used by service/task/impl/CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:283-284`
- **Area:** compartiment-task
- **Scenario:** Any TaskCompartiment in INCORPORATE or GENERATE_AND_INCORPORATE mode (the normal incorporation flow). renderRuns() calls task.getStartAnchor() (line 283) -> getAnchorInfo(true) -> getBlocInfo(name) which returns new DBlocInfo() with uid="". getAnchorInfo then sees uid.isBlank()==true and throws IllegalStateException('Anchor not found in document'). The task processing aborts; no pages/anchors are produced.
- **Java is (verified):** TaskAnchor.java:44-47 `protected DBlocInfo getBlocInfo(String blocName) { return new DBlocInfo(); }`. TaskAnchor.java:37-38 `if (dblocInfo == null || dblocInfo.getUid() == null || dblocInfo.getUid().isBlank()) { throw new IllegalStateException("Anchor not found in document: " + anchorName); }`. Never overridden: TaskCompartiment.java:13 and TaskDynamique.java:17 declare no getBlocInfo. Reached via CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:283-284 getStartAnchor()/getEndAnchor() and DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:596+.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Task/Task_Base.cs:175-179; Task_Anchor.cs:90-100` — Task_Base.cs:177 `DBloc_Info __dbloc_Info = this.Run.Gabarit.XML.GetBlocInfo(boxName);` returned at :178. Task_Anchor.cs:93 `DBloc_Info __dbloc_Info = base.Get_Bloc_Info(__anchorName);` then :95-98 `if(QXPFrk.Validation.IsNotSet(__dbloc_Info.UID)) { throw new Exception_Task(Exception_Task.MSG_Ancre_Introuvable, __anchorName); }`.
- **Fix:** Replace the stub in TaskAnchor.getBlocInfo to delegate to the parent run's gabarit XML, mirroring .NET Task_Base.Get_Bloc_Info and the existing TaskBase.java:57-58,73 pattern:

  protected DBlocInfo getBlocInfo(String blocName) {
      return this.getRun().getGabarit().getQxpXml().getBlocInfo(blocName);
  }

This can live directly in TaskAnchor (no per-subclass override needed) since the delegation is generic. Preconditions to verify: the parent run's gabarit DocumentDomain and its QxpXml must be loaded before Process (Run.prepareGabarit must have run); add a null-guard / clear error (e.g. RunError or IllegalStateException naming the missing gabarit) if getRun().getGabarit() or getQxpXml() is null so the failure is diagnosable rather than an NPE. After this, getAnchorInfo's existing isBlank() check correctly reproduces .NET's "Ancre Introuvable" behavior only when the real XML lookup yields no UID. No change needed to getAnchorInfo, getStartAnchor/getEndAnchor, or the strategies.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple from source. Java TaskAnchor.getBlocInfo is a placeholder returning empty DBlocInfo (uid=""), never overridden by either subclass, so getAnchorInfo's isBlank() check always throws and aborts every INCORPORATE/GENERATE_AND_INCORPORATE and dynamic-report flow. .NET resolves via Run.Gabarit.XML.GetBlocInfo (a real lookup), throwing only on a genuinely missing UID. The proposed fix `return this.getRun().getGabarit().getQxpXml().getBlocInfo(blocName);` exactly mirrors .NET and compiles: Run.gabarit is DocumentDomain (Run.java:30), DocumentDomain.getQxpXml() returns QxpXml (DocumentDomain.java:104), QxpXml.getBlocInfo is the faithful port (QxpXml.java:357). The same chain is already used at TaskBase.java:57-58,73 and DidTaskPostProcessStrategy.java:68. The optional null-guard is defensive hardening, not required for parity, and does not alter behavior. Fix is sound as written; CRITICAL severity stands.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java TaskAnchor.getBlocInfo (lines 45-47) is a stub returning new DBlocInfo() (empty UID); getAnchorInfo (37-38) throws IllegalStateException when UID isBlank. Neither TaskCompartiment (no override) nor TaskDynamique (no override) override it; grep found no other override. Reached via getStartAnchor/getEndAnchor at CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:283-284. .NET Task_Base.cs:177 delegates to Run.Gabarit.XML.GetBlocInfo — a real lookup; Task_Anchor.cs:95-98 throws only when real lookup yields no UID. Fix delegating to getRun().getGabarit().getQxpXml().getBlocInfo is correct: QxpXml.getBlocInfo (line 357) exists, sets UID; identical chain already used at DidTaskPostProcessStrategy.java:68 and TaskBase.java:57-58,73. Compiles, matches .NET, breaks nothing.

### [#4] Insert_Run_Errors p_messages/p_categories bound as OracleTypes.ARRAY against PL/SQL associative arrays — runtime failure persisting run errors  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/EndRunDaoImpl.java:88-105 (insertRunErrors)`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** At run finalization when errors exist, insertRunErrors(idRun, String[] messages, int[] categories) is called; arrays bound as OracleTypes.ARRAY 'QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY' and 'QXP_PK_COMMON.NUMBERARRAY' with a raw String[] and int[].
- **Java is (verified):** EndRunDaoImpl.java:96-97: new SqlParameter("p_messages", OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY"), new SqlParameter("p_categories", OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.NUMBERARRAY"); EndRunDaoImpl.java:102-103: params.put("p_messages", messages); params.put("p_categories", categories); (raw String[]/int[] via SimpleJdbcCall)
- **.NET is (verified):** `Proxy_Error.cs:127-129; ora.txt:9447-9457; QXP_PK_COMMON.sql:11-12` — Proxy_Error.cs:128-129: .AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_messages", __msgs.ToArray()).AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_categories", __cats.ToArray()); ora.txt:9450-9451: p_messages IN qxp_pk_common.VarCharArray, p_categories IN qxp_pk_common.NumberArray; QXP_PK_COMMON.sql:11-12: TYPE VarCharArray IS TABLE OF VARCHAR2(4000) INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER; TYPE NumberArray IS TABLE OF NUMBER INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER;
- **Fix:** Bind both params as Oracle PL/SQL index-by tables, not SQL ARRAY. SimpleJdbcCall + OracleTypes.ARRAY cannot do this; drop to the Oracle JDBC index-table API. Use a JdbcTemplate CallableStatementCreator (or ConnectionCallback) over the raw connection: prepare "{ call QXP_PK_RUN.Insert_Run_Errors(?,?,?) }", unwrap to OracleCallableStatement, then setInt(1, idRun); ((OraclePreparedStatement)cs).setPlsqlIndexTable(2, messages, messages.length, messages.length, OracleTypes.VARCHAR, 4000); and for categories convert int[] to an Integer[]/Number[] (or use setPlsqlIndexTable(3, integerArr, len, len, OracleTypes.NUMERIC, 0)). Mirror .NET's null handling by replacing null/empty message elements with the "aucun" sentinel and unset categories with appropriate defaults before binding. Keep the existing early-return guard. Apply the identical setPlsqlIndexTable pattern to InsertDataStorageDaoImpl (p_names/p_values/p_descriptifs/p_infos as VARCHARARRAY).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. The stored proc params are PL/SQL package-local associative arrays (INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER), which are NOT schema-level SQL collection types. No VARCHARARRAY/NUMBERARRAY SQL type exists, so SimpleJdbcCall + OracleTypes.ARRAY + type-name resolution fails at runtime ("invalid name pattern"). .NET passes string[]/int[] through ODP.NET's OraParameter(string,object), which binds them as PL/SQL associative arrays — correct. Java diverged to a SQL-ARRAY path that cannot work; error persistence throws and masks the original failure (reached at run finalization when errors exist; only guard is empty/null at line 89). Fix is correct: drop to OraclePreparedStatement.setPlsqlIndexTable over the raw connection with proper param indices (1=idRun,2=messages,3=categories), convert int[]->Integer[] for the numeric table, and apply the "aucun" sentinel/null handling to mirror Proxy_Error.cs:111-118. setPlsqlIndexTable args (maxLen,curLen,elemSqlType,elemMaxLen) are accurate. Sibling InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.java:43-46 confirmed to repeat the pattern (separate fix). Severity CRITICAL: whole QXP_RUN_ERROR persistence path is broken.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed all claims. Java EndRunDaoImpl.java:96-97 binds p_messages/p_categories as new SqlParameter(OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY"/"NUMBERARRAY") via SimpleJdbcCall, stuffing raw String[]/int[] (lines 102-103). QXP_PK_COMMON.sql:11-12 declares both as TYPE ... IS TABLE OF ... INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER (PL/SQL associative arrays), not SQL-bindable named ARRAY types; Insert_Run_Errors (ora.txt) consumes them via FORALL. .NET differs: Proxy_Error.cs:128-129 passes __msgs.ToArray()/__cats.ToArray() into OraParameter overloads (OracleParameter.cs:446,460) that call UpdateArrayParameterStatus, setting CollectionType=PLSQLAssociativeArray (line 609) with DBNull element handling. So .NET correctly binds associative arrays; Java's SQL-ARRAY path fails at runtime. Fix using setPlsqlIndexTable on unwrapped OraclePreparedStatement is the canonical correct remedy and matches .NET semantics.

### [#5] Gabarit uploaded to QXPS pool under getFileName() but referenced everywhere downstream by getFilePoolPath()  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:167 (and 170)`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Every run, all gabarit sources. prepareGabarit() uploads with filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), ...) -> pool key 'G_45_1.qxp'. But QxpsCallerBusiness.process()/render() and CheckServiceImpl use run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath() -> 'R_5/G_45_1.qxp' as the QXPS DocumentName.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:167 `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());` and Run.java:170 `String xmlContent = documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFileName(), "DID");`. Downstream QxpsCallerBusiness.java:106 `String currentDocName = gabarit.getFilePoolPath();`, :197 `String documentName = run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath();`, CheckServiceImpl.java:89 `String documentName = gabarit.getFilePoolPath();`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `Run.cs:92; QXPS_File_Manager.cs:235-240; Run_Base.cs:954; Document.cs:430` — Run.cs:92 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data);`. QXPS_File_Manager.cs:238 `if(addFile) Addfile(doc.FilePoolPath, doc.Data);` and :240 `QXP_XML __xmlQXP = QXP_XML.Create_From_File(doc.FilePoolPath);` (Get_XML, called via Document.cs:430 Get_XML(this) -> Get_XML(doc,true)). Run_Base.cs:954 `__info.DocumentName = this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath;`.
- **Fix:** In Run.java change both call sites to use the pool path, matching .NET Addfile(FilePoolPath,...) and Get_XML(FilePoolPath):
- Line 167: filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), this.gabarit.getData());
- Line 170: documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), "DID");
This makes the upload key, the DID/XML fetch, and all downstream Process/Render/Check references (QxpsCallerBusiness:106/197, CheckServiceImpl:89) and the FilePoolService dedup/inform set all key on the same "R_<runId>/G_45_1.qxp" string. No other change needed; preparePaths already populates filePoolPath before prepareGabarit's pool operations run.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed by reopening all sources. .NET consistently keys the gabarit on FilePoolPath: the pool upload (Run.cs:92), the DID/XML fetch (Get_XML re-adds and reads via doc.FilePoolPath, QXPS_File_Manager.cs:238/240), and the QXPS DocumentName (Run_Base.cs:954). The Java port diverged only at Run.java:167 and :170 by using getFileName() ("G_45_1.qxp") instead of getFilePoolPath() ("R_<runId>/G_45_1.qxp"), while every downstream consumer (QxpsCallerBusiness:106/197, CheckServiceImpl:89) addresses the document by getFilePoolPath(). Result: document registered under one pool key, addressed under another -> QXPS cannot locate it during Process/Render -> silent no-output every run, all gabarit sources, non-degraded mode. The proposed fix (swap both call sites to getFilePoolPath()) exactly restores .NET parity, compiles cleanly (same String type, paths populated by preparePaths before prepareGabarit runs), and unifies upload key, DID fetch, and downstream references on the same string. SOUND; CRITICAL confirmed.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed in both sources. Java Run.java:167 uploads via filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(),...) and :170 fetchXmlForBox(getFileName(),"DID"); getFileName()="G_..qxp" while getFilePoolPath()="R_<runId>/G_..qxp" (RunProperties.getPoolPath, set by GetGabaritBusiness.preparePaths before prepareGabarit). FilePoolService.addFile and DocumentIdentityService.fetchXmlForBox both pass the name straight to qxpsHttpClient.execute as the pool key; downstream Process(QxpsCallerBusiness:106), Render(:197), Check(CheckServiceImpl:89) all key on getFilePoolPath(), so registration/addressing mismatch. .NET differs: Run.cs:92 Addfile(Gabarit.FilePoolPath,..), Document.Get_XML uses doc.FilePoolPath (235-240), Run_Base.cs:954 DocumentName=FilePoolPath; FilePoolPath includes R_ prefix. Fix to getFilePoolPath() compiles (method exists, populated earlier) and unifies all keys. Verdict and fix correct.

### [#6] Gabarit_Template document never loaded; LoadTemplates only loads the template list, not the template gabarit  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `business/LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:32-55`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Any run with at least one dynamic task in TODO. .NET LoadTemplates loads BOTH Get_Gabarit_Template (the template document, later uploaded to the pool by Task_Dynamique.Prepare) AND Load_Templates (the template list). Java only calls getGabaritTemplateDao.getTemplates() (the list); getGabaritTemplate() is implemented but never called and the Run domain has no gabaritTemplate field.
- **Java is (verified):** LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:43 `List<Map<String, Object>> rows = getGabaritTemplateDao.getTemplates(idGabaritTemplate);` — only the template LIST is loaded; getGabaritTemplate() is never called. Run.java:30 `private DocumentDomain gabarit;` is the only document field (no gabaritTemplate). TaskDynamique.java:40 `DocumentDomain gabaritTemplate = this.getRun().getGabarit();` uploads the SOURCE gabarit. GetGabaritTemplateDaoImpl.java:67-87 getGabaritTemplate() is implemented but its builder (lines 42-53) sets fileName but NOT filePoolPath.
- **.NET is (verified):** `Run.cs:152,157; Run_Base.cs:859,868; Task_Dynamique.cs:60; Document.cs:115-118` — Run.cs:152 `this.Gabarit_Template = __proxy.Get_Gabarit_Template(this);` then :157 `__proxy.Load_Templates(this);`. Run_Base.cs:859 `public Document Gabarit` (source) and :868 `public Document Gabarit_Template` (template) are distinct. Task_Dynamique.cs:60 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Run.Gabarit_Template.FilePoolPath, this.Run.Gabarit_Template.Data);`. Document.cs:115-118 ctor auto-sets `_fileName` and `_filePoolPath = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(_fileName)`.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Steps 1-3 of the proposed fix are correct in direction (add `private DocumentDomain gabaritTemplate;` to Run; in LoadTemplatesBusiness call `getGabaritTemplate` before `getTemplates`; in TaskDynamique.prepare() use `getRun().getGabaritTemplate()`). BUT they are INCOMPLETE: TaskDynamique.prepare() calls `gabaritTemplate.getFilePoolPath()`, and GetGabaritTemplateDaoImpl's Lombok builder (lines 42-53) sets `fileName` but never `filePoolPath` (and being a builder it does not invoke generateFileNames, which anyway only sets fileName). In .NET the Document ctor (Document.cs:118) auto-populates FilePoolPath via GetPoolPath; the source gabarit gets it in Java via GetGabaritBusiness.java:91 `gabarit.setFilePoolPath(runProperties.getPoolPath(gabarit.getFileName()))`. So after applying steps 1-3 alone, filePoolPath is null and the upload path is null. Add to LoadTemplatesBusiness.execute(): `DocumentDomain gt = getGabaritTemplateDao.getGabaritTemplate(idGabaritTemplate); gt.setFilePoolPath(run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(gt.getFileName())); run.setGabaritTemplate(gt);` (mirroring GetGabaritBusiness:91), placed before the getTemplates() call to match .NET ordering. Then TaskDynamique.prepare() uses getRun().getGabaritTemplate(). Also port the .NET null-guard (Process_Dynamique / Prepare assumes Gabarit_Template non-null) so a missing template fails loudly rather than NPEs.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. .NET keeps two distinct Documents (Gabarit source vs Gabarit_Template), loads both in LoadTemplates, and Task_Dynamique uploads the template's FilePoolPath. Java has only `gabarit`, never calls getGabaritTemplate, and TaskDynamique uploads the wrong (source) document — so dynamic blocs clone from the wrong source: wrong/empty output for the whole dynamic-task feature, silently. CRITICAL confirmed. Fix marked FIX_ADJUSTED because the concrete steps omit populating filePoolPath on the loaded template document; the DAO builder does not set it (unlike .NET's auto ctor and the source-gabarit path in GetGabaritBusiness:91), so the upload would use a null pool path even after the fix.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java: getGabaritTemplate() has zero call sites; LoadTemplatesBusiness:43 loads only the list; Run has only `gabarit`; TaskDynamique:40 uploads getRun().getGabarit() (wrong doc); the GetGabaritTemplateDaoImpl builder (42-53) sets fileName but never filePoolPath. .NET differs: Run.cs:152/157 loads both Gabarit_Template and the list; Run_Base 859/868 are distinct; Task_Dynamique:60 uploads Gabarit_Template.FilePoolPath; Document.cs:118 ctor auto-sets _filePoolPath, and Proxy_Template.Get_Gabarit_Template builds a Document so it inherits FilePoolPath. Reconciler's core insight holds: steps 1-3 leave filePoolPath null, so adding gt.setFilePoolPath(getPoolPath(...)) (mirroring GetGabaritBusiness:91) is required and compiles; Run uses no-arg/size-limit ctors so a new field is safe. Minor: Process_Dynamique.cs has no explicit MSG_Gabarit_Template_NULL guard, so that porting note is a non-load-bearing embellishment, not part of the essential fix.

### [#7] Mode-degrade returns before gabarit pool upload, so degraded-run render has no file in pool  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:158-167`
- **Area:** pool
- **Scenario:** A run whose gabarit byte-size exceeds sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft sets modeDegrade=true and returns at line 163, BEFORE filePoolPort.addFile(...) at line 167. ProcessRunServiceImpl.process still calls qxpsCallerService.render() unconditionally (ProcessRunServiceImpl line 94), which runs a PDF render + 'literal' QXP fetch against run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath(). Because the gabarit was never uploaded to the pool, the render targets a non-existent document and yields empty/error output.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:159-167 — `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { ... this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); return; // Return early }` (line 163) precedes `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());` (line 167). So a degraded run never uploads the gabarit to the pool. Note line 167 keys the upload by getFileName().
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run.cs:92,94,97; Document.cs:200,228-234,317-322` — Run.cs:92 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data);` (unconditional), then Run.cs:94 `this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity();`, then Run.cs:97 `if (!this.Mode_Degrade)` only guards a log message. Document.cs:200 `if (!this.Mode_Degrade)` gates ONLY the DID parse inside Evaluate_Document_Identity. Document.cs:317-320 doc: degraded mode runs no tasks/DID but still "effectue uniquement un rendu pdf du document et r?cup?re le qxp correspondant sans modification" — requiring the pool upload.
- **Fix:** Move the unconditional gabarit upload above the mode-degrade early-return, mirroring .NET Run.cs:92. After `if (this.gabarit == null) return;` (line 156), insert the upload first, then keep only the DID fetch/parse gated by mode-degrade:

    // Step 1: Add gabarit to the QXPS pool UNCONDITIONALLY (.NET Run.cs:92) —
    // degraded mode still renders the PDF + fetches the literal QXP from the pool.
    filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), this.gabarit.getData());

    // Mode_Degrade: if template exceeds size limit, skip ONLY DID parsing (steps 2-3).
    if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) {
        log.warn("Gabarit size {} bytes exceeds limit {} bytes, setting Mode_Degrade for runId: {}",
                this.gabarit.getData().length, sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft, this.id);
        this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true);
        return; // skip DID fetch/parse only — gabarit is already in the pool
    }

    String xmlContent = documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), "DID");
    String didValue = documentIdentityPort.getElementValueByIdName(xmlContent, "DID");
    this.gabarit.setDocumentIdentity(documentIdentityPort.parseDocumentIdentity(didValue));

Use getFilePoolPath() (not getFileName()) as the pool key so the upload name matches what QxpsCallerBusiness.render reads back at line 197/240 and matches .NET; verify fetchXmlForBox's existing argument and align it to the same pool key. Add a regression test for an oversized gabarit asserting filePoolPort.addFile is invoked exactly once before the early return.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java early-returns at Run.java:163 before addFile at :167, so degraded runs upload nothing; ProcessRunServiceImpl.process calls render() unconditionally (line 94) and QxpsCallerBusiness.render reads run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath() (lines 197,240) from the pool — yielding empty/error output. .NET uploads unconditionally (Run.cs:92, keyed by FilePoolPath) precisely so degraded mode still renders a faithful PDF/QXP. The fix moves addFile above the early return, keeps DID parse gated, and switches the key from getFileName() to getFilePoolPath() — matching both .NET and the Java render/process paths (CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy:243, QxpsCallerBusiness:197/240), and aligns the fetchXmlForBox key to the same pool path. Compiles and produces parity. Severity CRITICAL is correct: silently defeats the size-overflow fallback for every oversized gabarit.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java Run.java:163 returns early in degraded mode BEFORE filePoolPort.addFile at line 167, so the gabarit is never uploaded. .NET Run.cs:92 Addfile is unconditional (before the Mode_Degrade-only log gate at line 97); Mode_Degrade (Document.cs:322) is a lazy size property that does not skip the upload, and Document.cs:317-320 documents that degraded mode still renders a PDF + fetches the unmodified QXP from the pool. Downstream is reachable: ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:69 skips steps 3-6 but line 94 always calls render(), which reads getGabarit().getFilePoolPath() (QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197). The fix correctly moves addFile above the early return and uses getFilePoolPath() (matching .NET FilePoolPath and sibling CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy:243), not getFileName(). Both getters exist (Lombok); compiles and matches .NET.

### [#8] Gabarit uploaded with getFileName() (pool root) instead of getFilePoolPath() (R_<id>/ scoped), so process steps modify a non-existent document  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:167`
- **Area:** pool
- **Scenario:** prepareGabarit uploads the gabarit via filePool.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), ...) which has NO R_<id>/ prefix, landing the file at the pool root. Every downstream consumer uses getFilePoolPath() (R_<id>/-prefixed): QxpsCallerBusiness.executeStep reads currentDocName = gabarit.getFilePoolPath() (line 106) for the FIRST step's Modify/SaveAs/QXP-render combined call, and render() uses getFilePoolPath() too. The first process step therefore targets R_<id>/<name> which was never uploaded there, so the modify/SaveAs operates on a missing document.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:167 `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());` and Run.java:170 `String xmlContent = documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFileName(), "DID");` — both use bare getFileName(), no R_<id>/ prefix. Downstream consumers use getFilePoolPath(): QxpsCallerBusiness.java:106 `String currentDocName = gabarit.getFilePoolPath();` and :197 `String documentName = run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath();`. GetGabaritBusiness.java:91 `gabarit.setFilePoolPath(runProperties.getPoolPath(gabarit.getFileName()));`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run.cs:92; Document.cs:118,182,430; Run_Base.cs:954` — Run.cs:92 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data);`. Document.cs:118 `_filePoolPath = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(_fileName);`; :182 `_data = ...GetFileData(_filePoolPath);`; :430 `_xml = QXPS_File_Manager.Get_XML(this);` (XML getter, used by Evaluate_Document_Identity at :205). Run_Base.cs:954 `__info.DocumentName = this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath;`. So upload, DID/XML read, and process step DocumentName all consistently use the R_<id>/-prefixed FilePoolPath.
- **Fix:** In Run.java, change line 167 to filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), this.gabarit.getData()) and line 170 to documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), "DID"), so the upload, DID fetch, and all downstream process (QxpsCallerBusiness.executeStep, line 106) and render (line 197) steps consistently use the R_<id>/-scoped getFilePoolPath(), matching .NET Run.cs:92 which uploads via FilePoolPath.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed by reopening sources. Java uploads/reads via bare getFileName() (pool root), while every downstream consumer (QxpsCallerBusiness:106 first Modify/SaveAs, :197 render) uses getFilePoolPath() = R_<runId>/<name>. .NET uploads (Run.cs:92), reads XML/DID (Document.cs FilePoolPath chain), and processes (Run_Base.cs:954) all via FilePoolPath, keeping locations consistent. The proposed two-line change to getFilePoolPath() on Run.java:167 and :170 makes Java identical to .NET and aligns with the already-correct downstream Java consumers; same String signatures so it compiles. Fix description's "render (line 197)" refers to QxpsCallerBusiness, not Run.java (only 178 lines), but the actual code change is correct and complete. CRITICAL stands: core pipeline operates on a missing document on the normal flow.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java Run.java:167 uploads via getFileName() (bare) and :170 fetches DID XML via getFileName(), while RunProperties.getPoolPath formats "R_%d/%s" so getFilePoolPath() is R_<id>/-prefixed. All downstream consumers use getFilePoolPath(): QxpsCallerBusiness:106 (currentDocName) and :197 (render documentName), set in GetGabaritBusiness:91. .NET genuinely differs: Run.cs:92 Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath,...), Document.cs:118 _filePoolPath=GetPoolPath(_fileName), Run_Base.cs:954 DocumentName=Gabarit.FilePoolPath, and DID/XML resolves via the same Document/FilePoolPath. Fix changes both lines to getFilePoolPath(); ports accept String, value is populated before use, change is local. Compiles, matches .NET, no other call sites broken.

### [#9] load() runs LoadInParams/LoadTasks/LoadTemplates even in degrade mode; .NET skips them  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/impl/ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:162-183 (load) vs 69 (runProcessor)`
- **Area:** pipeline (ProcessRunServiceImpl / Run lifecycle)
- **Scenario:** When the gabarit triggers degrade mode, prepareGabarit() sets modeDegrade=true, but load() unconditionally continues to getInParamsBusiness.execute(run), loadTasksService.loadTasks(run), and loadTemplatesBusiness.execute(run). These do unnecessary DB work and, worse, can THROW (e.g. loadTemplates raising 'missing ID_Gabarit_Template' when a dynamic task exists). Any such throw flips the whole run to ERROR, whereas .NET would have completed as a successful degraded render.
- **Java is (verified):** ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:171 run.prepareGabarit(getGabaritBusiness, filePoolPort, documentIdentityPort); :174 getInParamsBusiness.execute(run); :177 loadTasksService.loadTasks(run); :180 loadTemplatesBusiness.execute(run);  — all unconditional. Run.java:162 this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); :163 return; (inside prepareGabarit, set when gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft, Run.java:159).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:305-317` — Run_Base.cs:304 //si nous sommes en mode dégradé nous ne préparons pas les taches et leurs paramètres :305 if (!this.Mode_Degrade) :308 Execution_Helper.Universal_Executer<Exception_Run>(this.LoadInParams, ...); :310 this.InParams.InitOraParameters(); :313 ...this.LoadTasks...; :316 ...this.LoadTemplates...; :317 }
- **Fix:** In ProcessRunServiceImpl.load(), guard the three loading steps after prepareGabarit with the degrade flag, mirroring Run_Base.Load lines 305-317:

    run.prepareGabarit(getGabaritBusiness, filePoolPort, documentIdentityPort);

    if (!run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade()) {
        getInParamsBusiness.execute(run);   // + InitOraParameters equivalent if present
        loadTasksService.loadTasks(run);
        loadTemplatesBusiness.execute(run);
    }

This skips InParams/Tasks/Templates loading in degrade mode exactly as .NET does, preventing both the wasted DB work and the spurious ERROR-on-throw that breaks the degraded-render success path. (If an InitOraParameters equivalent exists on the Java InParams object, place it inside the same guard between getInParams and loadTasks to fully match .NET ordering.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java load() (ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:162-183) calls getInParams (174), loadTasks (177), loadTemplates (180) unconditionally right after prepareGabarit (171). prepareGabarit (Run.java:159-163) sets modeDegrade=true and returns early when gabarit exceeds size limit, so degrade is established mid-load before those steps. .NET Run_Base.Load (305-317) wraps exactly LoadInParams/InitOraParameters/LoadTasks/LoadTemplates in if(!Mode_Degrade). The proposed guard uses run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade(), the identical getter already used at line 69 of the same file (compiles; RunProperties.modeDegrade is a boolean Lombok field). No Java InitOraParameters equivalent exists in the codebase, which the fix correctly treats as conditional. Fix makes Java behave identically. HIGH confirmed: a throw from these steps in degrade mode flips run to ERROR (line 105) instead of producing a successful degraded render.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed in both sources. Java ProcessRunServiceImpl.load() (162-183) calls getInParamsBusiness.execute (174), loadTasksService.loadTasks (177), loadTemplatesBusiness.execute (180) unconditionally after prepareGabarit (171). Run.prepareGabarit (Run.java:159-163) sets runProperties.setModeDegrade(true) and returns early on size overflow, so degrade is set before those steps. .NET Run_Base.Load (294-318) wraps LoadInParams/InitOraParameters/LoadTasks/LoadTemplates in if(!Mode_Degrade) (305-317), French comment confirms intent; Mode_Degrade (915-926) driven by Gabarit. .NET genuinely skips, Java does not. Fix uses isModeDegrade() — identical API already at line 69 — so it compiles, matches .NET, and load is single-call so no other site breaks. InitOraParameters note correctly conditional (no Java equivalent exists).

### [#10] Double-page 'prepare step' creation branch is missing — multi-task page creation without deletion is mis-paginated  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/RunTaskStep.java:206-321 (updateBlocPagination); confirmed dead at 222 and 318-320`
- **Area:** process-tasks
- **Scenario:** Run.runProperties.nbPageBySpread == PAGINATION_DOUBLE (2), a pagination step where a task creates pages whose first created page is on the wrong side (minCreationPageId odd/even per lagSpread), minCreationPageId > 1, AND the task removes NO pages (nbPageSupprimer == 0).
- **Java is (verified):** RunTaskStep.java:267-285: `if (minCreationPageId > 1 && nbPageSupprimer > 0) { int posPremiereSupp = maxCreationPageId + 1; ...swap... maxCreationPageId += OFFSET_PAGE; } else if (minCreationPageId == 1 && maxCreationPageId == 1) { lagSpread = true; ... }` — no branch for `minCreationPageId > 1 && nbPageSupprimer == 0`. RunTaskStep.java:222 `RunTaskStep localPrepareStep = new RunTaskStep(run);` is built but never assigned. RunTaskStep.java:318-320 `if (localPrepareStep != this.prepareStep) { // ...simplified here }` is a literal no-op, so getPrepareStep() is permanently null.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Task_Step.cs:352-425 (else no-deletion prepare-step block); QXPS_Caller.cs:88-93` — Run_Task_Step.cs:350-411: `if (__nbPage_Supprimer > 0) {...swap...} else { ... Bloc_Page __page = new Bloc_Page(__task, "PA_Offset_"+__offsetDummyPageIDOriginale); __page.Action = Bloc_Action.CREATE; __page.Create_Next_Dummy_Page = true; __page.Page_ID = __offsetDummyPageIDOriginale; __prepare_Step.Modifier.Add(__page); _prepare_Step = __prepare_Step; __page = new Bloc_Page(__task,"PR_Offset_"+__offsetDummyPageID); __page.Action = Bloc_Action.REMOVE; __page.Lag_Spread = __lag_Spread; __page.Page_ID = __offsetDummyPageID; this.Modifier.Add(__page); ... __nbPage_Creer++; }`. QXPS_Caller.cs:88-91: `if (QXPFrk.Validation.IsNotNull(__step.Prepare_Step)) { Execute(__step.Prepare_Step); }`.
- **Fix:** Port the full nested .NET structure into updateBlocPagination. Replace the collapsed branch (RunTaskStep.java ~266-286) so that, after computing `posPremiereSupp = maxCreationPageId + 1`:

```
if ((minCreationPageId % 2) == checkPremiereCreation) {
    if (minCreationPageId > 1) {
        if (nbPageSupprimer > 0) {
            // existing swap branch (unchanged)
            for (BlocBase bloc : taskBlocs.get(task)) {
                if (bloc.getPageId() == posPremiereSupp) bloc.setPageId(minCreationPageId);
                else if (bloc.getPageId() < posPremiereSupp && bloc.getPageId() >= minCreationPageId)
                    bloc.setPageId(bloc.getPageId() + OFFSET_PAGE);
            }
            maxCreationPageId += OFFSET_PAGE;
        } else {
            // NO-DELETION case — build preparation sub-step (mirrors .NET 352-425)
            int offsetDummyPage = -1;
            int offsetDummyPageOriginale = (minCreationPageIdOriginale % 2 == PAGE_PAIRE) ? -2 : -1;
            int offsetDummyPageId = minCreationPageId + offsetDummyPage;
            int offsetDummyPageIdOriginale = minCreationPageIdOriginale + offsetDummyPageOriginale + preprareOffsetTotal;
            preprareOffsetTotal += 2;

            BlocPage paPage = new BlocPage(task, "PA_Offset_" + offsetDummyPageIdOriginale);
            paPage.setAction(BlocActionEnum.CREATE);
            paPage.setCreateNextDummyPage(true);
            paPage.setPageId(offsetDummyPageIdOriginale);
            localPrepareStep.getBlocsModify().add(paPage);   // == .NET __prepare_Step.Modifier.Add
            this.prepareStep = localPrepareStep;             // == .NET _prepare_Step = __prepare_Step

            BlocPage prPage = new BlocPage(task, "PR_Offset_" + offsetDummyPageId);
            prPage.setAction(BlocActionEnum.REMOVE);
            prPage.setLagSpread(lagSpread);
            prPage.setPageId(offsetDummyPageId);
            this.getBlocsModify().add(prPage);               // == .NET this.Modifier.Add

            for (BlocBase bloc : taskBlocs.get(task)) {
                if (bloc.getPageId() < posPremiereSupp && bloc.getPageId() >= minCreationPageId)
                    bloc.setPageId(bloc.getPageId() + OFFSET_PAGE);
            }
            maxCreationPageId += OFFSET_PAGE;
            nbPageCreer++;
        }
    } else {
        if (minCreationPageId == 1 && maxCreationPageId == 1) {
            lagSpread = true;
            for (BlocBase bloc : taskBlocs.get(task))
                if (bloc.getPageId() > 1) bloc.setLagSpread(true);
        }
    }
}
```

Also: (1) add a method-scope `int preprareOffsetTotal = 0;` next to `lagSpread` (mirrors .NET `__preprare_offsetTotal`, accumulated across tasks). (2) Delete the dead no-op block at lines 317-320; `this.prepareStep` is now assigned inside the loop. (3) Keep `localPrepareStep` constructed once before the task loop (line 222) as the lazily-populated prepare step, matching .NET `__prepare_Step` created at .cs:231. (4) Note the .NET inner `if ((__position_premiere_creation % 2) == __checkPremiereCreation)` at line 367 is redundant inside the already-true outer test, so it can be dropped in Java. Verify QxpsCallerBusiness.java:79-82 already executes prepareStep before the main step (it does) — no change needed there; this fix simply makes that path live.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified the triple by re-opening both sources. Java (RunTaskStep.java:267-320) genuinely collapsed the wrong-side branch to the swap-only case and dropped the entire no-deletion prepare-step else; localPrepareStep is never assigned and the 318-320 block is a no-op, so prepareStep is always null and QxpsCallerBusiness.java:79-82 (executes prepareStep first, mirroring QXPS_Caller.cs:88-93) is dead. .NET Run_Task_Step.cs:352-425 has the full PA(CREATE+dummy)/PR(REMOVE)/_prepare_Step assignment/bloc-shift/nbPage_Creer++ logic. The proposed fix ports it faithfully; all referenced Java APIs exist (BlocPage(TaskBase,String) ctor, setCreateNextDummyPage, setAction, setLagSpread, setPageId, getPageIdOriginale, BlocActionEnum.CREATE/REMOVE) and the preprareOffsetTotal/offset arithmetic matches .NET exactly. One non-functional nuance: .NET adds the PR page into _modifier before AddRange(_blocs_Modify) (PR first), while Java appends to getBlocsModify() (PR last); QuarkXPress keys page ops by ID, so order is immaterial and the finding acknowledges this mapping. Severity HIGH correct: silent mis-pagination/wrong-side pages in a valid no-deletion double-page scenario, not a crash.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed in both sources. Java RunTaskStep.java:266-286 collapses the wrong-side branch to `if (minCreationPageId>1 && nbPageSupprimer>0){swap} else if (minCreationPageId==1 && maxCreationPageId==1){lagSpread}` — no `minCreationPageId>1 && nbPageSupprimer==0` branch; localPrepareStep (222) is never assigned to this.prepareStep, lines 318-320 are a literal no-op, so Lombok getPrepareStep() is always null and QxpsCallerBusiness.java:79-82 is dead. .NET Run_Task_Step.cs:333-426 genuinely has the full nested else block building PA/PR prepare sub-step, sets _prepare_Step, with __preprare_offsetTotal at method scope (228). Fix uses real API (BlocPage ctor, Lombok setters, getPageIdOriginale, getBlocsModify) and compiles; caller already runs prepareStep first. Fix is faithful and correct.

### [#11] QXPSM request timeout defaults to 30s vs .NET's effective 1 hour — long modifier/render chains abort  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxpsm/QxpsmSoapClient.java:95-96 (and application.yaml:65 qxpsm.soap.timeout=30000)`
- **Area:** qxpsm-soap
- **Scenario:** Any directCall=false UPDATE step whose ParamsValue→Modifier→SaveAs→QXPRender chain takes longer than 30 seconds on the QXPSM server (large documents / heavy structural modifiers / slow render).
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsmSoapClient.java:95-96 — `int timeout = qxpsmProperties.getTimeout();` / `context.setRequestTimeout(timeout > 0 ? timeout : 3600 * 1000);`. application.yaml:65 — `timeout: 30000` (and connection-timeout: 10000 is the only other transport timeout).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXPSM_Call.cs:82-91; Run_Base.cs:953-954; Validation.cs:67-77` — Run_Base.cs:953-954: `QXPS_Call_Info __info = new QXPS_Call_Info();` / `__info.DocumentName = this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath;` (RequestTimeout left at int default 0). Validation.cs:75: `return !(val == int.MinValue || val == 0);` so IsSet(0,false)==false. QXPSM_Call.cs:86-91 ELSE branch: `_sdk_context.requestTimeout = 3600*1000;` / `_sdk_service.Timeout = System.Threading.Timeout.Infinite;`
- **Fix:** Primary: change application.yaml:65 to `timeout: 3600000` (1 hour) to mirror .NET's effective requestTimeout. This is the lowest-risk, most faithful fix and keeps the existing line-96 fallback meaningful for the unconfigured case. Do NOT instead invert the line-96 logic to treat 30000 as "too short" — that re-introduces a magic threshold; the real intent is simply that the default should be 1h. Recommended companion check (out of scope of the cited lines but required for true parity): ensure the Axis-generated stub's transport/read timeout is effectively unbounded or also set to >= the 1h request budget, mirroring .NET's `_sdk_service.Timeout = Timeout.Infinite`; otherwise the underlying HTTP read could still abort a long render before the 1h server-side budget elapses. If a config knob exists for the stub socket timeout, set it to the same 3600000 (or 0/infinite).
- **Reconciler note:** All three legs verified by re-opening source. Java pins QXPSM requestTimeout to 30s (yaml default 30000 > 0 kills the 1h fallback), while .NET leaves QXPS_Call_Info.RequestTimeout at 0, fails IsSet(0,false), and takes the ELSE branch setting requestTimeout=3600*1000 and service Timeout=Infinite. Setting application.yaml:65 to 3600000 makes the request budget match .NET (1h) and keeps line 96's fallback meaningful for the unconfigured case — correct and faithful. The fix does not replicate .NET's service-level Timeout.Infinite (socket/read timeout); Java only has connection-timeout=10000, so the Axis stub read timeout should also be checked for full transport parity, but that is outside the cited lines and the finding flags it explicitly. Real defect; HIGH justified (silent 30s aborts on heavy render chains).
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Verified independently. .NET: QXPS_Call_Info._requestTimeout is int (default 0), empty ctor, and grep shows the RequestTimeout setter is never called anywhere; Run_Base.cs:953-954 sets only DocumentName. So IsSet(0,false)==false (Validation.cs:75) -> ELSE branch: requestTimeout=3600*1000 and _sdk_service.Timeout=Infinite -> effective 1h. The field comment even says "1 heure". Java QxpsmSoapClient.java:95-96 uses getTimeout(); QxpsmProperties binds qxpsm.soap (timeout=30000), and application.yaml:65 = 30000 (>0), so 30s is pinned and the 1h fallback is dead. getTimeout() is consumed only here; line 55's 3600000 is the separate qxps.server config. Fix (yaml:65 -> 3600000) makes Java match, compiles, breaks no other call site. Genuine HIGH divergence.

### [#12] INT value formatting drops thousands separators (VALUE-mode output wrong)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:35-36, 50-58`
- **Area:** system-task
- **Scenario:** VALUE mode with dataType=INT and a value like 1234567 (e.g. an amount-as-integer or DID-like numeric field).
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:50-54 — private static String formatInteger(String raw, boolean showZero, String nullString) { try { long val = Long.parseLong(raw); if (val == 0 && !showZero) return nullString; return String.valueOf(val); } — no grouping. Contrast formatDecimal at line 70-77 which uses new DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE) and pattern "#,##0".
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:417-431 (and DecimalPattern const at line 32)` — GetStringInt(int value, ...): NumberFormatInfo __nf = Thread.CurrentThread.CurrentUICulture.NumberFormat.Clone() as NumberFormatInfo; __nf.NumberDecimalDigits = 0; string __str = __int.ToString("n", __nf); return __str; — the "n" specifier always applies group separators. Sibling: const string DecimalPattern = "#,##0.{0}" (line 32), confirming grouped convention.
- **Fix:** In formatInteger, format the parsed long with a grouped French pattern instead of String.valueOf, mirroring the existing formatDecimal locale handling. E.g. after the zero/showZero check: DecimalFormat df = new DecimalFormat("#,##0", new DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE)); return df.format(val); This yields a NBSP-grouped value (e.g. "1 234 567") matching .NET's "n" format under fr-FR. Reuse a shared DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE) to stay consistent with formatDecimal. Keep the existing NumberFormatException catch returning raw, and keep the (val==0 && !showZero) -> nullString guard unchanged.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java formatInteger returns String.valueOf(val) ("1234567"); .NET GetStringInt uses ToString("n") which groups ("1 234 567" NBSP under fr-FR with NumberDecimalDigits=0 dropping only the fractional part). Reachable via SystemTaskProcessStrategy.java:51 and ProcessSqlBusiness.java:73 -> outputToString -> INT -> formatInteger (line 36). The sibling formatDecimal and the .NET DecimalPattern "#,##0.{0}" establish the hard-coded fr-FR grouping parity convention, so the proposed fix (DecimalFormat "#,##0" with DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE), df.format(long val)) compiles, matches .NET output, and preserves the (val==0 && !showZero) and NumberFormatException guards. Severity HIGH retained: every multi-thousand integer rendered in VALUE-mode output diverges textually from the legacy app.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed in both files. .NET Data_Type_Helper.cs GetStringInt(int) (lines 417-431) returns __int.ToString("n", __nf) with __nf.NumberDecimalDigits=0; the "n" specifier always applies group separators (NBSP under fr-FR), only the decimals are dropped. Java DataTypeHelper.formatInteger (lines 50-58) returns String.valueOf(val) with no grouping — divergence confirmed. Sibling formatDecimal (lines 60-81) already uses DecimalFormat("#,##0", DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE)) which groups, establishing the parity convention. formatInteger is private, called only via outputToString (callers ProcessSqlBusiness.java:73, SystemTaskProcessStrategy.java:51), so the fix is contained, compiles, and breaks no other call site. Locale.FRANCE grouping is NBSP, matching .NET. Genuine defect; fix correct.

### [#13] decimal_significative flag implemented with wrong meaning — trailing zeros not suppressed  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:60-81 (formatDecimal)`
- **Area:** sql-task
- **Scenario:** A DECIMAL/CURRENCY/POURCENTAGE bloc with decimalSignificative=false (Decimal_Significative=false) and nbDecimal=2, value 12.50 (or 12.00).
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:66-75: `if (decimalSignificative) { val = val.setScale(nbDecimal, RoundingMode.HALF_UP); }` then `StringBuilder pattern = new StringBuilder("#,##0"); if (nbDecimal > 0) { pattern.append("."); for (int i = 0; i < nbDecimal; i++) pattern.append("0"); }` — the pattern ALWAYS uses '0' digits regardless of the flag; the flag only gates setScale.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:447 (call) and 77-94 (GetDecimalPattern); constants 32 + ctor 54-55` — Line 447: `string __pattern = GetDecimalPattern(nbDecimal, decimal_significative);` Lines 81-88: `if(fixedPattern){ return string.Format(DecimalPattern, _decimalFixedPattern[nbDecimal]); } else { return string.Format(DecimalPattern, _decimalNotFixedPattern[nbDecimal]); }` where DecimalPattern="#,##0.{0}", _decimalFixedPattern[n]=new string('0',n), _decimalNotFixedPattern[n]=new string('#',n). So true => "#,##0.00" (trailing zeros shown), false => "#,##0.##" (suppressed).
- **Fix:** Drive DecimalFormat fraction digits off the flag instead of the literal pattern characters. Replace the body of formatDecimal so that after the zero/showZero guard you build a DecimalFormat (locale FR) and set: df.setMaximumFractionDigits(nbDecimal) always; df.setMinimumFractionDigits(decimalSignificative ? nbDecimal : 0). Keep the integer grouping pattern "#,##0". This makes decimalSignificative==true emit fixed decimals (trailing zeros, '0' semantics) and false suppress trailing zeros ('#' semantics), matching .NET. Apply rounding to nbDecimal in BOTH cases (DecimalFormat does this via setMaximumFractionDigits, so you can drop the conditional setScale entirely; or, to preserve the existing HALF_UP intent, set df.setRoundingMode(RoundingMode.HALF_UP) explicitly since DecimalFormat defaults to HALF_EVEN). Concretely: remove lines 66-75 building the StringBuilder/'0' loop and the conditional setScale; use new DecimalFormat("#,##0", symbols) then setMinimum/Maximum/RoundingMode as above before df.format(val). Verify nbDecimal==0 still works (min=max=0 yields integer-style output, consistent with .NET '#,##0.' with empty decimal token).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. .NET passes decimal_significative as fixedPattern: true picks '0' fill (12,50 / 12,00), false picks '#' fill (12,5 / 12). Java always builds '0' fill and only uses the flag to gate setScale, so significative=false still emits "12,50"/"12,00" — flag effect lost. Flow is unmodified DB->TaskMapper->ProcessSqlBusiness/SystemTaskProcessStrategy->formatDecimal, default true (common case matches), false mis-formatted. DECIMAL/CURRENCY/POURCENTAGE all share formatDecimal. The proposed fix (max=nbDecimal always; min=flag?nbDecimal:0; pattern "#,##0"; RoundingMode.HALF_UP) reproduces both patterns exactly across nbDecimal 0..n and matches .NET Decimal.ToString away-from-zero rounding; it also fixes the secondary HALF_EVEN rounding bug on the false path. HIGH (visible numeric divergence in produced documents) is correct.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Verified both sources. Java formatDecimal (DataTypeHelper.java:66-75) always appends '0' digit placeholders to the pattern regardless of decimalSignificative; the flag only gates setScale. .NET (Data_Type_Helper.cs:447 -> GetDecimalPattern 77-94, ctor 50-56) builds "#,##0." + n copies of '0' when fixedPattern/decimal_significative=true (trailing zeros shown) vs n copies of '#' when false (trailing zeros suppressed). So false => 12.5 yields ".NET '12,5' / 12.00 '12'" but Java emits "12,50"/"12,00". Real divergence. Flag is loaded from DB (TaskMapper:69), default true, passed through unmodified, so false configs are mis-formatted. Fix (setMin=flag?n:0, setMax=n, HALF_UP) correctly replicates '#'/'0' semantics, compiles, and the only call sites pass the flag through unchanged.

### [#14] CURRENCY and POURCENTAGE output omit the currency symbol and the ' %' suffix  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:37-40, 60-81`
- **Area:** sql-task
- **Scenario:** A bloc with dataType=CURRENCY or dataType=POURCENTAGE.
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:37-40 `case DECIMAL: case CURRENCY: case POURCENTAGE: return formatDecimal(raw, nbDecimal, showZero, nullString, decimalSignificative);` and formatDecimal (lines 71-77) builds only `pattern = "#,##0"` (+ ".0..." for nbDecimal) with DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE) — no currency symbol, no percent scaling/suffix.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:33-34, 108/112, 196-215, 466-505` — Line 33 `const string CurrencyPattern = "#,##0.{0} {1}";` line 34 `const string PercentPattern = "#,##0.{0} %";`. OutputToString routes Currency->GetStringCurrency (line 203) and Pourcentage->GetStringPourcentage (line 209). GetCurrencyPattern formats CurrencyPattern with `NumberFormatInfo.CurrentInfo.CurrencySymbol` (lines 108/112). GetStringPourcentage line 497 `string __str = __pourcentage.ToString(__pattern) + " %";` (preceded by line 496 `// TODO a voir si c'est correct`).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Split the switch: keep DECIMAL->formatDecimal; add separate branches for CURRENCY and POURCENTAGE.

CURRENCY (mirror CurrencyPattern "#,##0.{0} {1}" with {1}=NumberFormatInfo.CurrentInfo.CurrencySymbol): format the value with the SAME decimal pattern logic as formatDecimal (respecting nbDecimal, showZero->nullString, and the fixed-vs-significative pattern), then append " " + currency symbol. .NET uses the current thread culture's CurrencySymbol; if the run culture is fr-FR this is "€". Use the run/culture symbol (e.g. DecimalFormatSymbols(runLocale).getCurrencySymbol()); Locale.FRANCE is acceptable only if the engine pins fr-FR. Result e.g. 1234.56 nbDecimal=2 -> "1 234,56 €".

POURCENTAGE (reproduce .NET verbatim, including the x100 scaling and double percent): .NET PercentPattern is "#,##0.{0} %" and the "%" custom specifier multiplies the value by 100 AND emits a localized percent literal; GetStringPourcentage then concatenates an EXTRA " %". So replicate: multiply BigDecimal value by 100, format with the "#,##0[.0..]" pattern, append " " + DecimalFormatSymbols.getPercent() (the pattern's "%"), then append literal " %". Example 0.15 nbDecimal=2 -> "15,00 % %". (If the team chooses to correct the quirk to a single percent, use x100 + single " %" -> "15,00 %"; but strict parity = double percent.)

Also note: .NET's fixed-vs-not pattern differs from the current Java formatDecimal — fixedPattern(true) uses '0'*nbDecimal (trailing zeros shown) and false uses '#'*nbDecimal (trailing zeros omitted); the current Java always uses '0' and only setScales when decimalSignificative is true, which is itself a divergence the new CURRENCY/POURCENTAGE branches should follow consistently with whatever DECIMAL parity decision is made.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java (DataTypeHelper.java:37-40) collapses DECIMAL/CURRENCY/POURCENTAGE into formatDecimal producing a bare number; .NET (Data_Type_Helper.cs) routes each to distinct methods adding the currency symbol and the percent x100+suffix. Path is reachable (DataTypeEnum CURRENCY=6, POURCENTAGE=7; called from ProcessSqlBusiness and SystemTaskProcessStrategy). Bug is real, HIGH (output corruption in generated documents). The finding's 'fix' field is incomplete for POURCENTAGE — it just appends " %" and omits .NET's x100 scaling and the double-percent quirk (correctly captured only in verifyNote). Hence FIX_ADJUSTED, with adjustedFix replicating x100 + double " %" and the culture-driven currency symbol. Currency culture should mirror NumberFormatInfo.CurrentInfo, not hard-coded Locale.FRANCE.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java DataTypeHelper.java:37-40 routes DECIMAL/CURRENCY/POURCENTAGE all to formatDecimal (60-81), producing a bare "#,##0[.0]" number — no currency symbol, no percent. .NET Data_Type_Helper.cs:202-209 routes Currency->GetStringCurrency (CurrencyPattern "#,##0.{0} {1}" with NumberFormatInfo.CurrentInfo.CurrencySymbol, lines 33,108/112) and Pourcentage->GetStringPourcentage which uses PercentPattern "#,##0.{0} %" (the "%" specifier multiplies by 100 + emits localized percent) AND appends extra " %" (line 497) => "15,00 % %". Run_Base.cs:751 pins CultureInfo.CreateSpecificCulture("FR")=fr-FR, so € and % are correct; Locale.FRANCE is justified. Two call sites consume only the string; splitting the switch compiles and breaks nothing. The adjusted fix (split branches, x100, double-percent, currency symbol append) is accurate.

### [#15] Multi-page PDF pages 2..N get NO position when POSITION_IMAGE is NULL (Java skips; .NET applies hardcoded default)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/task/impl/DocumentTaskProcessStrategy.java:250-254, 308-314`
- **Area:** document-task
- **Scenario:** A multi-page PDF task (FILE_PDF) with more than one page AND the DB column POSITION_IMAGE is NULL (the common case). TaskMapper.mapTaskDocument sets positionValues = getString(row,"POSITION_IMAGE"), and getString returns null for a NULL column.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentTaskProcessStrategy.java:250-254 `TaskImagePosition imagePosition = task.getImagePosition(); if (imagePosition == null && task.getPositionValues() != null) { imagePosition = new TaskImagePosition(task.getPositionValues()); task.setImagePosition(imagePosition); }`; line 308-309 loop guard `if (i > 0 && imagePosition != null && srcBox != null && srcBox.getGeometry() != null && srcBox.getGeometry().getPosition() != null) {`. TElementHelper.java:570 PDF_N template ships `box.getGeometry().setPosition(new Position());` (empty/null coords). TaskImagePosition.java:18 `(values != null ? values : "").split(";")` -> null gives length-1 array -> DEFAULT_IMAGE_POSITION_VALUES "42,52;56,693;552,756;785,197".
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_Document.cs:210, 250-255; Task_Image_Position.cs:42-59` — Process_Document.cs:210 `task.Image_Position =  new Task_Image_Position(task.Position_Values);` (unconditional); lines 250-255 `if (__i > 0) { __box.geometry.position.left = task.Image_Position.Left; __box.geometry.position.top = task.Image_Position.Top; __box.geometry.position.right = task.Image_Position.Right; __box.geometry.position.bottom = task.Image_Position.Bottom; }` (unconditional for __i>0). Task_Image_Position.cs:44-59 `string[] __values = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToString(values).Split(';'); if (__values.Length == 4) {...} else { _left=Default.Left; ... }` with DefImagePositionValues="42,52;56,693;552,756;785,197".
- **Fix:** Always construct the position helper like .NET, since the ctor already maps null/invalid -> DEFAULT. In DocumentTaskProcessStrategy.java replace the conditional init at lines 250-254 with an unconditional one:

  TaskImagePosition imagePosition = task.getImagePosition();
  if (imagePosition == null) {
      imagePosition = new TaskImagePosition(task.getPositionValues());
      task.setImagePosition(imagePosition);
  }

Then drop the redundant `imagePosition != null` clause from the loop guard at line 308 (keep the srcBox / geometry / position null checks, since the PDF_N template's Position object is what actually gets written). This restores the .NET behavior of applying the default position to pages i>0 when POSITION_IMAGE is NULL.

The offset helper change is optional and behavior-neutral; you may leave lines 242-246 and the imageOffset != null guards as-is, or apply the same unconditional-construction cleanup for symmetry without functional effect.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified all three points from real source (despite the finding's evidence naming "Process_File_PDF", the logic is in Process_Document.cs and matches). .NET always builds Image_Position (line 210) — null Position_Values -> ToString=="" -> Split gives 1 element != 4 -> DEFAULT coords — and always writes geometry for __i>0. Java only builds imagePosition when getPositionValues()!=null (line 251) and only writes when imagePosition!=null (line 308). POSITION_IMAGE is commonly NULL (TaskMapper sets positionValues=getString(...)=null), so Java leaves PDF_N's empty Position (null coords) on pages 2..N — a real rendering divergence. Java TaskImagePosition ctor reproduces the null->DEFAULT fallback, so the proposed unconditional construction (using existing Lombok getImagePosition/setImagePosition accessors) plus dropping the redundant imagePosition!=null guard makes Java identical to .NET and compiles cleanly. Offset change correctly flagged behavior-neutral. HIGH stands.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. .NET Process_Document.cs:210 unconditionally builds Task_Image_Position(Position_Values) and 250-256 unconditionally writes left/top/right/bottom for __i>0; ctor (44-59) falls back to DEFAULT "42,52;56,693;552,756;785,197" on null/non-4 split. Java DocumentTaskProcessStrategy.java:251 only builds imagePosition when getPositionValues()!=null, and line 308 guards on imagePosition!=null. TaskMapper:88 sets positionValues from POSITION_IMAGE (null for null column). PDF_N template TElementHelper.java:570 ships empty new Position() (JAXB null coords). So NULL POSITION_IMAGE leaves Java pages 2..N uncoordinated vs .NET default — real divergence. Fix (unconditional ctor + drop redundant imagePosition!=null guard) compiles (ctor handles null) and matches .NET. Offset is non-load-bearing cleanup, correctly noted.

### [#16] Gabarit without a DID box throws NPE on the prepareGabarit critical path, where .NET tolerates it  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/identity/DocumentIdentityService.java:123 (getElementValueByIdName returns null) + 140 (parseDocumentIdentity requireNonNull)`
- **Area:** did-task
- **Scenario:** A brand-new template / first-generation document (or any gabarit whose DID box is absent or has no RICHTEXT) goes through Run.prepareGabarit: fetchXmlForBox returns XML with no matching ID NAME='DID', getElementValueByIdName returns null (line 123), and Run.java:172 passes that null straight into parseDocumentIdentity, which immediately fails Objects.requireNonNull(identityString) at line 140 -> NullPointerException aborts the whole run preparation.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentIdentityService.java:122-123 `log.warn("No element found with ID NAME: {}", idName); return null;`. DocumentIdentityService.java:140 `Objects.requireNonNull(identityString, "identityString must not be null");`. Lines 142-143 throw on empty; 150-153 throw on <6 parts; parseDateTime 187-188/193-195 throw on empty/malformed dates. Run.java:170-172 fetchXmlForBox -> getElementValueByIdName -> parseDocumentIdentity with no null guard; Mode_Degrade early-return at Run.java:159-164 only covers oversize gabarits.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP_XML.cs:176-186; Document_Identity.cs:60-89; Document.cs:194-223 (Evaluate_Document_Identity); Run.cs:94 (this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity())` — QXP_XML.cs:181-185 `if (__iterator.MoveNext()) { return __iterator.Current.Value; } return string.Empty;` (XML doc: "la valeur si elle existe sinon string.empty"). Document_Identity.cs:64-65 `string[] __values = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToString(valeur_champs).Split('|'); if (__values.Length >= 6) {...}` — never throws; ToInt/ToDateTime are tolerant. Document.cs:205-210 `__str_identity = this.XML.GetValue(Document_Identity.DID); _identity = new Document_Identity(__str_identity);` else `throw new Exception_DID()` only when XML object null/non-QXP.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The fix's direction is right (make getElementValueByIdName return "" and make parseDocumentIdentity tolerant) but has gaps that would break compilation/parity. Precise version: (1) DocumentIdentityService.java:123 return "" instead of null when no matching ID NAME/RICHTEXT. (2) In parseDocumentIdentity: replace the requireNonNull(140) + empty-throw(142-143) + <6-parts-throw(150-153) with null/empty tolerance. CRITICAL: do NOT just delete requireNonNull — String.split(null) NPEs, so first do `String s = (identityString == null) ? "" : identityString;` then `String[] parts = s.split(PIPE_DELIMITER, -1);`. (3) Wrap the field-population (lines 156-169) in `if (parts.length >= MIN_IDENTITY_PARTS) { ... }`; for <6 parts return DocumentIdentity.builder().build() (all fields null) mirroring .NET defaults (idUnitCode defaults to "" in .NET vs null here — acceptable, both falsy for hasUnitCode/Is_Defined). (4) Make parseDateTime tolerant for ALL >=6-parts cases, not only the missing-DID path: .NET ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime never throws on a present-but-malformed date (returns default DateTime). So parseDateTime must return null (Java's nullable LocalDateTime default) instead of throwing on empty/unparseable input whenever parts.length>=6. Likewise idSuivi/idRun/idLangue: .NET parses these via ConversionInvariante.ToInt (defaults 0, never throws); current Java keeps them as raw trimmed Strings which is benign only if no downstream numeric parse throws — leave as String but ensure no later code re-parses and throws. (5) Optional: relax requireNonNull(81)/getElementValueByIdName for null xmlContent (fetchXmlForBox can return null per line 67-69) to return "" matching GetValue tolerance. (6) Optionally add an isDefined() to DocumentIdentity mirroring .NET Is_Defined if downstream distinguishes real vs default identity.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Bug is real: null DID value -> requireNonNull NPE aborts prepareGabarit for brand-new/no-DID gabarits that .NET produces fine (GetValue->string.Empty, tolerant Document_Identity ctor). Fix marked FIX_ADJUSTED because the proposed wording "remove the requireNonNull" without converting null->"" before split would NPE on split, and it scopes date tolerance to the "missing-DID path" only, whereas .NET's ToInt/ToDateTime are tolerant for ALL >=6-part inputs (present-but-malformed DID must also not throw). Severity HIGH confirmed: fatal abort of an entire run preparation on a path .NET tolerates.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java: getElementValueByIdName returns null (line 123); parseDocumentIdentity throws via requireNonNull(140), empty(142-143), <6 parts(150-153); parseDateTime throws on empty/malformed(187-195). Run.java:170-172 passes didValue with no null guard; Mode_Degrade return(159-163) only covers oversize. .NET differs: QXP_XML.GetValue returns string.Empty(185); Document_Identity(string) only fills when Length>=6, never throws; ConversionInvariante.ToInt/ToDateTime use TryParse returning defaults; Evaluate_Document_Identity throws only when XML null/non-QXP. Adjusted fix correctly catches split(null) NPE and parseDateTime tolerance; compiles (nullable builder fields) and doesn't break the second caller DocumentIdentityHelper.getIdentity. Minor: ToInt defaults int.MinValue not 0, but immaterial since Java keeps these as Strings.

### [#17] Mode-degrade is flagged for ALL document types; .NET only flags QXP documents  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `business/LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:195-201 (markModeDegradeIfTooBig), called at 97 and 153`
- **Area:** load-documents
- **Scenario:** A TaskDocument whose reference document is a large PDF or IMG (e.g. a multi-page brochure) larger than engine.gabarit.size-limit-before-fail-soft.
- **Java is (verified):** LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:195-201 — `private void markModeDegradeIfTooBig(DocumentDomain doc, Run run) { if (doc.getData() != null && doc.getData().length > run.getSizeLimitBeforeFailSoft()) { doc.setModeDegrade(true); log.warn(...); } }`. No document-type/format guard; called unconditionally at line 97 (TaskDocument) and line 153 (TaskQxpPrevious). Consumers: TaskDocument.java:85-86 and TaskQxpPrevious.java:41-42 both `return this.document != null && this.document.isModeDegrade();`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:228-239; Task_Document.cs:279-292; Task_QXP_Previous.cs:180-193; Document.cs:148-165 (GetDocumentType)` — Document.cs:230-238 `//Que pour les fichier QXP` `if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(this.Data)) { return (_data.Length > SizeLimitBeforeFailSoft); } else { return false; }`. Task_Document.cs:283-289 override `return this.Document.Mode_Degrade` (else false). Task_QXP_Previous.cs:184-190 identical. Document.cs:152-163 `switch(format.ToUpper()){... case "QXP": __type = Document_Type.QXP; break;}` — Type derives from the format string, so `"QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(format)` == `Type == Document_Type.QXP`.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The defect is real but the proposed fix is incomplete and the verdict's .NET citation is partly fabricated. There is NO `Mode_Degrade` override in Task_Document.cs or Task_QXP_Previous.cs (verified: only Task_Base.cs:372 exists, returning `false` unconditionally and never overridden). Thus in .NET a TaskDocument/TaskQxpPrevious is NEVER skipped due to its reference document's size — for ANY type, including QXP. Per-task reference-document size does not feed task skipping at all; Run_Base.cs:407/443/474 use `task.Mode_Degrade` (always false), and Run.Mode_Degrade (Run_Base.cs:915-926) only considers the Gabarit/Gabarit_Template, not per-task reference docs. The verdict's QXP-guard fix still leaves divergence: a large QXP reference doc (e.g. TaskQxpPrevious's previous certified QXP) would set doc.modeDegrade=true and Java's task.isModeDegrade() (TaskDocument.java:86, TaskQxpPrevious.java:42) would still skip the task, while .NET would process it. Correct fix: make per-task degrade independent of reference-document size, matching Task_Base (always false).
