Simplest faithful change — make TaskDocument.isModeDegrade()/TaskQxpPrevious.isModeDegrade() return false (inherit TaskBase), and delete the markModeDegradeIfTooBig calls at LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:97 and :153 (and the helper at 195-201), since document.isModeDegrade() has no other consumer in Java (only these two overrides read it). This compiles and breaks no other call site; RunProperties.modeDegrade (the real Run-level degrade) is set separately in Run.java:162.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java flags mode-degrade on byte length with no type guard; .NET (Evaluate_Mode_Degrade) only does so for Document_Type.QXP, and Type is derived from the format string ("QXP"->QXP), so non-QXP refs (PDF/IMG/DOC/RTF/XTG) are never degraded in .NET. Both Task overrides delegate to Document.Mode_Degrade, confirming the guard reaches the task level. The fix's `"QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(doc.getFormat())` exactly mirrors the .NET guard; DocumentDomain has getFormat() (field at line 44) and already uses this same pattern in getRatioSizeBox (line 249), and getSizeLimitBeforeFailSoft is already used at line 196 — so it compiles and matches .NET. HIGH is correct: silent dropped insertions for large non-QXP docs.
- **Skeptic:** CORRECTED -> FIX_ADJUSTED — .NET: Evaluate_Mode_Degrade (Document.cs:228-239) has the QXP guard, but Task_Base.Mode_Degrade (Task_Base.cs:372) returns false and is NEVER overridden — Task_Document.cs/Task_QXP_Previous.cs have zero Mode_Degrade references (grep-confirmed). Run.Mode_Degrade only checks the Gabarit, not per-task reference docs. So .NET never skips a TaskDocument/TaskQxpPrevious based on its reference doc size, even for QXP. Java wires task.isModeDegrade() to document.isModeDegrade() (byte-size only) and skips the task. The verdict's claimed Task overrides are fabricated, and its QXP-guard fix still wrongly skips large QXP reference docs. Defect real; fix must remove the per-task degrade wiring.

### [#18] Unknown boxRef in a group is logged-and-skipped instead of aborting the run (silent wrong geometry/missing boxes)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/element/TGroup.java:142-145`
- **Area:** telement-helper
- **Scenario:** A gabarit template TGroup whose <boxRefs> names a box/group that is not present in QxpProject.getElements() (e.g. mistyped ref, or the gabarit XML wasn't fully loaded before the Process phase — see KNOWN CONTEXT). evaluateBoxes hits the missing key.
- **Java is (verified):** TGroup.java:142-145 — `if (!elements.containsKey(refName)) { log.error("TGroup [{}] references unknown sub-element [{}]", getName(), refName); continue; }`. Caller QxpProject.java:213-220 wraps `tGroup.evaluate(this)` in try/catch and records `new RunError(2, ...)` on exception — but no exception is ever thrown today.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Dynamique/Template/TGroup.cs:157-160` — else { throw new Exception_TElement(Constantes.Error_Messages.TGroupUnKnownSubGroup, __boxRef.name, _srcGroup.name); }  (TGroup.cs line 159)
- **Fix:** In TGroup.evaluateBoxes, replace the log-and-continue at lines 142-145 with a thrown exception so the existing QxpProject.evaluatePendingGroups catch converts it into a CRITIQUE RunError (parity with .NET's Exception_TElement throw). Concretely:

    if (!elements.containsKey(refName)) {
        throw new IllegalStateException(
            String.format("Le sous-groupe [%s] du groupe [%s] est inconnu",
                refName, srcGroup != null ? srcGroup.getName() : getName()));
    }

(Use the project's actual TGroupUnKnownSubGroup message text/constant if one exists; the .NET message format is the equivalent of "unknown sub-group {0} in group {1}" with args boxRef.name and srcGroup.name.) Throwing a RuntimeException is sufficient because the caller already wraps the call and records RunError(2 = CRITIQUE) which interrupts the task. Do NOT add the RunError directly inside evaluateBoxes — that would double-record it via the caller's catch; let the existing caller mechanism handle it. Note (out of scope but adjacent): the subGroup case at Java lines 173-176 also only log.warn whereas .NET throws TGroupSubGroupProblem (TGroup.cs line 169); consider aligning that separately.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple fully verified. Java (TGroup.java:142-145) does log.error+continue for an unknown boxRef; .NET (TGroup.cs:159) throws Exception_TElement(TGroupUnKnownSubGroup) — a hard abort. The Java caller QxpProject.evaluatePendingGroups (lines 211-225) ALREADY wraps evaluate() in try/catch and records RunError(2=CRITIQUE), and RunError.java:23 documents CRITIQUE=2 as "interrupts the current task." So the parity design expects evaluateBoxes to throw; the log-and-continue bypasses it, dropping the box from srcBoxes and the min/max bounding box -> wrong Left/Top/Width/Height and a BlocGroup missing boxes, with no run error. Reachable via any mistyped/unloaded boxRef. The proposed fix (throw a RuntimeException at 142-145, no RunError inside evaluateBoxes) correctly restores fail-fast parity and compiles; srcGroup is already non-null here (early return at line 130) so the ternary is harmless redundancy. HIGH (silent geometry corruption vs .NET fail-fast) is correct. The author's adjacent note about subGroup (Java 173-176 log.warn vs .NET 169 throw TGroupSubGroupProblem) is accurately flagged as out-of-scope.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java TGroup.evaluateBoxes lines 142-145 only log.error+continue on unknown boxRef. .NET TGroup.cs lines 157-160 else-branch throws Exception_TElement(TGroupUnKnownSubGroup, ...) — a hard abort. The sole top-level caller QxpProject.evaluatePendingGroups (213-220) wraps evaluate() in try/catch and records RunError(2). RunError.java confirms CRITIQUE=2 interrupts the task. So Java currently silently drops the missing box from srcBoxes and the bounding box, producing corrupt geometry instead of failing. The fix (throw RuntimeException) propagates out of evaluate, is caught by the existing caller, and parallels .NET; it does not double-record. The recursive sub-group caller (line 163) propagates consistently with .NET's recursive Evaluate. Compiles fine; no other call sites break.

### [#19] Group structural errors are never recorded in Run.Errors — the entire logError path is dead code  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/project/QxpProject.java:211-226 (evaluatePendingGroups) + dependency domain/element/TGroup.java:142-145,173-176`
- **Area:** qxp-project (QxpProject.analyse / Elements map building)
- **Scenario:** DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy calls analyse(task, true) (line 444). A template TGroup references a boxRef name that is not in the elements map, OR a TGroup contains a sub-group. In .NET this surfaces a Critique error to the operator; in Java it is silently dropped.
- **Java is (verified):** TGroup.java:142-144 `if (!elements.containsKey(refName)) { log.error("TGroup [{}] references unknown sub-element [{}]", getName(), refName); continue; }`; TGroup.java:173-176 `if (subGroup) { log.warn("TGroup [{}] on page [{}] contains sub-groups — this may cause issues", getName(), page); }`; evaluate() never rethrows (TGroup.java:84-87 catches and returns). QxpProject.java:213-220 `try { tGroup.evaluate(this); } catch (Exception e) { if (logError && task != null) { ... task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(2, "Erreur evaluation du groupe [" + tGroup.getName() + "] : " + e.getMessage())); } }`. DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:444 `qxpProject.analyse(task, true);`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Dynamique/Template/TGroup.cs:159,169; QXP_Project.cs:140-146; Errors.cs:98-104; Error.cs:56,19` — TGroup.cs:159 `throw new Exception_TElement(Constantes.Error_Messages.TGroupUnKnownSubGroup, __boxRef.name, _srcGroup.name);` (else of ContainsKey); TGroup.cs:169 `throw new Exception_TElement(Constantes.Error_Messages.TGroupSubGroupProblem, _srcGroup.name, this.Page);` (when __subGroup). QXP_Project.cs:140-146 `catch (Exception_TElement tex) { if (logError) { task.Run.Errors.Add(tex.ToString()); } }`. Errors.cs:98 `public void Add(string message, params object[] args) { Error __error = new Error(message, args); ... }`; Error.cs:56 `public Error(string message, params object[] args): this(Error_Type.Unspecified, message, args){}` (Error_Type.Unspecified = 1).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Bug confirmed: Java replaced .NET's two throws with log.error+continue (TGroup.java:142-145) and log.warn (TGroup.java:173-176), so QxpProject.evaluatePendingGroups' catch never fires and structural group errors are silently dropped on analyse(task,true) (DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy:444). Restore the throws as the finding proposes: introduce a RuntimeException (e.g. ExceptionTElement). (1) Unknown boxRef: at TGroup.java:142-145 replace log.error+continue with `throw new ExceptionTElement(TGroupUnKnownSubGroup, refName, getName())` (aborts the whole group on first unknown ref, matching .NET's foreach throw). (2) Sub-group: at TGroup.java:173-176 replace log.warn with `if (subGroup) throw new ExceptionTElement(TGroupSubGroupProblem, getName(), getPage())`. Let it bubble out of evaluate() (do not let the reflection catch at TGroup.java:84-87 swallow it — that catch only guards the getElements reflection, so it is fine). CORRECTION to the finding's claim about the recorded error: the proposed fix keeps `new RunError(2, ...)` (CRITIQUE) and asserts that matches .NET's `Errors.Add(string)` default — it does NOT. .NET's Errors.Add(string) -> new Error(message) -> Error(Error_Type.Unspecified) = category 1 (UNSPECIFIED), not 2. RunError.java itself documents this numeric value is persisted to the DB (Insert_Run_Errors) and is load-bearing. So change QxpProject.java:219 to `new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED, ...)` (i.e. category 1). Also, to match the persisted message, .NET stores `tex.ToString()` (Exception_TElement chain message), not a hand-built French string; the Java message text should mirror the exception's formatted message rather than the custom "Erreur evaluation du groupe [...]" wrapper. Net: keep the throw-restoration, but set category to 1 (UNSPECIFIED) and align the message to the exception text for true parity.
- **Reconciler note:** The core defect (dead catch / silently dropped structural errors) is real and confirmed in both sources on the production dynamic-task path. The proposed throw-restoration is the right approach. However the fix explicitly preserves RunError(2 = CRITIQUE) and justifies it as matching .NET — I verified the .NET Errors.Add(string) overload constructs Error with Error_Type.Unspecified (=1), so the correct category is UNSPECIFIED (1). Since RunError.category is persisted to the DB and load-bearing per the class's own docs, this is a genuine parity error in the fix, hence FIX_ADJUSTED. Message text should also mirror tex.ToString() rather than the custom wrapper. Severity HIGH stands: operator-facing structural errors are lost on the dynamic-task path.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. .NET TGroup.cs:159/:169 throw Exception_TElement for unknown boxRef (aborts loop) and post-loop sub-group; Java (TGroup.java:142-145, 173-176) replaced these with log.error+continue / log.warn, and evaluate() never rethrows, so QxpProject.java:214-220's catch is dead code — real HIGH regression on analyse(task,true). Reconciler's category correction is right: .NET QXP_Project.cs:145 Errors.Add(string)->new Error(message)->Error(Error_Type.Unspecified), NOT Critique. RunError.java authoritatively maps UNSPECIFIED=1, CRITIQUE=2, so Java's hardcoded RunError(2,...) is wrong; should be UNSPECIFIED(1). Message-alignment refinement reasonable. Fix compiles: ExceptionTElement is correctly introduced new, getName()/getPage() exist, sole caller is try/catch-wrapped, arg order matches .NET.

### [#20] Insert_Data array params bound as OracleTypes.ARRAY but Oracle type is a PL/SQL associative array (INDEX BY) — binding fails at runtime  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.java:43-46, 55-58`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** Any run with store_data enabled (StoreDataType SQL or DOCUMENT) calls insertData; the 4 String[] arrays are bound via new SqlParameter(name, OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY") and a raw String[].
- **Java is (verified):** InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.java:43-46: new SqlParameter("p_names", OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY") [+ p_values/p_descriptifs/p_infos]; lines 55-58 params.put("p_names", names) raw String[]. EndRunDaoImpl.java:96-97: new SqlParameter("p_messages", OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY") / new SqlParameter("p_categories", OracleTypes.ARRAY, "QXP_PK_COMMON.NUMBERARRAY").
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.SQL/06 - Packages/618 - QXP_PK_COMMON/01 - QXP_PK_COMMON.sql:11-12; QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Error.cs:122-124; ora.txt:3086-3090` — QXP_PK_COMMON.sql:11 "TYPE VarCharArray IS TABLE OF VARCHAR2(4000) INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER;" / :12 "TYPE NumberArray IS TABLE OF NUMBER INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER;". Proxy_Error.cs:122-124 __oraParameters.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_id_run", run.ID).AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_messages", __msgs.ToArray()).AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_categories", __cats.ToArray()). ora.txt:3086 "p_names IN qxp_pk_common.varchararray".
- **Fix:** Bind the associative arrays via the Oracle PL/SQL index-table API rather than OracleTypes.ARRAY. Two practical options: (1) Drop SimpleJdbcCall for these calls and use a JdbcTemplate CallableStatementCreator that unwraps to OraclePreparedStatement and calls setPlsqlIndexTable(index, javaArray, maxLen, currentLen, OracleTypes.VARCHAR, elementMaxLen) for each VarCharArray param (and OracleTypes.NUMBER for NumberArray in EndRunDaoImpl), with an explicit anonymous-block CALL like `{ call QXP_PK_DATA_STORAGE.Insert_Data(?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?) }`. (2) Add a small wrapper PL/SQL procedure / or change the API to accept a schema-level CREATE TYPE ... AS TABLE OF VARCHAR2(4000) nested table and have the proc convert (QXP_PK_COMMON already exposes ARRAY_TO_TABLE_VARCHAR2), then OracleTypes.ARRAY with a real schema type works via a StructDescriptor/createOracleArray SqlTypeValue. Option (1) is the lowest-risk parity fix and avoids DB DDL changes. Apply the identical fix to EndRunDaoImpl.java:96-97 (both QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY and QXP_PK_COMMON.NUMBERARRAY). Keep the existing length-0/null guard.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Oracle type is INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER (PL/SQL associative array), not a schema-level SQL collection. Java's OracleTypes.ARRAY + package-qualified type name routes through createOracleArray, which resolves only data-dictionary collection types; a package-scoped associative array is unresolvable, so binding fails deterministically at runtime (ORA-00902/PLS-00306). .NET passes plain arrays through QXP.Framework's OraParameterList, whose only valid ODP.NET mechanism for an INDEX BY type is CollectionType.PLSQLAssociativeArray (the framework binder itself is outside the dump, but the type forces it). No setPlsqlIndexTable/createOracleArray/SqlTypeValue helper exists in the Java project. Proposed Option-1 fix (JdbcTemplate CallableStatementCreator unwrapping to OraclePreparedStatement.setPlsqlIndexTable with element type VARCHAR for VARCHARARRAY and NUMBER for NUMBERARRAY, covering both DAOs) is the correct JDBC equivalent and achieves parity. Severity HIGH: loud deterministic failure on the feature-gated store_data / run-error paths, not silent corruption.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed in both sources. Java: InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.java:43-46/55-58 and EndRunDaoImpl.java:96-97/102-103 bind arrays as OracleTypes.ARRAY with package type name "QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY"/"NUMBERARRAY" and pass raw String[]/int[] via SimpleJdbcCall. .NET QXP_PK_COMMON.sql:11-13 declares these as TYPE ... IS TABLE OF ... INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER (PL/SQL associative arrays); procedures (ora.txt:3086, 9450) take qxp_pk_common.varchararray. No schema-level CREATE TYPE VARCHARARRAY exists, so OracleTypes.ARRAY's createOracleArray path cannot resolve a package associative array — deterministic runtime bind failure. Associative arrays need setPlsqlIndexTable, unsupported by SimpleJdbcCall. Fix direction (JdbcTemplate+OraclePreparedStatement.setPlsqlIndexTable) is correct, compiles, parity-faithful, no other call sites. Note: exact ODP.NET PLSQLAssociativeArray mechanism inferred (proxy in absent assembly) but type's INDEX BY nature makes it the only valid binding.

### [#21] DATE_TIME (type 5) param is bound as a date-truncated java.sql.Date instead of the raw string the .NET engine passes  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `mapper/InParamSqlMapper.java:28-33`
- **Area:** mappers
- **Scenario:** A run InParam whose INPUT_DATA_TYPE = 5 (DATE_TIME) — e.g. a timestamp parameter with a time-of-day — is fed into a Task_SQL bind variable via ProcessSqlBusiness.execute -> taskSqlDao.executeSql.
- **Java is (verified):** InParamSqlMapper.java:28-30 `if ((inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE || inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE_TIME) ... ) { params.put(entry.getKey(), toSqlDate(inParam.getStringValue())); }`; line 45-46 `LocalDate date = LocalDate.parse(dateValue.trim(), INPUT_FORMAT); return Date.valueOf(date);` — converts BOTH DATE(4) and DATE_TIME(5) to java.sql.Date, discarding time-of-day.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:154-167; BusinessObject/Run/InParam/InParam.cs:54; BusinessObject/Run/InParam/InParams.cs:69; QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:301-314` — Data_Type_Helper.cs:158-165 `case Data_Type.Int: ... case Data_Type.Decimal: ... case Data_Type.Date: return ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime(value); default: return value;` (DateTime(5) hits default -> raw string). ConversionInvariante.cs:306 `DateTime.TryParse(value, ... out __newvalue)` preserves time. InParam.cs:54 `_value = Data_Type_Helper.InputToTypedValue(_string_value, _type);`; InParams.cs:69 `new OraParameter(__paramName, __inParam.Value)`. Data_Type.cs: Date=4, DateTime=5.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The fix is directionally correct (DATE_TIME=5 must bind the raw string; DATE=4 must preserve time) but as written it is incomplete/non-compilable: it keeps toSqlDate returning java.sql.Date, which cannot hold the time-of-day, so DATE(4) would still be truncated. Make it concrete:

1) Narrow the branch to DATE only:
   `if (inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE && inParam.getStringValue() != null && !inParam.getStringValue().isEmpty()) { params.put(entry.getKey(), toSqlTimestamp(inParam.getStringValue())); } else { params.put(entry.getKey(), inParam.getStringValue()); }`
   This makes DATE_TIME(5) and every other type bind the raw string, matching .NET's default branch.

2) Replace toSqlDate with a time-preserving Timestamp converter:
   `import java.sql.Timestamp; import java.time.LocalDateTime;`
   `private Timestamp toSqlTimestamp(String v) { try { return Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDateTime.parse(v.trim(), INPUT_FORMAT)); } catch (Exception e) { try { return Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDate.parse(v.trim()).atStartOfDay()); } catch (Exception e2) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Unable to parse date parameter value: '" + v + "'", e2); } } }`
   Note INPUT_FORMAT "M/d/yyyy HH:mm:ss" already carries the time fields, so LocalDateTime.parse keeps HH:mm:ss — mirroring .NET's DateTime. Remove the now-unused java.sql.Date/LocalDate import only if no longer referenced.

(INT/DECIMAL typed conversion remains out of scope; binding them as strings is acceptable given Oracle implicit conversion and is unchanged.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java truncates time for both DATE(4) and DATE_TIME(5) and binds DATE_TIME as a sql.Date rather than the raw string .NET passes. .NET: DateTime(5) -> default branch returns raw string; Date(4) -> ToDateTime via DateTime.TryParse, preserving time. ora.txt:13471/13580/13668 use TO_DATE(sv_prm.valeur,'MM/DD/YYYY HH24:MI:SS') and compare with time precision, so truncation can change result sets. Bug is real. The proposed fix has the right intent but keeps toSqlDate->java.sql.Date, which still drops time for DATE(4); adjustedFix binds DATE via java.sql.Timestamp (LocalDateTime) and DATE_TIME as raw string. HIGH severity stands: silent wrong/missing rows in generated documents.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java InParamSqlMapper.java:28-30 routes BOTH DATE(4) and DATE_TIME(5) to toSqlDate -> java.sql.Date (LocalDate.parse), stripping time-of-day. .NET differs: Data_Type_Helper.cs:158-166 types only Int(2)/Decimal(3)/Date(4); DateTime(5) hits default returning the raw string; ConversionInvariante.cs:306 DateTime.TryParse preserves time. InParam.cs:54 stores InputToTypedValue result as Value; InParams.cs:69 binds it. Data_Type.cs: Date=4, DateTime=5. So DATE(4) should bind a time-preserving value and DATE_TIME(5) the raw string. The reconciler's adjusted fix (narrow to DATE only, replace toSqlDate with toSqlTimestamp via Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDateTime.parse(...,INPUT_FORMAT))) correctly mirrors .NET, compiles (getters/enum/INPUT_FORMAT exist; java.sql.Date import becomes unused but harmless), and both call sites (ProcessSqlBusiness:43, DynamicQueryPortImpl:40) accept Map<String,Object> unchanged. Original one-liner fix kept java.sql.Date and would still truncate DATE; adjustment is warranted.

### [#22] Dynamic task prepare uploads the run's source gabarit instead of the gabarit_template  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/task/TaskDynamique.java:40-41`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Any dynamic task with todo=true. prepare() does getRun().getGabarit() (the run source document) and uploads it, instead of the template gabarit.
- **Java is (verified):** TaskDynamique.java:40-41: `DocumentDomain gabaritTemplate = this.getRun().getGabarit();` / `filePoolService.addFile(gabaritTemplate.getFilePoolPath(), gabaritTemplate.getData());`. Run.java:30 declares only `private DocumentDomain gabarit;` (no gabaritTemplate field). LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:43 only calls getGabaritTemplateDao.getTemplates(idGabaritTemplate) and never getGabaritTemplate(int) nor sets a template document on the run.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Task/Task_Dynamique.cs:60; Run_Base.cs:50-51,859-871; Run.cs:147-163` — Task_Dynamique.cs:60: `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Run.Gabarit_Template.FilePoolPath, this.Run.Gabarit_Template.Data);` — Gabarit_Template, not Gabarit. Run_Base.cs:50-51 declares two distinct fields `_gabarit` and `_gabarit_Template`; Run.cs:152 loads `this.Gabarit_Template = __proxy.Get_Gabarit_Template(this);` when at least one todo dynamic task exists and ID_Gabarit_Template is set.
- **Fix:** Sequenced fix (depends on finding #2): (1) Add a `DocumentDomain gabaritTemplate` field to Run.java with getter/setter. (2) Wire its loading: when there is at least one todo dynamic task and runProperties.idGabaritTemplate != Integer.MIN_VALUE, call GetGabaritTemplateDao.getGabaritTemplate(idGabaritTemplate), store the result on run.setGabaritTemplate(...), and (mirroring .NET prepareGabarit) add it to the file pool / parse its DID as appropriate. This is the analogue of .NET Run.cs LoadTemplates loading Gabarit_Template, which LoadTemplatesBusiness currently omits. (3) Change TaskDynamique.prepare() to upload the template: `DocumentDomain gabaritTemplate = this.getRun().getGabaritTemplate();` then `filePoolService.addFile(gabaritTemplate.getFilePoolPath(), gabaritTemplate.getData());` (keep a null check / Exception_Task-equivalent if gabaritTemplate is null, matching .NET MSG_Gabarit_Template_NULL). (4) Resolve the fileName-vs-filePoolPath key inconsistency (finding #1) consistently so the document is uploaded and later looked up under the same key.
- **Reconciler note:** All three legs verified from source. Java uploads getRun().getGabarit() (the source document) where .NET uploads Run.Gabarit_Template — confirmed two genuinely distinct Document fields in Run_Base.cs:50-51/859-871, with Gabarit_Template loaded separately in Run.cs:152 from Properties.ID_Gabarit_Template. The Java Run domain has no gabaritTemplate field at all, and GetGabaritTemplateDao.getGabaritTemplate(int) (returns DocumentDomain) is never called; LoadTemplatesBusiness loads only template rows. Path is reachable: ProcessTasksServiceImpl.prepareTasks() -> task.prepare() guarded by isTodo() && filePoolService != null. Net effect: dynamic bloc cloning runs against the wrong pool document — silent wrong/empty output, not a crash, so HIGH is correct (not CRITICAL). The proposed fix is sound and matches .NET: it adds the missing Run.gabaritTemplate field, wires its load mirroring LoadTemplates/Get_Gabarit_Template (gated on a todo dynamic task + idGabaritTemplate set, with MSG_Gabarit_Template_NULL/MSG_Missing_ID_Gabarit_Template equivalent), and changes prepare() to upload the template. The getFileName-vs-getFilePoolPath key reconciliation (step 4, tied to finding #1) is correctly flagged as a separate concern.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java TaskDynamique.java:40-41 uploads getRun().getGabarit() (the run SOURCE document); Run.java:30 declares only `gabarit`, with no gabaritTemplate field, and getGabaritTemplate(int) (DAO impl line 68) is never called. LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:43 only loads template metadata rows. .NET Task_Dynamique.cs:60 uploads this.Run.Gabarit_Template (FilePoolPath/Data) — a genuinely distinct Document; Run_Base.cs:50-51 declares separate _gabarit and _gabarit_Template, and Run.cs:152 loads Gabarit_Template via Get_Gabarit_Template when ID_Gabarit_Template is set. Real parity defect. Fix is sound: adding the field is safe (@AllArgsConstructor unused; only new Run()/new Run(long) callers exist), and the proposed prepare() line mirrors .NET.

### [#23] GABARIT source with no DID throws NPE/IllegalArgument; .NET tolerates an empty identity  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/identity/DocumentIdentityService.java:139-154 (parseDocumentIdentity); called from Run.java:171-172`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Gabarit_Source = GABARIT (a fresh template that has no DID box) or any generated doc whose DID box is missing/empty. getElementValueByIdName returns null -> parseDocumentIdentity(null) hits Objects.requireNonNull -> NPE; an empty/short DID string -> IllegalArgumentException ('empty' or 'parts.length < 6'). Run aborts during load.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentIdentityService.java:140 Objects.requireNonNull(identityString, "identityString must not be null"); :142-143 if (identityString.trim().isEmpty()) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Identity string must not be empty"); } :150-153 if (parts.length < MIN_IDENTITY_PARTS) { throw new IllegalArgumentException(...); }. Run.java:170-172 fetches DID box and unconditionally calls parseDocumentIdentity(didValue); getElementValueByIdName returns null (line 123) when no DID box -> NPE.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document_Identity.cs:60-89; Document.cs:194-223` — Document_Identity.cs:62-65 _value = valeur_champs; string[] __values = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToString(valeur_champs).Split('|'); if (__values.Length >= 6) {...}  (else: no throw, fields stay default). :83 if(__values.Length >= 7){ _id_Unit_Code = __values[6]; }. Document.cs:203-211 if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXPFrk.Validation.IsNotNull(this.XML)) { _identity = new Document_Identity(this.XML.GetValue(DID)); } else { throw new Exception_DID(); } — throws only when XML is null, not when DID value empty/short.
- **Fix:** Make parseDocumentIdentity lenient to mirror .NET's Document_Identity(string) constructor: when identityString is null, blank, or splits into fewer than MIN_IDENTITY_PARTS (6) parts, return an empty/default DocumentIdentity (all fields null/default) instead of throwing — do not call Objects.requireNonNull and remove the empty-string and parts.length<6 throws. Only populate fields when parts.length >= 6 (and the optional unit code when parts.length > 6), exactly as .NET's `if (__values.Length >= 6)` / `if (__values.Length >= 7)` gating. Keep the date-parse validation only inside the >=6 branch (matching .NET which only parses dates when the 6-field block is present); note .NET uses lenient invariant conversions (ToInt/ToDateTime tolerate bad values), so consider degrading the date parse failures to defaults rather than hard-throwing if strict parity is desired. Preserve the genuinely-fatal case (no XML at all) at the Run/Evaluate level — that maps to .NET's Exception_DID thrown only when this.XML is null — rather than in the identity-string parser. Add/keep a way to detect 'incomplete' identity downstream (an isDefined()-style check) to match .NET's Is_Defined semantics.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple from source. Java parseDocumentIdentity (line 140-153) throws on null/empty/<6-parts; Run.java:170-172 reaches this for every gabarit source including GABARIT templates that have no DID box (getElementValueByIdName returns null). .NET Document_Identity(string) ctor only assigns fields when Split('|').Length>=6 and otherwise leaves defaults without throwing; Conversion.ToString normalizes null->empty. Evaluate_Document_Identity throws Exception_DID only when this.XML is null, never for empty/short DID. So Java wrongly aborts the load. Fix is correct in direction: drop requireNonNull, drop empty and <6 throws, return empty DocumentIdentity, populate only when >=6 (>6 for unit code) — exact mirror of .NET gating; empty identity is buildable via builder().build(). The fix's own caveat about lenient date parsing is optional and does not affect correctness of the primary fix. SOUND.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed in both sources. Java DocumentIdentityService.java:140 calls Objects.requireNonNull(identityString), :142-143 throws on empty, :150-153 throws when <6 pipe parts; getElementValueByIdName returns null (line 123) when no DID box. Run.java:170-172 unconditionally parses didValue for ALL gabarit sources (including GABARIT, switch line 129), so a template lacking a DID box yields null -> NPE. Second caller DocumentIdentityHelper.getIdentity:86 shares the same null-prone path. .NET Document_Identity.cs:60-89 normalizes null via Conversion.ToString and only assigns fields when Length>=6, never throwing; Document.cs:203-211 throws Exception_DID only when XML is null. Genuine parity defect; lenient fix compiles (object-typed fields) and helps both callers.

### [#24] Mode_Degrade skips the gabarit pool upload, but Render still runs in degraded mode and needs the pooled document  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:158-176`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Oversized gabarit -> line 159 sets modeDegrade and returns early before filePoolPort.addFile. ProcessRunServiceImpl still calls qxpsCallerService.render() in degraded mode (render is outside the !modeDegrade guard, matching .NET). Render references gabarit.getFilePoolPath() but the file was never uploaded.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:159-167: `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { log.warn(...); this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); return; }` ... then `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());`. The early return precedes the addFile, so an oversized gabarit is never uploaded. ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:94 calls qxpsCallerService.render() OUTSIDE the `if (!run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade())` block (lines 69-91). QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197: `String documentName = run.getGabarit().getFilePoolPath();`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run.cs:92,94; Document.cs:200,228-233` — Run.cs:92 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data);` (unconditional, before any degrade check), Run.cs:94 `this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity();`. Document.cs:200 inside Evaluate_Document_Identity `if (!this.Mode_Degrade) { ... }` — only the DID/XML parse is degrade-gated. Document.cs:228-233 Evaluate_Mode_Degrade returns `(_data.Length > SizeLimitBeforeFailSoft)` — Mode_Degrade is a computed property, not set during PrepareGabarit.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** In Run.prepareGabarit, after the `if (this.gabarit == null) return;` null check (Run.java:154-156), upload to the pool UNCONDITIONALLY, then evaluate degrade and gate ONLY the DID fetch/parse, mirroring .NET (Addfile unconditional at Run.cs:92; Evaluate_Document_Identity at :94 internally skips only the DID parse when Mode_Degrade). Concretely:
1) `filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), this.gabarit.getData());` — use getFilePoolPath(), NOT getFileName(), so the uploaded pool name exactly matches what render() requests (QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197 uses getFilePoolPath()) and what .NET uses (Gabarit.FilePoolPath for both Addfile and render). For the gabarit these currently resolve to the same value (GetDocumentByIdDaoImpl.java:77-80 sets fileName==filePoolPath), so getFileName() works today by coincidence, but getFilePoolPath() is the .NET-faithful, render-aligned choice.
2) Then: `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { log.warn(...); this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); return; }` so degraded mode skips ONLY the DID fetch/parse.
3) Keep the existing DID fetch/parse (fetchXmlForBox/getElementValueByIdName/parseDocumentIdentity + setDocumentIdentity) AFTER that guard.
The finding's core fix (move addFile before the early return) is correct; the only adjustment is using getFilePoolPath() instead of getFileName() for the addFile argument to strictly match render() and .NET.
- **Reconciler note:** Bug is real and confirmed against both sources. .NET uploads the gabarit to the pool unconditionally (Run.cs:92) before any degrade evaluation; only the DID/XML parse is gated by Mode_Degrade (Document.cs:200). Java's prepareGabarit returns early on oversized gabarit BEFORE the addFile (Run.java:163), so the pool document is missing. render() runs in degraded mode (ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:94, outside the degrade guard) and resolves the source via getFilePoolPath() (QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197) — degraded render fails/returns empty. Defeats the very purpose of safe-mode rendering. HIGH severity confirmed. Fix is essentially right but I adjusted the addFile argument from getFileName() to getFilePoolPath() to align with render()/.NET; today they are equal (DAO sets both to prefix_id.format) so it works, but getFilePoolPath() is the faithful, robust choice.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java Run.java:159-167 returns early (sets modeDegrade) BEFORE filePoolPort.addFile, so oversized gabarit is never pooled. .NET Run.cs:92 Addfile is unconditional; Mode_Degrade is a computed property (Document.cs:228-233) and only gates the DID parse inside Evaluate_Document_Identity (Document.cs:200). ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:94 calls render() outside the !modeDegrade block, and render uses getFilePoolPath() (QxpsCallerBusiness.java:197) — genuine divergence. Adjusted fix is sound: DocumentDomain has @Getter+filePoolPath field so getFilePoolPath() compiles; DAO sets fileName==filePoolPath (GetDocumentByIdDaoImpl.java:78,80), so no call site breaks, and getFilePoolPath() is the render-/.NET-aligned choice.

### [#25] Full gabarit XML never loaded during load; only box-scoped DID XML is fetched  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:170-176`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** prepareGabarit fetches XML scoped to the 'DID' box (FetchXmlMessage('DID')) and never populates gabarit.qxpXml. Any task/strategy reading run.getGabarit().getQxpXml() during Prepare/Process gets QxpXml.EMPTY until CheckServiceImpl loads the full XML much later.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:170-175: `String xmlContent = documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFileName(), "DID"); ... DocumentIdentity identity = documentIdentityPort.parseDocumentIdentity(didValue); this.gabarit.setDocumentIdentity(identity);` (never sets qxpXml). DocumentDomain.java:104-108: `public QxpXml getQxpXml() { if (this.qxpXml == null) { return QxpXml.EMPTY; } return this.qxpXml; }`. QxpXml.java:122-126: EMPTY = `<PROJECT></PROJECT>`. Only setter during run window: CheckServiceImpl.java:96 `gabarit.initXmlFromContent(xmlContent);` inside refreshGabaritXml, first called at check() (CheckServiceImpl.java:53), invoked from ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:89 — after prepareTasks (72)/processTasks (81). Consumers read it earlier: TaskBase.java:57-58 getPageNum/getLayoutName, RunTaskStep.java:121-122 getNbBox, BlocPage.java:51 getNbBoxOnPage.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:421-446; Run.cs:92-99; Task_Base.cs:95-96` — Document.cs:421-444 (XML getter): `if(this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && ...IsNull(_xml)) { if (!this.Mode_Degrade) { _xml = QXPS_File_Manager.Get_XML(this); } else { _xml = QXP_XML.Empty; } } return _xml;` — lazy-loads and CACHES the FULL document XML (Get_XML, no box filter). Run.cs:92-99: after `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(...)` it calls `this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity();` then reads `this.Gabarit.XML.Project_Info.NbBox/NbPage` (line 99) — first access populates the cache. Task_Base.cs:95-96: `this.Properties.PageNum = this.Run.Gabarit.XML.GetPageNum(this.DestinationBlocName); this.Properties.LayoutName = this.Run.Gabarit.XML.GetLayoutName(...)` — reads the cached full XML during prepare/process.
- **Fix:** Mirror .NET: populate the gabarit's full XML during prepareGabarit, right after the Addfile step and before/alongside DID parsing, so getGabarit().getQxpXml() returns the real document XML throughout Prepare/Process. Concretely, after filePoolPort.addFile(...) in Run.prepareGabarit (Run.java:167), fetch the full XML and set it: e.g. this.gabarit.initXmlFromContent(getGabaritXmlBusiness.fetchXml(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath())) — the full-XML fetch already exists (GetGabaritXmlBusiness.fetchXml via FetchXmlMessage() with no box filter). To keep the domain I/O-free, pass this capability in as a port/functional dependency to prepareGabarit (same pattern as documentIdentityPort), or have ProcessRunServiceImpl.load() set the gabarit XML immediately after run.prepareGabarit(...) (line 171) using getGabaritXmlBusiness. Then the existing box-scoped DID parse can remain (or be derived from the full XML via getElementValueByIdName). Guard for null/empty fetch (skip set, leave EMPTY) and respect Mode_Degrade (do not fetch when modeDegrade, matching .NET XML getter which returns Empty in degraded mode). CheckServiceImpl.refreshGabaritXml stays as-is for the post-execution refresh.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. .NET Document.XML lazy-loads+caches the FULL doc XML (Get_XML, no box filter) on first access during PrepareGabarit (Run.cs:99), so all prepare/process consumers (Task_Base.Evaluate_Info etc.) see real layout. Java never populates gabarit.qxpXml before Check; getQxpXml() returns QxpXml.EMPTY (empty PROJECT), so getPageNum=0/empty layout/getNbBox=0 silently for the entire prepare/process window — wrong page/layout/box/complexity math with no exception. Fix is correct: GetGabaritXmlBusiness.fetchXml uses `new FetchXmlMessage()` (no box) = full XML matching .NET Get_XML; populate it right after filePoolPort.addFile in the non-degraded path (early-return at Run.java:163 already skips degraded mode, matching .NET's Empty-in-degraded). Guard null/empty. Note: option (b) requires injecting GetGabaritXmlBusiness into ProcessRunServiceImpl (currently only in CheckServiceImpl); option (a) passes it as a port to keep domain I/O-free. Either is sound. HIGH confirmed.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed both sides. Java Run.java:170-175 fetches only box-scoped DID XML and never sets qxpXml; DocumentDomain.getQxpXml() (104-108) returns QxpXml.EMPTY (non-lazy). .NET Document.cs:421-446 XML getter lazy-loads the FULL document via Get_XML(this) (no box param) and caches it; Run.cs:94 Evaluate_Document_Identity reads this.XML (Document.cs:203-205) populating that cache, line 99 reads NbBox/NbPage. Consumers (TaskBase.java:57-58/73, matching Task_Base.cs:95-96) read getQxpXml during prepare(72)/process(81), but Java first populates full XML only at check()/refreshGabaritXml (step 6). Genuine parity break -> silent EMPTY data. Fix uses existing getGabaritXmlBusiness.fetchXml (full-XML, already used in CheckServiceImpl/LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness); requires injecting it into ProcessRunServiceImpl plus null/modeDegrade guard, both noted.

### [#26] DID XML fetch uses getFileName() (root) instead of getFilePoolPath() (R_<id>/), inconsistent with .NET and with the actual modify/render path  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:170`
- **Area:** pool
- **Scenario:** documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFileName(), "DID") fetches the DID box from the pool-root copy. This only resolves because line 167 also (wrongly) uploaded to root. If line 167 is corrected to getFilePoolPath() (as .NET does) without also fixing line 170, the DID fetch will hit a non-existent root document and the run fails DID parsing.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:167  filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData());
Run.java:170  String xmlContent = documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFileName(), "DID");
GetGabaritBusiness.java:91  gabarit.setFilePoolPath(runProperties.getPoolPath(gabarit.getFileName()));
RunProperties.java:75  return String.format("R_%d/%s", this.runId, fileName);
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run.cs:92; Document.cs:23 (PoolPattern), Document.cs:430 (Get_XML), Document.cs:118/137/175 (_filePoolPath via GetPoolPath), Document.cs:299-301 (FilePoolPath property)` — Run.cs:23  const string PoolPattern = "R_{0}\\";
Run.cs:92  QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data);
Run.cs:94  this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity();
Document.cs:205  string __str_identity = this.XML.GetValue(Document_Identity.DID);
Document.cs:430  _xml = QXPS_File_Manager.Get_XML(this);
Document.cs:118  _filePoolPath = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(_fileName);
- **Fix:** Change both lines in Run.java to use the R_-prefixed pool path so they match .NET and the rest of the engine: line 167 -> filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), this.gabarit.getData()); and line 170 -> documentIdentityPort.fetchXmlForBox(this.gabarit.getFilePoolPath(), "DID"). getFilePoolPath() is already populated by GetGabaritBusiness.preparePaths (line 91) to R_<runId>/<fileName>. Do not fix line 170 in isolation; both must change together so the DID fetch hits the same pool document that was uploaded and that the downstream modify/render path (QxpsCallerBusiness.executeStep, gabarit.getFilePoolPath()) operates on.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java Run.java:167 and :170 both use the bare getFileName(); getFilePoolPath() resolves to R_<runId>/<fileName> (GetGabaritBusiness:91 + RunProperties:75), so they genuinely differ. .NET uploads at Run.cs:92 via this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath (R_-prefixed; PoolPattern "R_{0}\\") and Evaluate_Document_Identity reads this.XML, lazy-loaded by Get_XML(this) (Document.cs:430) which operates on the Document's FilePoolPath. So .NET keys both upload and DID/XML load on the R_-prefixed path. Java diverges on both lines; downstream modify/render (QxpsCallerBusiness:106,197) already uses getFilePoolPath(), so the bare-root upload is never seen by the render step. The proposed fix changes BOTH 167 and 170 to getFilePoolPath() and correctly warns against fixing one in isolation, which makes Java match .NET. Minor: evidence line refs for Get_XML are imprecise (actual 430, not 235-242) but verifyNote corrects this; substance sound. HIGH justified for non-degraded runs.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Independently confirmed. Java Run.java:167/170 both use bare getFileName(); getFilePoolPath()=R_<runId>/<file> (RunProperties:71-75), populated by GetGabaritBusiness:91. Every other Java pool consumer (QxpsCallerBusiness:106/197, CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy:243, TaskDynamique:41) keys on getFilePoolPath(), so Run.java is the lone outlier. .NET Run.cs:92 uploads via Gabarit.FilePoolPath (R_-prefixed, PoolPattern "R_{0}\\"); Evaluate_Document_Identity->XML getter->Get_XML(this) (Document.cs:430), and engine universally keys pool ops on FilePoolPath (Run_Base:954, Task_Dynamique.cs:60). addFile/fetchXmlForBox pass the name straight through, so changing both lines to getFilePoolPath() compiles and breaks nothing. Caveat: QXPS_File_Manager.Get_XML body is in an external assembly not in source, but FilePoolPath-keying is consistent throughout. Fix must change both lines together, as stated.

### [#27] Modify XML uploaded to pool ROOT without R_<id>/ scoping, breaking per-run isolation and risking cross-run timestamp collisions  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/QxpsCallerBusiness.java:150-157`
- **Area:** pool
- **Scenario:** The per-step modify XML is named String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, HHmmssSSS) = 'Modify_<time>.xml' with NO R_<id>/ prefix, uploaded directly via qxpsHttpClient.execute(modifyFileName, new AddFileMessage(...)) and referenced by ModifyMessage(modifyFileName). All runs share the pool root for these files; two concurrent runs (or rapid steps) producing the same HHmmssSSS timestamp overwrite each other's modify file, applying the wrong modifications.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsCallerBusiness.java:150-151 `String modifyFileName = String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, LocalDateTime.now().format(TIMESTAMP_FORMAT));` then line 154 `qxpsHttpClient.execute(modifyFileName, new AddFileMessage(modifyXml));` and line 157 `messages.add(new ModifyMessage(modifyFileName));` — no R_<id>/ prefix. MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN="Modify_%s.xml" (line 40), TIMESTAMP_FORMAT="HHmmssSSS" (line 43, no date).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXPS_Caller.cs:142-147; QXPS_File_Manager.cs:92-94` — QXPS_Caller.cs:142 `string __modify_Name = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(string.Format(ModifyNamePattern, DateTime.Now));` :143 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(__modify_Name, ...);` :147 `__messageModifier.Modify_File = __modify_Name;`. QXPS_File_Manager.cs:92-94 `public static string GetPoolPath(string documentName){ return string.Concat(_currentPoolPath, documentName); }` (doc line 88: "_currentPoolPath ex: R_1451\").
- **Fix:** In QxpsCallerBusiness.executeDirectCall, scope the modify file name to the run's pool directory (run is already a parameter). Replace lines 150-151:

    String modifyFileName = run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(
            String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, LocalDateTime.now().format(TIMESTAMP_FORMAT)));

This single change makes both the qxpsHttpClient.execute(modifyFileName, new AddFileMessage(...)) upload (line 154) and the new ModifyMessage(modifyFileName) reference (line 157) use R_<runId>/Modify_<time>.xml, restoring .NET parity (GetPoolPath wrapping) and per-run isolation. No method-signature changes are required. Optionally, to match .NET's HHmmssff exactly, the timestamp pattern could be narrowed, but that is cosmetic; the load-bearing fix is the R_<id>/ prefix.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple. .NET wraps the modify filename with GetPoolPath, which concatenates _currentPoolPath (R_<id>\) so the modify XML is uploaded and referenced as R_<id>/Modify_<time>.xml, isolated per run. Java drops this prefix, writing to the shared pool root; with a date-less HHmmssSSS timestamp, two concurrent runs or rapid same-millisecond steps overwrite each other's modify file, applying wrong structural modifications. The fix `run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, ...))` compiles (run is param at line 129; getPoolPath(String) exists at RunProperties.java:71-75 returning "R_%d/%s") and makes both the AddFileMessage upload (line 154) and ModifyMessage reference (line 157) use R_<runId>/, exactly matching .NET. No signature change needed (finding correctly notes the reporter's earlier claim was wrong). HIGH: output corruption but gated on probabilistic timestamp collision, not CRITICAL.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Verified independently. .NET QXPS_Caller.cs:142 builds the modify name via QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(...), which (cs:92-94) concatenates _currentPoolPath (R_<id>/, doc line 88) before Addfile (143) and Modify_File ref (147), isolating per run. Java QxpsCallerBusiness.java:150-151 uses bare String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, ...) = "Modify_<time>.xml" with no R_<id>/ for both the AddFileMessage upload (154) and ModifyMessage ref (157). Genuine divergence. Fix using run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(...) is correct: RunProperties.java:71-75 returns "R_%d/%s"; run is already a param (129), one variable feeds both sites, so it compiles and restores parity. Timestamp ff vs SSS is cosmetic.

### [#28] Absolute_Repeat_Type modeled as a fixed enum instead of a [Flags] bit-field — combined values crash and break first/other/last-page logic  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `enums/AbsoluteRepeatType.java:9-67 (fromValue at 60-67; consumers at DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java 699-726, TemplateMapper.java 37)`
- **Area:** enums-modes
- **Scenario:** A dynamic-task template row whose ABSOLUTE_REPEAT column holds a combined flag value (3=First|Other, 5=First|Last, 6=Other|Last, 7=all). TemplateMapper.fromValue(absoluteRepeatVal) is called during template load.
- **Java is (verified):** AbsoluteRepeatType.java:60-66 — `public static AbsoluteRepeatType fromValue(int value){ for (AbsoluteRepeatType type : values()){ if (type.value == value) return type; } throw new IllegalArgumentException("Unknown AbsoluteRepeatType value: " + value); }`. TemplateMapper.java:37 — `template.setAbsoluteRepeat(AbsoluteRepeatType.fromValue(absoluteRepeatVal));` (unconditional). Template.java:36 — `private AbsoluteRepeatType absoluteRepeat = AbsoluteRepeatType.DEFAULT;` (single-enum field). Consumer DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:704 `&& cell.getTemplate().getAbsoluteRepeat() != AbsoluteRepeatType.DEFAULT` and :726 `|| cell.getTemplate().getAbsoluteRepeat() == AbsoluteRepeatType.DEFAULT`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Enums/Absolute_Repeat_Type.cs:10-33; QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Template.cs:185-189; Process_Dynamique_Complete_Analysis.md:616-649` — Absolute_Repeat_Type.cs:10-32 `[Flags] public enum Absolute_Repeat_Type { Default=0x00, First_Page=0x01, Other_Page=0x02, Last_Page=0x04, All=0xFF }`. Proxy_Template.cs:185-188 `int __absolute_Repeat_val = __odr[COL_TEMPLATE_ABSOLUTE_REPEAT]; if(QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(...)) __template.Absolute_Repeat = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToEnum<Absolute_Repeat_Type>(__absolute_Repeat_val);`. Analysis md:616 `(__cell.Template.Absolute_Repeat & Absolute_Repeat_Type.Other_Page) == Absolute_Repeat_Type.Other_Page`; :624 `& First_Page == First_Page`; :626 `== Absolute_Repeat_Type.Default`; :635 `& Last_Page == Last_Page`.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Commit to the int bit-field representation (the enum approach cannot return a combined instance such as 3/5/6, so fromValue can never be made both non-throwing AND parity-faithful while keeping AbsoluteRepeatType as the field type). Concrete patch: (1) Template.java:36 — change field to `private int absoluteRepeat = 0;` (0 == .NET Default); update getter/setter to int. (2) TemplateMapper.java:37 — `template.setAbsoluteRepeat(absoluteRepeatVal);` (store raw int directly; never throws — matches ToEnum/Enum cast on a [Flags] enum which does not validate bit combinations). (3) DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy: replace `getAbsoluteRepeat().hasOtherPage()` with `(ar & 0x02)==0x02`, `hasFirstPage()` with `(ar & 0x01)==0x01`, `hasLastPage()` with `(ar & 0x04)==0x04`, and the two DEFAULT identity checks (:704 `!= DEFAULT`, :726 `== DEFAULT`) with `ar != 0` / `ar == 0` respectively (where `int ar = cell.getTemplate().getAbsoluteRepeat()`). Optionally keep AbsoluteRepeatType only as named int constants. This exactly reproduces .NET lines 616/624/626/635/647/649 for values 0..7 and 0xFF. Add a test covering fromValue/store of 0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,255 asserting no throw and correct first/other/last/default classification. (Alternatively, keep AbsoluteRepeatType but add a parallel int holder; the int-field route above is the lowest-risk minimal-churn patch given only 2 files consume it.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. .NET [Flags] enum loaded via ToEnum<Absolute_Repeat_Type>(int) does not throw on bit combinations and is consumed bitwise; combined values 3/5/6/7 are legal by design. Java fromValue throws IllegalArgumentException for any non-singleton value (called unconditionally in TemplateMapper), so a template row with a combined ABSOLUTE_REPEAT crashes template load — a real divergence and hard crash. Even absent the throw, the single-enum field cannot hold a combo, so one cell can never report both hasFirstPage and hasOtherPage. The finding's fix direction is right but offered as ambiguous alternatives and does not commit to a compilable patch; critically, a Java enum cannot return a combined instance, so 'keep AbsoluteRepeatType but make fromValue non-throwing' is unworkable for the field type — the field must become an int (or use an int holder). Adjusted fix pins the int bit-field route and enumerates all 5 consumer edits (2 files). HIGH stands: failure mode is a crash on load. Note: actual Process_Dynamique.cs body absent from sources; bitwise consumption confirmed via the project's analysis markdown, consistent with the faithful Java consumer.
- **Skeptic:** agreed — Verified independently. Java: AbsoluteRepeatType.fromValue (lines 60-66) throws IllegalArgumentException for any non-singleton int; called unconditionally at TemplateMapper.java:37, so combined ABSOLUTE_REPEAT values (3/5/6/7) crash template load. Template.java:36 holds a single enum, unable to represent combined flags. .NET differs: Absolute_Repeat_Type.cs is [Flags] (Default=0,First=1,Other=2,Last=4,All=0xFF); Proxy_Template.cs:185-188 stores via ToEnum<int>, and the Process_Dynamique routine (analysis md 616-651) consumes bitwise (& Other/First/Last) plus ==Default identity checks. Combined values are by design. Real divergence + crash. The enum genuinely cannot be made both non-throwing and parity-faithful, so the reconciler's int-bit-field patch is the correct sharpening. Only 2 consumer files use it (grep confirmed); no tests reference it. Patch compiles, matches .NET for 0..7/0xFF, breaks nothing.

## MEDIUM

### [#29] Degrade size check derives only from the gabarit; the gabarit_template size never triggers degrade  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:159`
- **Area:** pipeline (ProcessRunServiceImpl / Run lifecycle)
- **Scenario:** If the gabarit itself is under the limit but the gabarit_template (loaded later in loadTemplatesBusiness) exceeds it, the run should enter degrade mode. Java never re-evaluates degrade after loading the template, so it proceeds with full Prepare/Process/Steps/Check on an over-sized template document, which is the exact case degrade mode exists to prevent.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:159 `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { ... this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); }` — modeDegrade is set ONLY from the gabarit. LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:43-54 only calls getGabaritTemplateDao.getTemplates(idGabaritTemplate) and never loads the gabarit_template document nor evaluates its size. GetGabaritTemplateDaoImpl.java:68 `public DocumentDomain getGabaritTemplate(int idGabaritTemplate)` exists but grep shows zero callers. Run.java has no gabaritTemplate document field. ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:69 `if (!run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade())` and :114 gate the heavy steps on the single flag.
- **.NET is (verified):** `Run_Base.cs:915-926; Document.cs:228-239; Run.cs:147-159; Run_Base.cs:113` — Run_Base.cs:919-924 `return ((QXPFrk.Validation.IsNotNull(this.Gabarit) && this.Gabarit.Mode_Degrade) || (QXPFrk.Validation.IsNotNull(this.Gabarit_Template) && this.Gabarit_Template.Mode_Degrade));` — computed OR over BOTH documents. Document.cs:231-233 `if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(this.Data)) { return (_data.Length > SizeLimitBeforeFailSoft); }` — per-document. Run.cs:152 `this.Gabarit_Template = __proxy.Get_Gabarit_Template(this);` loads the template QXP in LoadTemplates (only when a dynamic task is present, Run.cs:137-144). Run_Base.cs:113 `if (!this.Mode_Degrade)` is evaluated AFTER Launch_Load() (which runs LoadTemplates), gating Prepare/Process/Steps/Check.
- **Fix:** The report's proposed fix is directionally right but incomplete: the Java code does not merely fail to "re-evaluate" after loading the template — it never loads the gabarit_template document. Implement the full path:

1. In LoadTemplatesBusiness.execute (or a sibling step invoked from ProcessRunServiceImpl.load after loadTasks), when idGabaritTemplate != Integer.MIN_VALUE and a dynamic task is present, load the gabarit_template document via the already-existing getGabaritTemplateDao.getGabaritTemplate(idGabaritTemplate). Store it on the Run (add a gabaritTemplate DocumentDomain field, mirroring .NET Run.Gabarit_Template).
2. Apply the same per-document degrade test used elsewhere: if gabaritTemplate.getData() != null && gabaritTemplate.getData().length > run.getSizeLimitBeforeFailSoft(), set run.getRunProperties().setModeDegrade(true). This makes modeDegrade an OR over both documents, matching Run_Base.cs:915-926.
3. Ensure this evaluation happens before ProcessRunServiceImpl's degrade gates (lines 69/114) consume isModeDegrade() — i.e. complete it within load() before run() branches on the flag. If introducing the field is too invasive short-term, at minimum load the template document, check its length, and OR it into setModeDegrade within the load() sequence prior to the process steps.

(For tighter parity, mirror .NET's Evaluate_Mode_Degrade guard which only degrades QXP-type documents with non-empty data; the existing LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.markModeDegradeIfTooBig pattern is a good template.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java sets modeDegrade only from the gabarit (Run.java:159); the gabarit_template document is never loaded (LoadTemplatesBusiness only calls getTemplates), so the template side of the .NET OR (Run_Base.cs:919-924) is entirely missing. The DAO method getGabaritTemplate exists with no callers. The bad path is reachable: an oversized template alone leaves modeDegrade=false and full Prepare/Process/Steps/Check run, defeating the safe-mode guard. The proposed fix correctly reproduces .NET: load the gabarit_template via getGabaritTemplate when a dynamic task is present, store it on Run, and OR its size (QXP + non-empty data > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft, mirroring Document.Evaluate_Mode_Degrade) into modeDegrade inside load() before the gates at lines 69/114. The fix is narrative/offers fallbacks but its prescribed end state yields true parity. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: not a crash/data-loss, but defeats the OOM/timeout safeguard for the template case.

### [#30] NullPointerException if the fetched gabarit has null data  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:159`
- **Area:** pipeline (ProcessRunServiceImpl / Run lifecycle)
- **Scenario:** prepareGabarit guards 'if (this.gabarit == null) return;' but then dereferences this.gabarit.getData().length. If GetGabaritBusiness returns a DocumentDomain whose data is null (e.g. an empty/missing pool fetch), this throws NPE, which the pipeline catches and turns into a Bloquante ERROR — instead of the .NET behavior which guards Data with Validation.IsSet before measuring length.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:154-159: "if (this.gabarit == null) { return; }" then "if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) {". getData() is a plain Lombok @Getter over byte[] data (DocumentDomain.java:42) with no null sentinel, so a gabarit with data==null throws NPE at line 159.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:228-239` — private bool Evaluate_Mode_Degrade() { if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(this.Data)) { return (_data.Length > SizeLimitBeforeFailSoft); } else { return false; } }
- **Fix:** In Run.prepareGabarit, replace the unguarded check at line 159 with a guard that mirrors .NET Evaluate_Mode_Degrade — only treat as degrade-mode candidate for QXP documents with non-null/non-empty data:

byte[] gabaritData = this.gabarit.getData();
if ("QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(this.gabarit.getFormat())
        && gabaritData != null && gabaritData.length > 0
        && gabaritData.length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) {
    log.warn("Gabarit size {} bytes exceeds limit {} bytes, setting Mode_Degrade for runId: {}",
            gabaritData.length, sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft, this.id);
    this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true);
    return;
}

Note that subsequent steps (filePoolPort.addFile(this.gabarit.getFileName(), this.gabarit.getData()) at line 167) also pass possibly-null data downstream; if the file pool / QXPS calls cannot tolerate null bytes, consider whether a null-data gabarit should be handled explicitly earlier, but at minimum the size check must not NPE. The QXP-type restriction keeps parity with .NET, which only evaluates degrade mode for QXP templates.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified the full triple. Java line 159 unconditionally dereferences getData().length after only the null-object guard at line 154, so a DocumentDomain with data==null (SQL NULL contenu via ResultSet.getBytes) throws NPE. .NET Evaluate_Mode_Degrade (Document.cs:231) guards Type==QXP && Validation.IsSet(Data) before reading _data.Length and returns false otherwise, so a null/empty-data gabarit proceeds non-degraded without throwing. The proposed fix restricts to "QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(getFormat()) && data!=null && length>0 before the size comparison — this exactly mirrors .NET (DocumentDomain has no Type enum; format string is the .NET Document_Type analogue, and the repo's own getRatioSizeBox at DocumentDomain.java:249 already uses this identical idiom for IsSet(this.Data)). All getters (getFormat/getData) exist; comparison long vs int compiles; the false/fall-through path (filePoolPort.addFile + DID at line 167) matches .NET Mode_Degrade=false normal processing. MEDIUM correct: edge-case NULL-content DB state, blocks the run when hit.

### [#31] Modify XML uploaded/referenced without the run pool-path prefix (R_runId/)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/QxpsCallerBusiness.java:150-157 (executeDirectCall)`
- **Area:** qxps-caller (QxpsCallerBusiness / QxpsCallerServiceImpl / GetGabaritXmlBusiness)
- **Scenario:** Any directCall=true step that has structural modifications (modifier non-empty): a PAGINATION or MODIFY step.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsCallerBusiness.java:150-157: String modifyFileName = String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, LocalDateTime.now().format(TIMESTAMP_FORMAT)); qxpsHttpClient.execute(modifyFileName, new AddFileMessage(modifyXml)); messages.add(new ModifyMessage(modifyFileName)); — both upload and reference use the bare name with NO pool prefix. Contrast line 257 where the gabarit IS prefixed: run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(newGabaritName).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXPS_Caller.cs:142-147; QXPS_File_Manager.cs:92-94` — QXPS_Caller.cs:142 string __modify_Name = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(string.Format(ModifyNamePattern, DateTime.Now)); :143 QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(__modify_Name, ...); :147 __messageModifier.Modify_File = __modify_Name; | QXPS_File_Manager.cs:94 return string.Concat(_currentPoolPath, documentName); (_currentPoolPath = "R_runId/")
- **Fix:** In executeDirectCall, prefix the modify file name with the run pool path before BOTH the upload and the reference, mirroring .NET GetPoolPath. Reuse the existing RunProperties.getPoolPath already used at line 257:

    String modifyFileName = String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN,
            LocalDateTime.now().format(TIMESTAMP_FORMAT));
    String modifyPoolName = run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(modifyFileName); // -> "R_runId/Modify_xxx.xml"

    qxpsHttpClient.execute(modifyPoolName, new AddFileMessage(modifyXml));
    messages.add(new ModifyMessage(modifyPoolName));

This restores per-run isolation (no cross-run collision), matches the .NET pool layout, and lets the run-folder purge clean it up. Note getPoolPath returns the bare name unchanged if runId is null (RunProperties.java:72-73), preserving current behavior when no runId is set.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java (lines 150-157) uses the bare modifyFileName for both AddFile upload and ModifyMessage reference, while .NET wraps it in GetPoolPath -> "R_runId/Modify_xxx.xml" for both Addfile and Modify_File. The fix calls run.getRunProperties().getPoolPath(modifyFileName) for both — and run is in scope (executeDirectCall signature line 129). RunProperties.getPoolPath (lines 71-76) returns "R_runId/name" or the bare name when runId is null, mirroring .NET's empty _currentPoolPath at root, preserving current behavior in that case. The fix matches .NET exactly. Note: an unrelated pre-existing format diff (.NET HHmmssff vs Java HHmmssSSS) is outside this finding's scope. Within a run upload+reference share the path so no in-run break; the real impact is lost per-run isolation -> concurrent-run collision and escape from run-folder purge. MEDIUM is correct.

### [#32] Null textValue renders the literal string "null" instead of empty  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/message/ParamsValueMessage.java:40 (getCommandQuery)`
- **Area:** qxps-messages (infra/interop/qxps/message + domain/modifier QxpsModifier/ModifierSpread)
- **Scenario:** A NameValueParam with a null textValue is added to a step; the combined URL produces `paramName=null` (Java string concatenation of a null reference yields "null").
- **Java is (verified):** ParamsValueMessage.java:40 `args.add(nv.getParamName() + "=" + nv.getTextValue());` — Java string concatenation of a null reference yields the literal token "null", producing `paramName=null`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Interop/QuarkServer/BusinessObject/QXPS_SDK/QXPS/Message/QXPS_Message_ParamsValue.cs:67` — `__results.Add(string.Format(NameValuePattern, __nameValue.paramName, __nameValue.textValue));` with `const string NameValuePattern = "{0}={1}";` (line 24). .NET string.Format renders a null argument as String.Empty, yielding `paramName=`.
- **Fix:** In ParamsValueMessage.getCommandQuery(), coalesce a null textValue to empty string to match .NET string.Format semantics: replace line 40 with `String value = nv.getTextValue() != null ? nv.getTextValue() : ""; args.add(nv.getParamName() + "=" + value);`. (Optionally also coalesce paramName, though paramName is consistently set from bloc.getName() and is not the reported concern.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java line 40 concatenates getTextValue() directly; a null textValue becomes the literal "null", whereas .NET string.Format (line 67, pattern "{0}={1}") emits empty string for a null arg, producing `paramName=`. The proposed fix coalesces textValue to "" before concatenation, which exactly reproduces .NET semantics, compiles cleanly, and addresses the reported scenario. paramName is consistently non-null (sourced from bloc name), so leaving it un-coalesced does not diverge in practice; coalescing only textValue is sufficient and correct. Impact is silent data corruption (literal "null" pushed into a QuarkXPress box) but conditional on a genuinely null value, so MEDIUM is appropriate.

### [#33] maxRetries always forced (3) — .NET leaves it at the SDK default at runtime  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxpsm/QxpsmSoapClient.java:93`
- **Area:** qxpsm-soap
- **Scenario:** Every SOAP processRequest call.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsmSoapClient.java:93 `context.setMaxRetries(qxpsmProperties.getMaxRetries());` (unconditional). QxpsmProperties.java:21 `private int maxRetries = 3;`. application.yaml:66 `max-retries: 3`. Generated QRequestContext.java:17 `private int maxRetries;` (defaults to 0 if never set).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXPSM_Call.cs:79-80; QXPS_Call_Info.cs:29; Validation.cs:67-77; Run_Base.cs:953-954` — QXPSM_Call.cs:79-80 `if(QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(_call_Info.MaxRetries, false)) _sdk_context.maxRetries = _call_Info.MaxRetries;`. Validation.cs:75 (IsSet int,false branch) `return !(val == int.MinValue || val == 0);`. QXPS_Call_Info.cs:29 `int _maxRetries;` (no initializer => 0, "pas utiliser dans notre cas"). Run_Base.cs:953-954 constructs QXPS_Call_Info and sets only DocumentName, never MaxRetries.
- **Fix:** Make the Java wire value match .NET. Simplest parity-faithful option: change the default in QxpsmProperties.java:21 and application.yaml to 0 (so the field is left at the SDK default, exactly like .NET). To preserve the option of configuring retries while still matching .NET when unset, also guard the call in QxpsmSoapClient.java:93: `if (qxpsmProperties.getMaxRetries() > 0) context.setMaxRetries(qxpsmProperties.getMaxRetries());` — this mirrors IsSet(MaxRetries, false) (treat 0 as "not set", leave context default). Do both: default to 0 AND guard with >0, so out-of-the-box behavior is identical to .NET (maxRetries=0) and an operator can still opt into retries via config if ever needed.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. .NET: MaxRetries stays 0 at runtime (only DocumentName is set in Run_Base.cs:953-954), IsSet(0,false)==false (Validation.cs:75), so _sdk_context.maxRetries is never assigned and keeps the proxy default of 0 — wire value 0. Java unconditionally writes 3 (QxpsmSoapClient.java:93, default QxpsmProperties.java:21 + application.yaml:66). Genuine divergence: Java retries failing requests up to 3 times where .NET uses the SDK default of 0. The proposed fix (default max-retries to 0 AND guard with `if (getMaxRetries() > 0) context.setMaxRetries(...)`) faithfully mirrors IsSet(MaxRetries,false): with default 0 the guard skips the call, leaving QRequestContext.maxRetries=0 (Java primitive int default), identical to .NET. >0 guard treats 0/negative as unset, matching .NET intent. MEDIUM correct — behavior/latency divergence, not output correctness.

### [#34] CURRENCY value formatting omits the currency symbol  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:37-40, 60-81`
- **Area:** system-task
- **Scenario:** VALUE mode with dataType=CURRENCY.
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:37-40 `case DECIMAL: case CURRENCY: case POURCENTAGE: return formatDecimal(raw, nbDecimal, showZero, nullString, decimalSignificative);` and formatDecimal:71-77 builds pattern `"#,##0"` (+ optional `.0...`) via `DecimalFormat(pattern, new DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE))` and returns `df.format(val)` with no symbol/suffix appended.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:33, 102-119, 363-364, 466-479; PercentPattern:34, 490-505; culture: Run_Base.cs:751` — Data_Type_Helper.cs:33 `const string CurrencyPattern = "#,##0.{0} {1}";` :364 `case Data_Type.Currency: return GetStringCurrency(...);` :471-472 `string __pattern = GetCurrencyPattern(nbDecimal, decimal_significative); string __str = __currency.ToString(__pattern);` :108 `string.Format(CurrencyPattern, _decimalFixedPattern[nbDecimal], NumberFormatInfo.CurrentInfo.CurrencySymbol)`. Run_Base.cs:751 `CultureInfo.CreateSpecificCulture("FR")` (CurrencySymbol left as default = euro sign).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Split the collapsed case. Keep DECIMAL -> formatDecimal as-is. Add CURRENCY branch matching .NET pattern "#,##0.{0} {1}" where {1}=NumberFormatInfo.CurrentInfo.CurrencySymbol; since Run_Base.InitThreadCulture sets CreateSpecificCulture("FR") and only overrides decimal/group separators (CurrencySymbol stays the FR default = euro sign), source the symbol from new DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE).getCurrencySymbol(). Concatenate with a single space exactly like the format literal: case CURRENCY -> { String n = formatDecimal(raw,nbDecimal,showZero,nullString,decimalSignificative); return n.equals(nullString) ? nullString : n + " " + new DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE).getCurrencySymbol(); } -- this part of the proposal is correct. The POURCENTAGE part of the proposed fix (n + " %") is WRONG: .NET's PercentPattern "#,##0.{0} %" uses the .NET `%` custom-format specifier, which MULTIPLIES the value by 100 and emits a localized percent sign, and GetStringPourcentage THEN appends an additional literal " %" (line 497), so .NET emits e.g. for 0.5 "50,00 % %". To match exactly: case POURCENTAGE -> { String n = formatDecimal(raw,nbDecimal,showZero,nullString,decimalSignificative); if (n.equals(nullString)) return nullString; BigDecimal v = new BigDecimal(raw).multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100)); String scaled = formatDecimal(v.toPlainString(),nbDecimal,showZero,nullString,decimalSignificative); return scaled + " % %"; } (the percent symbol from the FR culture is "%"). If exact reproduction of the .NET double-percent quirk is not desired, the POURCENTAGE branch should be excluded from this finding's scope and tracked separately rather than shipped as "n + \" %\"", which matches neither .NET's *100 nor its double suffix.
- **Reconciler note:** Bug is real: Java routes CURRENCY (and POURCENTAGE) through the same DECIMAL branch and never appends the currency symbol / percent suffix, whereas .NET routes Currency to GetStringCurrency using CurrencyPattern "#,##0.{0} {1}" with CurrencySymbol. CURRENCY portion of the proposed fix is correct and the euro-sign source (FR culture) matches Run_Base.InitThreadCulture. But the POURCENTAGE portion (n + " %") does not match .NET, which applies the `%` format specifier (x100 + percent sign) and then appends an extra " %", yielding a "% %" double-suffix on the x100 value. Hence FIX_ADJUSTED. Severity MEDIUM confirmed: display-only suffix gap; numeric value and grouping already correct; symbol is culture-driven, not per-row data.

### [#35] POURCENTAGE value formatting omits the ' %' suffix  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:37-40, 60-81`
- **Area:** system-task
- **Scenario:** VALUE mode with dataType=POURCENTAGE.
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:37-40 `case DECIMAL: case CURRENCY: case POURCENTAGE: return formatDecimal(raw, nbDecimal, showZero, nullString, decimalSignificative);` — formatDecimal (lines 60-81) builds pattern `"#,##0"` + optional `.0...` and returns `df.format(val)`, no `%`, no x100 scaling.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:369-370, 490-505, 34, 127-144` — Line 369-370: `case Data_Type.Pourcentage: return GetStringPourcentage(...)`. Line 34: `const string PercentPattern = "#,##0.{0} %";`. Lines 495-498: `string __pattern = GetPercentPattern(nbDecimal, decimal_significative); // TODO à voir si c'est correct  string __str = __pourcentage.ToString(__pattern) + " %"; return __str;`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The bug is real (POURCENTAGE is folded into the plain DECIMAL branch, so the percent marker AND .NET's x100 scaling are lost). For true parity, replicate .NET's GetStringPourcentage exactly, including its quirks (x100 via the '%' pattern specifier and the literal '+ \" %\"' double-append). Add a dedicated branch and method:

case POURCENTAGE:
    return formatPourcentage(raw, nbDecimal, showZero, nullString, decimalSignificative);

private static String formatPourcentage(String raw, int nbDecimal, boolean showZero, String nullString, boolean decimalSignificative) {
    try {
        BigDecimal val = new BigDecimal(raw);
        if (val.compareTo(BigDecimal.ZERO) == 0 && !showZero) return nullString;
        DecimalFormatSymbols symbols = new DecimalFormatSymbols(Locale.FRANCE);
        StringBuilder pattern = new StringBuilder("#,##0");
        if (nbDecimal > 0) { pattern.append('.'); for (int i = 0; i < nbDecimal; i++) pattern.append(decimalSignificative ? '0' : '#'); }
        pattern.append(" %"); // Java DecimalFormat '%' multiplies by 100 and inserts the localized percent symbol, matching .NET's '%' specifier
        DecimalFormat df = new DecimalFormat(pattern.toString(), symbols);
        return df.format(val) + " %"; // matches .NET literal '+ \" %\"' (yes, this double-appends, exactly like .NET)
    } catch (NumberFormatException e) { return raw; }
}

NOTE: the finding's proposed fix is NOT a .NET-parity fix — it (a) does not multiply by 100, (b) appends only a single \" %\", and (c) reuses formatDecimal which ignores the decimalSignificative fixed/non-fixed distinction that GetPercentPattern honors. Applying the finding's fix yields e.g. \"0,50 %\" where .NET yields \"50,00 % %\" for input 0.5/nbDecimal=2. If business confirms .NET's double-% / x100 is an unintended bug (per the TODO comment), that is a deliberate divergence from .NET and must be flagged as such, not silently shipped under the banner of 'parity'. As a strict port, the adjustedFix above reproduces .NET byte-for-byte.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java DataTypeHelper.java:37-40 folds POURCENTAGE into the plain decimal branch (no %, no x100). .NET (Data_Type_Helper.cs:369-370 -> 490-505) routes to GetStringPourcentage which uses PercentPattern "#,##0.{0} %" (line 34) where .NET's '%' specifier multiplies by 100 and inserts the localized percent symbol, THEN literally appends '+ \" %\"' (line 497, with a TODO comment). So .NET output for 0.5 = \"50,00 % %\"; current Java = \"0,50\". The finding's fix only appends one \" %\" and skips x100, so it does NOT match .NET -> fixCorrect=false. Provided adjustedFix replicates .NET exactly (x100 via DecimalFormat '%' + literal \" %\", honoring decimalSignificative). Path is reachable from ProcessSqlBusiness/SystemTaskProcessStrategy. MEDIUM confirmed: cosmetic box-text formatting divergence, not data corruption.

### [#36] decimal_significative=false branch still shows trailing zeros; rounding mode differs  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:66-77`
- **Area:** system-task
- **Scenario:** VALUE mode DECIMAL/CURRENCY/POURCENTAGE with decimalSignificative=false (e.g. nbDecimal=2, value 1.5 -> expected '1,5').
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:66-75: `if (decimalSignificative) { val = val.setScale(nbDecimal, RoundingMode.HALF_UP); } ... StringBuilder pattern = new StringBuilder("#,##0"); if (nbDecimal > 0) { pattern.append("."); for (int i = 0; i < nbDecimal; i++) pattern.append("0"); }` — pattern uses '0' digits unconditionally, ignoring decimalSignificative.
