- **.NET is (verified):** `Data_Type_Helper.cs:54-55, 81-88, 447-448` — L54-55: `_decimalFixedPattern[__i] = new string('0', __i); _decimalNotFixedPattern[__i] = new string('#', __i);` L81-88: `if(fixedPattern){ return string.Format(DecimalPattern, _decimalFixedPattern[nbDecimal]); } else { return string.Format(DecimalPattern, _decimalNotFixedPattern[nbDecimal]); }` L447-448: `string __pattern = GetDecimalPattern(nbDecimal, decimal_significative); string __str = __decimal.ToString(__pattern);` (DecimalPattern = "#,##0.{0}", L32)
- **Fix:** Fix only the pattern, leave rounding as HALF_EVEN to match .NET Framework Decimal.ToString. In formatDecimal, when decimalSignificative is false build a '#'*nbDecimal fractional pattern (optional digits, suppresses trailing zeros) and do NOT setScale; when true keep the '0'*nbDecimal fixed pattern. Concretely:

  StringBuilder pattern = new StringBuilder("#,##0");
  if (nbDecimal > 0) {
      pattern.append('.');
      char digit = decimalSignificative ? '0' : '#';
      for (int i = 0; i < nbDecimal; i++) pattern.append(digit);
  }
  DecimalFormat df = new DecimalFormat(pattern.toString(), symbols);
  // leave df at default HALF_EVEN rounding to match .NET Decimal.ToString (banker's rounding)
  return df.format(val);

Drop the setScale(nbDecimal, HALF_UP) call entirely (DecimalFormat already applies the scale via the pattern, and HALF_EVEN matches .NET). Do NOT switch rounding to HALF_UP as the original proposal suggested. Apply the same '#' vs '0' pattern logic to CURRENCY and POURCENTAGE if/when those gain distinct formatting, since .NET drives all three off the same fixedPattern flag.
- **Reconciler note:** Confirmed real parity bug. .NET passes decimal_significative directly as fixedPattern (GetStringDecimal L447, Currency L471, Pourcentage L495). fixedPattern=true -> N '0' chars ("#,##0.00", trailing zeros forced); false -> N '#' chars ("#,##0.##", trailing zeros suppressed). Java always emits '0'*nbDecimal, so false+1.5+nbDecimal=2 yields "1,50" vs .NET "1,5". The false path is reachable (DB column DECIMAL_SIGNIFICATIVE -> TaskMapper.java:69 -> formatDecimal; default true but per-task configurable). The proposed fix correctly builds '#'*nbDecimal for false and '0'*nbDecimal for true, drops setScale, and relies on DecimalFormat default HALF_EVEN — which matches .NET Framework Decimal.ToString banker's rounding (ToEven). This also incidentally corrects the pre-existing true-branch HALF_UP mismatch. Fix compiles (reuses symbols/nbDecimal/val, returns df.format(val); zero-check and try/catch preserved). The verifyNote's rejection of the HALF_UP rounding sub-claim is correct. MEDIUM (cosmetic divergence, flag-dependent, no crash).

### [#37] Duplicate bloc in SQL result only logged, not recorded as a Run error  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/ProcessSqlBusiness.java:77-80`
- **Area:** sql-task
- **Scenario:** The SQL query returns two rows with the same NOM_BLOC value.
- **Java is (verified):** ProcessSqlBusiness.java:77-80: `if (task.getBlocsUpdate().containsKey(blocName)) {` / `    log.warn("Duplicate bloc '{}' in task {}", blocName, task.getDebugInfo());` / `    continue;` / `}` — only logs and skips; nothing is added to task.getRun().getErrors().
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_SQL.cs:119-122` — `if(task.Blocs_Update.ContainsKey(__bloc_Name))` / `{` / `    task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.ErrorDuplicateBlocInTask, task.Debug_Info, __bloc_Name);` / `}` — the duplicate branch records a Run error (then else-branch adds the bloc). Errors.cs:98-110 `Add(string,params)` -> Error.cs:56 delegates with Error_Type.Unspecified (Error_Type.cs:16 Unspecified=1).
- **Fix:** In ProcessSqlBusiness.addBlocs, replace the log-only duplicate branch (lines 77-80) so it also records the run error, mirroring QxpPreviousTaskProcessStrategy and .NET parity:

    if (task.getBlocsUpdate().containsKey(blocName)) {
        task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,
                "ErrorDuplicateBlocInTask: bloc [" + blocName + "] deja present dans la tache " + task.getDebugInfo()));
        log.warn("Duplicate bloc '{}' in task {}", blocName, task.getDebugInfo());
        continue;
    }

Use RunError.UNSPECIFIED (not CRITIQUE) to match the .NET message-string Errors.Add overload and the existing sibling port. Keep the log.warn for operability. (Optionally, for full parity, also record ErrorAddingBlocSQLInTask in the catch at lines 92-94, but that is outside this issue.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java (lines 77-80) only log.warn+continue; .NET (Process_SQL.cs:119-122) records task.Run.Errors via the message-string Add overload, which Error.cs:56 + Error_Type.cs:16 prove is type Unspecified=1. Java RunError.UNSPECIFIED=1 (RunError.java:22), constructor RunError(int,String) exists (line 30) so the fix compiles. Sibling port QxpPreviousTaskProcessStrategy.java:121-122 and 168-169 handles the same .NET message with RunError.UNSPECIFIED, confirming the convention and the chosen category. The fix uses blocName/getDebugInfo (in scope), keeps log.warn, keeps continue — making Java surface the run error exactly as .NET. Message text differs from .NET's exact format string but matches the existing sibling port; the load-bearing parity behavior (record an Unspecified run error) is correct. MEDIUM is right: dropped run error usable downstream to flag/fail a run, no crash/corruption. Catch-branch ErrorAddingBlocSQLInTask gap (.NET line 136 vs Java line 93) is real but correctly noted out-of-scope.

### [#38] Per-row exception is logged but not recorded as a Run error  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/ProcessSqlBusiness.java:92-94`
- **Area:** sql-task
- **Scenario:** Any exception thrown while building a single bloc (e.g. a malformed value), per row.
- **Java is (verified):** ProcessSqlBusiness.java:92-94: `} catch (Exception e) { log.error("Error adding bloc '{}' in task {}", blocName, task.getDebugInfo(), e); }` (no RunError added). Also lines 77-79: `if (task.getBlocsUpdate().containsKey(blocName)) { log.warn("Duplicate bloc '{}' in task {}", ...); continue; }` (no RunError added).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_SQL.cs:134-137 and 119-122` — Process_SQL.cs:134-137: `catch { task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.ErrorAddingBlocSQLInTask, task.Debug_Info, __bloc_Name); }`. Process_SQL.cs:119-122: `if(task.Blocs_Update.ContainsKey(__bloc_Name)) { task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.ErrorDuplicateBlocInTask, task.Debug_Info, __bloc_Name); }`. Both use Errors.Add(string,params) (Errors.cs:98-110) → new Error(message,args) (Error.cs:56) → defaults Error_Type.Unspecified=1 (Error_Type.cs:16).
- **Fix:** In ProcessSqlBusiness.addBlocs, record a RunError in the per-row catch with category UNSPECIFIED (matching .NET's Add(string) -> Error_Type.Unspecified), keeping the log:\n\n} catch (Exception e) {\n    log.error("Error adding bloc '{}' in task {}", blocName, task.getDebugInfo(), e);\n    task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,\n            "ErrorAddingBlocSQLInTask: tache " + task.getDebugInfo() + " bloc " + blocName));\n}\n\nFor full parity, also restore the duplicate-bloc run error (lines 77-79), which .NET records via ErrorDuplicateBlocInTask:\n\nif (task.getBlocsUpdate().containsKey(blocName)) {\n    log.warn("Duplicate bloc '{}' in task {}", blocName, task.getDebugInfo());\n    task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,\n            "ErrorDuplicateBlocInTask: tache " + task.getDebugInfo() + " bloc " + blocName));\n    continue;\n}\n\nUse RunError.UNSPECIFIED (1) — NOT CRITIQUE — to match the .NET Error_Type and the existing QxpPreviousTaskProcessStrategy precedent. Note: the class comment at lines 36-37 ("1=Bloquante ... 3=Warning") is itself inaccurate; the authoritative mapping is in RunError.java (UNSPECIFIED=1, CRITIQUE=2, BLOQUANTE=3).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java drops two RunErrors that .NET records: per-row catch (Java 92-94 vs .NET 134-137) and duplicate-bloc (Java 77-79 vs .NET 119-122). Both .NET calls go through Errors.Add(string,params) which defaults Error_Type.Unspecified=1, so RunError.UNSPECIFIED (1) is correct — the finding rightly overrides the report's CRITIQUE. RunError.java javadoc confirms category is persisted (Insert_Run_Errors), so the loss is observable, not cosmetic. The sibling port QxpPreviousTaskProcessStrategy.java:168-169,181-183 already uses new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,...) for the identical .NET messages, confirming fix shape. The fix's message text is paraphrased (not the exact .NET format "Error when adding bloc in task : {0} bloc : {1}") but matches the established Java convention; message text is not load-bearing, the category and presence are. Severity MEDIUM correct: parity/observability regression in persisted run report, no data corruption.

### [#39] PDF with no existing gabarit blocs returns silently; .NET records a run error  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/task/impl/DocumentTaskProcessStrategy.java:231-235`
- **Area:** document-task
- **Scenario:** FILE_PDF task whose destination prefix matches zero boxes in the gabarit XML (existingBoxNames empty) - e.g. wrong DestinationBlocName, or gabarit XML not loaded at process time (returns QxpXml.EMPTY -> empty list).
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentTaskProcessStrategy.java:231-235: "if (existingBoxNames.isEmpty()) { log.warn(\"PDF task [{}] no existing blocs found for prefix [{}], skipping\", task.getId(), task.getDestinationBlocName()); return; }" — only logs, adds nothing to task.getRun().getErrors(). Also lines 65-67: "if (task.getDestinationBlocName() == null || task.getDestinationBlocName().isBlank()) { log.warn(\"Missing destinationBlocName for document task [{}], skipping\", task.getId()); return; }"
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_Document.cs:196-200 (and line 52)` — Process_Document.cs:196-200: "if (__lstBoxName.Count == 0) { task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.MissingDestinationBlocNameInTask, task.DestinationBlocName, task.ID); return; }". Line 52: "task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.MissingBlocNameInTask, task.Debug_Info);"
- **Fix:** In processFilePdf, replace the silent return at lines 231-235 with a run-error before returning, mirroring .NET line 198. E.g.:

if (existingBoxNames.isEmpty()) {
    task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,
        "Aucun bloc de destination trouve pour le prefixe [" + task.getDestinationBlocName()
        + "] (tache " + task.getId() + ") - MissingDestinationBlocNameInTask"));
    log.warn("PDF task [{}] no existing blocs found for prefix [{}], skipping",
        task.getId(), task.getDestinationBlocName());
    return;
}

(Use whatever category constant matches the .NET Error_Messages.MissingDestinationBlocNameInTask severity; UNSPECIFIED=1 is the safe default mirroring how the file's other ported messages map.) Optionally apply the same treatment to the destinationBlocName-blank guard at lines 65-67 to fully restore parity with .NET line 52 (MissingBlocNameInTask), since that error is currently dropped too.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple. Java processFilePdf (231-235) only log.warn+return on empty box list; .NET adds MissingDestinationBlocNameInTask to Run.Errors before returning. Confirmed via Errors.Add(string,params) -> new Error(message,args) -> default Error_Type.Unspecified (Error.cs:56,19), so .NET records this as an Unspecified-severity run error. Java RunError(int,String) with UNSPECIFIED=1 (RunError.java:22,30) is the correct mapping, and the file already uses task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(...)) elsewhere (398,486,515), so the fix compiles and matches .NET semantics. Functional outcome (no blocs produced) already matches; only the missing error record is restored, so MEDIUM (observability) is correct. Note: for FULL parity the same UNSPECIFIED error should also be added at lines 65-67 (MissingBlocNameInTask, .NET line 52), which the finding correctly flags as a sibling divergence; the reported 231-235 fix itself is sound as written.

### [#40] storeData uses enum equality instead of bitwise flag test on a [Flags] enum  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/task/impl/DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:251-254`
- **Area:** dynamique-task
- **Scenario:** Run configured with a store_data_type bitmask that includes the SQL bit but is not exactly SQL(0x01) or ALL(0xFF) — e.g. SQL|DOCUMENT (0x03). Then storeData evaluates to false and Get_Report never records DataNameValue rows, so the SQL data is silently not stored.
- **Java is (verified):** DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:251-254: `boolean storeData = task.isStoreData() && (task.getRun().getRunProperties().getStoreDataType() == com.socgen.sgs.api.quark.engine.domain.StoreDataType.SQL || task.getRun().getRunProperties().getStoreDataType() == com.socgen.sgs.api.quark.engine.domain.StoreDataType.ALL);` — strict enum equality. Internal inconsistency confirmed: EndRunBusiness.java:138 `storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.SQL)`, ProcessSqlBusiness.java:63 `(...getStoreDataType().getCode() & StoreDataType.SQL.getCode()) != 0`, CheckServiceImpl.java:62 `storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.SQL)`. StoreDataType.java:36 fromCode returns NONE for unmodeled codes; RunPropertiesMapper.java:85-86 maps via fromCode and RunProperties.storeDataType is the enum (not raw int), so 0x03 collapses to NONE.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_Dynamique.cs:167 (Get_Report); Enums/Store_Data_Type.cs (whole file)` — Process_Dynamique.cs:167 `bool __store_Data = __task.Store_Data && ((__task.Run.Properties.Store_Type & Store_Data_Type.SQL) == Store_Data_Type.SQL);` — actual source, not just the analysis md. Store_Data_Type.cs: `[Flags] public enum Store_Data_Type { NONE=0x00, SQL=0x01, DOCUMENT=0x02, ALL=0xFF }`. Identical bitwise pattern also at Process_SQL.cs:92.
- **Fix:** Replace the equality check with the bitwise flag test to match .NET and the rest of the codebase: in DynamiqueTaskProcessStrategy.java:251-254 use `boolean storeData = task.isStoreData() && task.getRun().getRunProperties().getStoreDataType().hasFlag(StoreDataType.SQL);` (add the import for com.socgen.sgs.api.quark.engine.domain.StoreDataType rather than the fully-qualified inline reference). Additionally, to fully restore parity for combination bitmasks, harden StoreDataType.fromCode so it does not collapse unmodeled SQL-containing codes (e.g. 0x03) to NONE — either store the raw int code on RunProperties and test bits against it, or change fromCode to preserve the integer mask so hasFlag(SQL) returns true for any code with the SQL bit set. Without the fromCode fix, hasFlag(SQL) still returns false for a 0x03 mask because it is mapped to NONE.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified against actual source (not just the reverse-engineered markdown): real Process_Dynamique.cs:167 uses the bitwise [Flags] test; Java strategy uses strict enum equality, diverging from the rest of the codebase which correctly uses hasFlag/bitwise-AND. Genuine parity defect. The proposed fix is comprehensive and correct: it replaces the equality with hasFlag(StoreDataType.SQL) AND explicitly requires hardening StoreDataType.fromCode (which currently collapses combo codes like 0x03 to NONE via RunPropertiesMapper.java:85-86) so hasFlag works for combination bitmasks. The finding correctly notes the equality fix alone is insufficient for 0x03. Severity MEDIUM is right: the only modeled SQL-containing value besides exactly SQL is ALL=0xFF (already handled), and .NET's canonical store-all is ALL, so silent data loss requires an out-of-band combo mask actually persisted — real but limited reachability, silent (no exception).

### [#41] parseDocumentIdentity throws on identity strings with fewer than 6 parts; .NET silently leaves defaults  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/identity/DocumentIdentityService.java:150-154`
- **Area:** did-task
- **Scenario:** An older or malformed DID value (e.g. only 'code|suivi|run' or an empty string after a successful fetch) reaches parseDocumentIdentity. parts.length < MIN_IDENTITY_PARTS (6) triggers IllegalArgumentException, aborting prepareGabarit.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentIdentityService.java:140 `Objects.requireNonNull(identityString, "identityString must not be null");`; :142-143 `if (identityString.trim().isEmpty()) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Identity string must not be empty"); }`; :150-153 `if (parts.length < MIN_IDENTITY_PARTS) { throw new IllegalArgumentException(...) }`; :186-188 parseDateTime: `if (dateTimeString == null || dateTimeString.isEmpty()) { throw new IllegalArgumentException(...) }`. Caller Run.java:171-172 feeds getElementValueByIdName result (null when no DID box, per :122-123) straight into parseDocumentIdentity.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document_Identity.cs:64-89; ConversionInvariante.cs:164-167, 301-314; Document.cs:205-206` — Document_Identity.cs:64-65 `string[] __values = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToString(valeur_champs).Split('|'); if (__values.Length >= 6)` — no else, no throw; comment :82 "les anciens document ne possedent pas cette informations, il sont pourtant valide !". ConversionInvariante.cs:166 `return ToInt(value, int.MinValue);` and :301-312 `ToDateTime(string)` returns `DateTime.MinValue` on unset/unparseable — never throws. Document.cs:205-206 `string __str_identity = this.XML.GetValue(DID); _identity = new Document_Identity(__str_identity);` passes possibly-null/short DID without guarding.
- **Fix:** Make parseDocumentIdentity lenient to mirror .NET Document_Identity(string). Do not throw on null, empty, or fewer-than-6 parts: instead build a DocumentIdentity from whatever parts are present, using .NET-equivalent defaults for missing/blank fields (null/empty strings, and for the int/date fields the .NET MinValue equivalents — e.g. null LocalDateTime, since .NET maps blank ints/dates to int.MinValue/DateTime.MinValue rather than throwing). Concretely: (1) at line 140, treat null identityString as empty rather than NPE; (2) remove the empty-string throw at 142-144 and the length<6 throw at 150-154; (3) only read parts that exist (guard each index against parts.length), defaulting absent ones; (4) make parseDateTime return null (or a defined sentinel) instead of throwing on empty/unparseable, matching ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime returning DateTime.MinValue. To preserve the dropped Is_Defined semantics, add an isDefined() method on DocumentIdentity (all of idFndCode/idRun/idSuivi/idLangue/dueDate/generationDateTime set) so downstream code (Task_DID equivalent) can detect under-defined identities exactly as .NET did, rather than relying on a thrown exception. Keep these as parsing-leniency changes only; downstream gating should use isDefined().
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple. Java throws on null (NPE :140), empty (:142-143), <6 parts (:150-153), and empty/unparseable dates (:186-195); .NET Document_Identity ctor never throws — short/empty/null inputs leave fields at defaults (int.MinValue, DateTime.MinValue via ConversionInvariante) yielding an under-defined-but-valid identity. Both Java call paths (Run.PrepareGabarit and DocumentIdentityHelper.getIdentity) can pass null DID, turning a tolerated legacy/raw-template case into a hard abort. Fix is directionally correct and compiles: treat null as empty, drop the empty/length throws, guard indices, make parseDateTime return null (Java analog of DateTime.MinValue). The proposed isDefined() is optional — grep shows .NET Is_Defined is never consumed downstream, so it does not affect parity; including it is harmless. Note: Java stores idSuivi/idRun/idLangue as Strings (not ints), so absent parts become null rather than int.MinValue — acceptable for the no-throw parity goal. Severity MEDIUM stands: only the incomplete/legacy/empty-DID path is affected.

### [#42] Strict MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss date parsing throws where .NET ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime falls back to DateTime.MinValue  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/identity/DocumentIdentityService.java:185-197 (parseDateTime) called from 160-161`
- **Area:** did-task
- **Scenario:** A DID value whose Due_Date or Generation_DateTime is empty, malformed, or written with single-digit components (e.g. '12/1/2009 0:00:00') — which the .NET invariant DateTime.TryParse accepts — fails the rigid DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss") and parseDateTime throws IllegalArgumentException, aborting prepareGabarit.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentIdentityService.java:185-196: `private LocalDateTime parseDateTime(String dateTimeString, String fieldName) { if (dateTimeString == null || dateTimeString.isEmpty()) { throw new IllegalArgumentException(...); } try { return LocalDateTime.parse(dateTimeString, DATE_TIME_FORMATTER); } catch (DateTimeParseException e) { throw new IllegalArgumentException(...); } }` with line 45-46 `DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss")`. Called at lines 160-161 inside parseDocumentIdentity, reached from Run.java:172 prepareGabarit.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:301-314; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document_Identity.cs:65,78,80` — ConversionInvariante.cs:301-314: `public static DateTime ToDateTime(string value){ DateTime __defaultValue = DateTime.MinValue; DateTime __newvalue = DateTime.MinValue; if (Validation.IsSet(value) && DateTime.TryParse(value, InvariantCulture.DateTimeFormat, DateTimeStyles.None, out __newvalue)) return __newvalue; else return __defaultValue; }`. Document_Identity.cs:65 `if (__values.Length >= 6)`; :78 `_date_Echeance = QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime(__values[4]);` :80 `_date_Last_Execution = QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime(__values[5]);`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The fix's direction is correct (make parseDateTime non-throwing + lenient with a MinValue fallback), but the chosen sentinel is wrong for parity. .NET DateTime.MinValue is 0001-01-01T00:00:00, whereas Java LocalDateTime.MIN is -999999999-01-01T00:00; using LocalDateTime.MIN diverges from .NET if the value is ever formatted/compared. Use the exact equivalent. Concretely:
private static final LocalDateTime DATE_MIN = LocalDateTime.of(1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0); // == .NET DateTime.MinValue
private static final DateTimeFormatter DATE_TIME_FORMATTER = new DateTimeFormatterBuilder().appendPattern("M/d/uuuu H:mm:ss").toLocalDateTime?... -> build a lenient formatter: new DateTimeFormatterBuilder().appendPattern("M/d/uuuu H:mm:ss").toFormatter(java.util.Locale.ROOT);
private LocalDateTime parseDateTime(String dateTimeString, String fieldName) {
    if (dateTimeString == null || dateTimeString.isEmpty()) { return DATE_MIN; }
    try { return LocalDateTime.parse(dateTimeString, DATE_TIME_FORMATTER); }
    catch (DateTimeParseException e) { log.debug("Falling back to MinValue for {}: '{}'", fieldName, dateTimeString); return DATE_MIN; }
}
Apply the identical lenient single-digit pattern (M/d/uuuu H:mm:ss) so values like "12/1/2009 0:00:00" parse, matching DateTime.TryParse(InvariantCulture). DocumentIdentityHelper's twin formatter only formats output, so leave it zero-padded (no change). Note: the parts.length < 6 guard at parseDocumentIdentity lines 150-154 still throws where .NET (Document_Identity.cs:65) silently leaves defaults — that is a separate divergence not in scope of this finding's date fix, but worth flagging.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java parseDateTime (DocumentIdentityService.java:185-196) throws on empty input and on any value not matching strict "MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss"; this aborts prepareGabarit (Run.java:172). .NET ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime (cs:301-314) never throws: returns DateTime.MinValue on empty/unparseable and uses InvariantCulture which leniently accepts single-digit month/day/hour. The empty-Due_Date round-trip is real: DocumentIdentityHelper.getNewIdentity emits "" for Due_Date when dateEcheance is null (lines 50-51), then parseDateTime rejects it. Bug is genuine. Fix direction is right but sentinel LocalDateTime.MIN != .NET DateTime.MinValue (0001-01-01); use LocalDateTime.of(1,1,1,0,0,0) and a lenient builder pattern. Status FIX_ADJUSTED. MEDIUM correct: reachable, non-mainline, fatal-vs-graceful divergence.

### [#43] Unknown compartiment mode logs and returns instead of raising a task exception  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/task/impl/CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:104-107`
- **Area:** compartiment-task
- **Scenario:** props.getCompartimentMode()==UNKNOWN. Java logs an error and silently returns from process(), leaving the task as a no-op (todo unchanged, no blocs).
- **Java is (verified):** CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:104-107: `if (props.getCompartimentMode() == TaskCompartimentMode.UNKNOWN) {` / `    log.error("Unknown compartiment mode for task [{}]", task.getId());` / `    return;` / `}` — process() returns as a no-op; no RunError added, task.isInError() stays false.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_Compartiment.cs:62-65; Helper/Exception_Helper.cs:49-52; BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:421-424; BusinessObject/Run/Exception/Exception_Task.cs:60` — Process_Compartiment.cs:62-65: `if (task.Run.Properties.Compartiment_Mode == Task_Compartiment_Mode.Unknown) { Exception_Helper.Raise_Exception(new Exception_Task(Exception_Task.MSG_Load_Task_Compartiment, task.ID)); }`. Exception_Helper.cs:49-52: `public static void Raise_Exception(Exception_Base ex) { throw ex; }`. Run_Base.cs:421-424: `catch (Exception_Base exb) { __task.InError = true; this.Errors.Add(Error_Type.Critique, exb); }`. Exception_Task.cs:60: MSG_Load_Task_Compartiment = "Erreur sur la tache {0} - manque le mode de la tache compartiment sur le run".
- **Fix:** Make process() signal the failure rather than silently returning, so the existing ProcessTasksServiceImpl try/catch converts it to a CRITIQUE RunError + setInError(true), matching .NET's Raise_Exception(Exception_Task)->Run_Base Critique path. Preferred (closest parity): throw an exception, e.g. replace lines 104-107 with `if (props.getCompartimentMode() == TaskCompartimentMode.UNKNOWN) { throw new IllegalStateException("Erreur sur la tache " + task.getId() + " - manque le mode de la tache compartiment sur le run"); }` (use the project's existing task-exception type if one exists; the wrapper at ProcessTasksServiceImpl:86-91 will record CRITIQUE and flag the task). Acceptable alternative if throwing from a strategy is discouraged here: directly record the critical error and flag the task before returning, e.g. `task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.CRITIQUE, "Erreur sur la tache " + task.getId() + " - manque le mode de la tache compartiment sur le run")); task.setInError(true); return;`. Either way the message should mirror .NET MSG_Load_Task_Compartiment formatted with task.ID.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java (104-107) silently returns; .NET throws Exception_Task (Raise_Exception literally `throw ex`), caught by Run_Base.Process catch(Exception_Base) which sets InError=true and Errors.Add(Critique). The Java pipeline already mirrors this: ProcessTasksServiceImpl.java:86-91 catches any Exception, calls task.setInError(true) and adds RunError(CRITIQUE=2). The finding's preferred fix (throw IllegalStateException with the MSG_Load_Task_Compartiment-style message) is correctly converted by that wrapper to a CRITIQUE error + InError, achieving functional parity (task aborted, run continues — Critique, not Bloquante). Minor cosmetic divergence: Java wrapper prefixes "Erreur lors du traitement de la tache X : " before the message vs .NET recording the raw formatted message, but severity/flag/flow match. Severity MEDIUM is right: silent clean-looking output vs recorded critical failure (parity + data-integrity visibility), not whole-run-blocking. Both fix variants offered work; the throw is closer to .NET's mechanism.

### [#44] Group containing a sub-group only logs a warning instead of raising the .NET TGroupSubGroupProblem error  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/element/TGroup.java:158-176`
- **Area:** telement-helper
- **Scenario:** A template group whose boxRef resolves to another TGroup (nested group). After the loop, subGroup==true.
- **Java is (verified):** TGroup.java:142-145 `if (!elements.containsKey(refName)) { log.error("TGroup [{}] references unknown sub-element [{}]", getName(), refName); continue; }` and TGroup.java:173-176 `if (subGroup) { log.warn("TGroup [{}] on page [{}] contains sub-groups — this may cause issues", getName(), page); }` — both only log, then proceed.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Dynamique/Template/TGroup.cs:159,164,167-170` — Line 159: `throw new Exception_TElement(Constantes.Error_Messages.TGroupUnKnownSubGroup, __boxRef.name, _srcGroup.name);` Line 164: `_srcBoxes = __srcBoxes.ToArray();` Lines 167-170: `if(__subGroup){ throw new Exception_TElement(Constantes.Error_Messages.TGroupSubGroupProblem, _srcGroup.name, this.Page); }`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Real defect, confirmed on both branches. The proposed fix is directionally correct and (unlike the title) correctly covers BOTH divergences, but it is a sketch that will not compile: it references `TElementException`, `ErrorMessages.TGROUP_SUB_GROUP_PROBLEM`, `TGROUP_UNKNOWN_SUB_GROUP`, and `getPage()` that do not exist in the Java port. Concrete patch: (1) Create a RuntimeException type, e.g. `public class TElementException extends RuntimeException { public TElementException(String msg){ super(msg);} }` under .../domain/element/ (must be unchecked, since evaluate()/evaluateBoxes() are not declared to throw, and so it propagates to the same generation-abort boundary other engine fatal errors use). (2) Unknown-element branch — replace lines 143-144 (`log.error(...)` + `continue`) with: `throw new TElementException(String.format("Groupe inconnu %s référencé par le groupe %s", refName, srcGroup.getName()));` matching .NET line 159 (note .NET throws when the key is ABSENT — the Java `if (!elements.containsKey(refName))` guard is the correct equivalent). (3) Sub-group branch — replace lines 173-176 with: `if (subGroup) { throw new TElementException(String.format("Le groupe %s contient un sous-groupe (page %s)", srcGroup.getName(), page)); }`, keeping it AFTER `this.srcBoxes = resolvedBoxes.toArray(new Box[0]);` (line 171) to match .NET ordering at lines 164/167-170. (4) Use the field accessor `srcGroup.getName()` (or `getName()`) and `page` — the finding's `getPage()` is fine since @Getter generates it, but `page` field is in scope directly. Reuse the exact .NET message-key constants if they are later ported into the Java ErrorMessages.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java (TGroup.java:142-145, 173-176) only logs; .NET (TGroup.cs:159, 167-170) throws Exception_TElement (extends Exception_Base, real exception) in both the unknown-element and sub-group cases, aborting generation. Bug is real and reachable (any group boxRef resolving to a TGroup, or referencing a missing element). The proposed fix is conceptually right and notably covers both divergences with correct post-srcBoxes ordering, but it is not compilable as written (references nonexistent TElementException/ErrorMessages constants), so FIX_ADJUSTED with a concrete unchecked-exception implementation. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: silent malformed output vs. clear failure — data-incorrectness, not a crash.

### [#45] Table-cell boxes are never counted in getProjectInfo (Attr.getParentNode() is always null)  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/xml/QxpXml.java:483-499`
- **Area:** xml-xpath (domain/xml/QxpXml.java)
- **Scenario:** Any gabarit/document containing TABLE elements: buildProjectInfo()'s step-4 loop iterates //TABLE/GEOMETRY/@PAGE (attribute nodes) and then calls pageAttr.getParentNode() to reach GEOMETRY/TABLE. Per the W3C DOM spec, an Attr node's getParentNode() is ALWAYS null, so tableGeomNode is null, the `if (tableGeomNode != null && tableGeomNode.getParentNode() != null)` guard is always false, findCellIds() is never called, and no table cells are ever added to pageBoxes or nbBox.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpXml.java:483-489: `NodeList tableGeomPages = (NodeList) xpath.evaluate(BOX_TABLE_GEOMETRY_PAGE_PATTERN, ...);` ... `Node pageAttr = tableGeomPages.item(i);` ... line 489 `Node tableGeomNode = pageAttr.getParentNode(); // GEOMETRY`; line 490 `if (tableGeomNode != null && tableGeomNode.getParentNode() != null) {`. Pattern is line 103 `BOX_TABLE_GEOMETRY_PAGE_PATTERN = "//TABLE/GEOMETRY/@PAGE";` so item(i) is a org.w3c.dom Attr node whose getParentNode() is always null, making the guard always false and findCellIds() never invoked.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/QXP_XML.cs:423-444` — L423 `__iterator = this.Nav_XML.Select(BoxTableGeometryPagePattern);` (pattern L50 = "//TABLE/GEOMETRY/@PAGE"); L428 `__currentPage = ...ToInt(__iterator.Current.Value);`; L431 `__subIterator = __iterator.Current.Select(BoxTableRelativeTableGeometryPagePattern);` (pattern L51 = "../../ROW/CELL/ID"); L433-444 `while(__subIterator.MoveNext()){ ...PageBoxes[__currentPage]++; __dProjet_Info.NbBox++; }`. .NET XPathNavigator resolves "../.." from the @PAGE attribute up to GEOMETRY then TABLE, so each table cell IS counted.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The bug and the fix's logic are correct, but the primary approach as written ("cast pageAttr to org.w3c.dom.Attr") will not compile because org.w3c.dom.Attr is not imported (only Document, Node, NodeList are imported at lines 16-18). Two valid options:

OPTION A (matches finding's primary idea): add `import org.w3c.dom.Attr;` to the imports, then replace QxpXml.java lines 489-491 with:
  Element geomEl = ((Attr) pageAttr).getOwnerElement();          // GEOMETRY
  Node tableNode = (geomEl != null) ? geomEl.getParentNode() : null; // TABLE
  if (tableNode != null) {
(this also needs `import org.w3c.dom.Element;`). Keep the existing cell loop (lines 492-496) and the `currentPage > 0` guard.

OPTION B (cleaner, no new imports, recommended): change the pattern at line 103 to select elements: `BOX_TABLE_GEOMETRY_PAGE_PATTERN = "//TABLE/GEOMETRY";`. Then in the step-4 loop use the GEOMETRY element directly:
  Node geomNode = tableGeomPages.item(i);
  int currentPage = parseIntSafe(getAttributeValue(geomNode, "PAGE"));
  if (currentPage > 0) {
    Node tableNode = geomNode.getParentNode(); // TABLE
    if (tableNode != null) {
      NodeList cellIds = findCellIds(tableNode);
      for (int j = 0; j < cellIds.getLength(); j++) {
        info.getPageBoxes().merge(currentPage, 1, Integer::sum);
        totalBoxes++;
      }
    }
  }
Both restore .NET parity. Note: findCellIds uses ".//ROW/CELL/ID" (descendant) whereas .NET's "../../ROW/CELL/ID" is TABLE-relative children "ROW/CELL/ID"; for the standard TABLE/ROW/CELL/ID schema these are equivalent. The unused constant BOX_TABLE_RELATIVE_TABLE_GEOMETRY_PAGE_PATTERN (lines 105-107) can be removed.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java step-4 branch is dead because Attr.getParentNode()==null in org.w3c.dom; .NET (QXP_XML.cs:423-444) counts each table cell because XPathNavigator resolves the relative "../../ROW/CELL/ID" from the @PAGE attribute. So Java under-counts NbBox and per-page boxes for any document with TABLE elements. The finding's primary fix is logically right but does not compile as written (missing org.w3c.dom.Attr/Element imports), hence FIX_ADJUSTED with two compilable variants. Severity MEDIUM is correct: NbBox only feeds Document.cs:466-468 (guarded by NbBox>100, non-fatal length heuristic) and Run.cs:99 trace logging — wrong numbers, no crash/corruption.

### [#46] getPageNum returns 0 for a not-found bloc whereas .NET returns int.MinValue  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/xml/QxpXml.java:309-322`
- **Area:** xml-xpath (domain/xml/QxpXml.java)
- **Scenario:** getPageNum/getBlocInfo called for a bloc name that doesn't exist (getPageID returns empty). .NET: GetPageID()->"", "".TrimEnd('*')->"", ToInt("")->int.MinValue, so GetPageNum returns int.MinValue. Java returns 0. Downstream Task_Base.getPageIdFromRelative does spreadNum + relativePage, and DBlocInfo.page differs (0 vs MinValue).
- **Java is (verified):** QxpXml.java:310-321: `String pageId = getPageID(blocName); if (pageId == null || pageId.isBlank()) { return 0; } try { String cleaned = pageId.replaceAll("\\*+$", ""); return Integer.parseInt(cleaned); } catch (NumberFormatException e) { ...; return 0; }` — both not-found/unparseable branches return 0. getPageID (line 331-334) returns "" when bloc absent.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/QXP_XML.cs:284-290; QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:164-184` — QXP_XML.cs:286-289: `string __pageID = this.GetPageID(blocName); return QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToInt(__pageID.TrimEnd('*'));`. ConversionInvariante.cs:164-166: `public static int ToInt(string value){ return ToInt(value, int.MinValue); }`; lines 169-184: returns `__defaultValue` (int.MinValue) when `!Validation.IsSet(value)` or TryParse fails. GetPageID returns `string.Empty` when bloc not found.
- **Fix:** Match .NET ConversionInvariante.ToInt semantics by returning Integer.MIN_VALUE (not 0) when the cleaned pageID is blank or unparseable. Concretely, change the two `return 0;` branches in getPageNum (QxpXml.java:311-313 and 318-321) to `return Integer.MIN_VALUE;`. For closer parity also note .NET parses with Decimal.TryParse(NumberStyles.Any) (permissive: spaces, signs, decimals, thousands separators) and truncates to int; if any real pageID can carry such formatting, strip spaces and parse leniently (e.g., parse as BigDecimal then intValue) before falling back to Integer.MIN_VALUE. At minimum, the sentinel must be Integer.MIN_VALUE to preserve downstream page/spread arithmetic and sort behavior; update the Javadoc ("or 0 if not found") accordingly. If product owners instead want 0, that must be a documented intentional deviation, but the default should restore parity.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed: missing/unparseable bloc -> Java returns 0, .NET returns int.MinValue (ToInt default). The proposed fix (change both `return 0;` to `return Integer.MIN_VALUE;`) restores the sentinel and is the load-bearing correction; it compiles and matches .NET. The secondary note about .NET's more permissive Decimal.TryParse(NumberStyles.Any, strip spaces) parsing is optional hardening, not required for the described defect since real pageIds are plain integers (after TrimEnd('*')). The port already uses Integer.MIN_VALUE as the bloc page sentinel (BlocBase.java:19-20), so returning 0 is internally inconsistent too. Severity MEDIUM correct: no crash, only on misconfigured/absent bloc, but silently alters page/spread arithmetic, sort order, and masks the invalid-bloc signal. Should also update the Javadoc "or 0 if not found".

### [#47] Insert_Document SimpleJdbcCall omits withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() and is rebuilt per call — metadata-access dependency + double return-param risk  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:36-53`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** Every insertDocument() builds a fresh SimpleJdbcCall with withFunctionName("Insert_Document") and a declared SqlOutParameter("RETURN") but WITHOUT disabling procedure-column metadata access (unlike every read DAO). EndRunDaoImpl and InsertDataStorageDaoImpl share the same omission.
- **Java is (verified):** InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:36-39 `SimpleJdbcCall jdbcCall = new SimpleJdbcCall(dataSource).withCatalogName("QXP_PK_RUN").withFunctionName("Insert_Document").declareParameters(` — built inside insertDocument(), NO .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess(). Same omission: EndRunDaoImpl.java:32-35 (End_Run), :65-68 (Update_Status_Run), :91-94 (Insert_Run_Errors); InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.java:35-38 (Insert_Data). Contrast GetRunPropertiesDaoImpl.java:46 `.withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess()` (cached in @PostConstruct) and AuditDaoImpl.java:35 `.withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess()` (cached in constructor).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Document.cs:258-273` — `QXPDataOracle.OraParameter __id_doc = new QXPDataOracle.OraParameter("p_id_doc", OracleDbType.Int32, ParameterDirection.ReturnValue); __oraParameters.Add(__id_doc); __oraParameters.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_code_port", id_Fnd_Code)... .AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_id_run", id_run);` — all params declared explicitly; ODP.NET binds them without querying the data dictionary. ora.txt:9213 `FUNCTION Insert_Document(... 12 IN params) RETURN NUMBER` matches the declared set.
- **Fix:** In InsertDocumentDaoImpl, add .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() to the SimpleJdbcCall and move construction out of insertDocument() into a field initialized in @PostConstruct (or the constructor), mirroring AuditDaoImpl/GetRunPropertiesDaoImpl, so the call object is built once. Apply the identical change to EndRunDaoImpl (all three SimpleJdbcCalls: End_Run, Update_Status_Run, Insert_Run_Errors) and InsertDataStorageDaoImpl (Insert_Data). Keep the explicit declareParameters lists unchanged — with metadata access disabled Spring relies solely on the declared params, which already match the DDL and .NET. After the change, verify the function-return binding still resolves the "RETURN" key (it does, since withFunctionName treats the first declared SqlOutParameter as the return value); a quick integration test against the real proc is advisable to confirm the return-id is still populated.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java rebuilds the SimpleJdbcCall per call and omits .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() in 3 write DAOs (5 calls total); the project's own convention (GetRunPropertiesDaoImpl, AuditDaoImpl — also a write/PROCEDURE DAO) disables metadata access and caches. .NET declares every param explicitly and never touches the dictionary, so disabling metadata access is the parity-faithful choice. The fix correctly (a) adds .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() to all four DAOs and (b) caches the call object, keeping the existing declareParameters lists which already match DDL/.NET. With metadata disabled and SqlOutParameter("RETURN") declared first, withFunctionName still binds it as the function return — no compile or binding regression. Parity-impacting symptom (dictionary-SELECT dependency) is real but happy-path-equivalent in dev; MEDIUM is right.

### [#48] Get_Compartiment_Runs swallows all exceptions and returns empty map, diverging from .NET which propagates  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/GetCompartimentRunsDaoImpl.java:116-119`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** If the Oracle function or cursor mapping fails, the catch block logs and returns an empty LinkedHashMap; the compartiment task then processes zero child runs and the run completes 'successfully' with missing content.
- **Java is (verified):** GetCompartimentRunsDaoImpl.java:116-119: "} catch (Exception e) { log.error(\"Error fetching compartment runs for gabarit={}: {}\", idGabaritTete, e.getMessage(), e); return new LinkedHashMap<>(); }". Caller CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:144-148: "if (compartimentRuns.isEmpty()) { ... task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(2, \"Aucun run compartiment trouve...\"));"
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Run.cs:204-212; QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_Compartiment.cs:104-136` — Proxy_Run.cs:204-212: "using (QXPDataOracle.OraDataReader __odr = this.GetReader(__sqlRequest)) { while (__odr.Read()) { __list.Add(__odr[\"id_fnd_code\"], __odr[\"id_run\"]); } } return __list;" (no try/catch). Process_Compartiment.cs:133-135: "else { task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.NoneRunCompartiment, task.ID, task.Run.ID); }"
- **Fix:** Remove the swallow-and-return-empty catch so DB/cursor failures propagate (matching .NET, which has no try/catch). Either delete the try/catch entirely, or rethrow as an unchecked exception after logging: catch (Exception e) { log.error(...); throw new RuntimeException("Failed to fetch compartment runs for gabarit=" + idGabaritTete, e); }. This lets a genuine infra failure fail the run, while a legitimately empty (0-row) successful result still flows through and is correctly handled by CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.prepareRuns as the NoneRunCompartiment critique — preserving .NET's distinction between 'DB error' and 'no rows found'. Align with the other DAOs that let SQL exceptions propagate.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java DAO catches all exceptions and returns an empty LinkedHashMap (lines 116-119); the caller then records only a severity-2 RunError ("Aucun run compartiment trouve"), the benign no-data path. .NET Proxy_Run.Get_Compartiment_Runs has no try/catch around GetReader (Proxy_Run.cs:204-210), so a DB/cursor failure propagates and fails the run; only a genuine 0-row result triggers NoneRunCompartiment (Process_Compartiment.cs:133-135). The Java catch collapses the "DB error" case into the "no rows" case. The fix (delete the try/catch, or rethrow as RuntimeException after logging) makes infra failures propagate while a true 0-row success still flows through and yields the same severity-2 critique, exactly matching .NET. Either fix option is functionally correct; deleting the catch most closely mirrors .NET. The reporter's verifyNote correctly notes the scenario overstates "no error surfaced" (a critique is still recorded); MEDIUM is appropriate.

### [#49] DATE_TIME in-params truncated to date-only (java.sql.Date) — time component dropped  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `mapper/InParamSqlMapper.java:43-46`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** A run in-param with type DATE_TIME (code 5) and a value like '12/29/2023 14:30:00' is bound for task SQL via DynamicQueryPort/TaskSqlDao.
- **Java is (verified):** InParamSqlMapper.java:28-30 `if ((inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE || inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE_TIME) ...) { params.put(entry.getKey(), toSqlDate(inParam.getStringValue())); }`; line 43-46 `private Date toSqlDate(String dateValue) { ... LocalDate date = LocalDate.parse(dateValue.trim(), INPUT_FORMAT); return Date.valueOf(date); }`. Both DATE(4) and DATE_TIME(5) route through toSqlDate which returns java.sql.Date (date-only), discarding time.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:162-165; InParam.cs:54; InParams.cs:69; Enums/Data_Type.cs:31,35` — Data_Type_Helper.cs:162-165 `case Data_Type.Date: return QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime(value); default: return value;` — no case for DateTime(5), so DATE_TIME falls to default and returns the raw string. InParam.cs:54 `_value = Data_Type_Helper.InputToTypedValue(_string_value, _type);`. InParams.cs:69 `return new QXPDataOracle.OraParameter(__paramName, __inParam.Value);`. Data_Type.cs:31 `Date = 4`, :35 `DateTime = 5`.
- **Fix:** Split DATE and DATE_TIME handling. Keep DATE -> java.sql.Date (date-only), but for DATE_TIME parse to LocalDateTime with DateTimeFormatter 'M/d/yyyy HH:mm:ss' and bind java.sql.Timestamp (Timestamp.valueOf(localDateTime)) so the full time component is preserved, matching .NET's behavior where Oracle's to_date parses the complete timestamp. Concretely: in toParameterMap, branch on the type — if DATE call toSqlDate(...), if DATE_TIME call a new toSqlTimestamp(...) that returns java.sql.Timestamp. Provide an analogous fallback chain in toSqlTimestamp (try INPUT_FORMAT, then ISO LocalDateTime) and throw IllegalArgumentException on parse failure as the existing code does. (Equivalently, to mirror .NET's literal default path you could bind the raw string for DATE_TIME, but Timestamp is the cleaner JDBC-native equivalent.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. .NET: DATE(4)->typed DateTime (time preserved); DATE_TIME(5) has no case in InputToTypedValue, falls to default->raw string, bound directly so Oracle's to_date(:p,'M/d/yyyy HH:mm:ss') keeps full timestamp. Java routes BOTH DATE and DATE_TIME through toSqlDate->java.sql.Date, silently truncating time for DATE_TIME. Reachable via DynamicQueryPortImpl.executeQuery->toParameterMap. Real silent-wrong-results parity break; MEDIUM justified (modeled first-class type, no exception thrown). Proposed fix (branch DATE_TIME to new toSqlTimestamp returning java.sql.Timestamp via Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDateTime.parse(value,INPUT_FORMAT)) with ISO fallback) restores the time component and matches .NET, consistent with the existing pattern of binding native temporal types. Needs added imports java.time.LocalDateTime and java.sql.Timestamp. (InputToTypedValue is invoked only from InParam.cs:54 ctor.)

### [#50] DATE (type 4) param loses time component by converting to java.sql.Date instead of DateTime  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `mapper/InParamSqlMapper.java:43-45`
- **Area:** mappers
- **Scenario:** An InParam with INPUT_DATA_TYPE = 4 (DATE) whose stored value carries a non-midnight time (Oracle date values include time-of-day).
- **Java is (verified):** InParamSqlMapper.java:28-30 `if ((inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE || inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE_TIME) ... ) { params.put(entry.getKey(), toSqlDate(inParam.getStringValue())); }`; :43-46 `private Date toSqlDate(String dateValue) { ... LocalDate date = LocalDate.parse(dateValue.trim(), INPUT_FORMAT); return Date.valueOf(date); }` — both DATE and DATE_TIME run through LocalDate -> java.sql.Date, truncating time-of-day.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:162-165; QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:301-313; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/InParam/InParam.cs:54; InParams.cs:69` — Data_Type_Helper.cs:162-165 `case Data_Type.Date: return QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToDateTime(value); default: return value;`; ConversionInvariante.cs:306-308 `if (Validation.IsSet(value) && DateTime.TryParse(value, InvariantCulture.DateTimeFormat, DateTimeStyles.None, out __newvalue)) return __newvalue;`; InParam.cs:54 `_value = Data_Type_Helper.InputToTypedValue(_string_value, _type);`; InParams.cs:69 `return new QXPDataOracle.OraParameter(__paramName, __inParam.Value);`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The Timestamp conversion is correct for the DATE branch (type 4) and faithfully matches .NET's System.DateTime bind. But the fix's instruction to apply it to BOTH branches does NOT match .NET for DATE_TIME (type 5): in Data_Type_Helper.InputToTypedValue the switch has no Data_Type.DateTime case, so it hits `default: return value` and .NET binds the RAW STRING (not a DateTime). To match .NET exactly:

1) Keep a Timestamp conversion for DATE only:
```java
import java.sql.Timestamp;
import java.time.LocalDateTime;

private Timestamp toSqlTimestamp(String dateValue) {
    try {
        return Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDateTime.parse(dateValue.trim(), INPUT_FORMAT));
    } catch (Exception e) {
        try {
            return Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDate.parse(dateValue.trim()).atStartOfDay());
        } catch (Exception e2) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Unable to parse date parameter value: '" + dateValue + "'", e2);
        }
    }
}
```
2) Change toParameterMap so ONLY DATE goes through toSqlTimestamp; DATE_TIME (and everything else) is bound as the raw string, matching .NET's default:
```java
if (inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE
        && inParam.getStringValue() != null && !inParam.getStringValue().isEmpty()) {
    params.put(entry.getKey(), toSqlTimestamp(inParam.getStringValue()));
} else {
    params.put(entry.getKey(), inParam.getStringValue());
}
```
Remove the now-unused toSqlDate and the java.sql.Date import. This both preserves time-of-day for DATE (the reported bug) and stops Java from typing DATE_TIME, restoring .NET's raw-string bind.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. .NET: InParam.Value = ToDateTime(value) for Date, which DateTime.TryParse preserves time-of-day; InParams.InitOraParam binds that typed value. Java truncates via LocalDate->java.sql.Date for both DATE and DATE_TIME. Real parity bug for DATE. The proposed fix fixes DATE correctly but wrongly applies Timestamp to DATE_TIME too — .NET's InputToTypedValue has no DateTime case and returns the raw string, so faithful behavior is to bind DATE_TIME as a string. Hence FIX_ADJUSTED. Severity MEDIUM: silent data-correctness divergence only when a date param carries a non-midnight time and SQL compares the full timestamp; no crash.

### [#51] INT/DECIMAL params are bound as raw strings instead of typed numeric values  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `mapper/InParamSqlMapper.java:31-32`
- **Area:** mappers
- **Scenario:** An InParam with INPUT_DATA_TYPE = 2 (INT) or 3 (DECIMAL) used in a SQL bind variable, particularly when the stored 'valeur' string uses a locale-specific decimal separator.
- **Java is (verified):** InParamSqlMapper.java:28-33: `if ((inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE || inParam.getType() == DataTypeEnum.DATE_TIME) && ...) { params.put(entry.getKey(), toSqlDate(inParam.getStringValue())); } else { params.put(entry.getKey(), inParam.getStringValue()); }` — INT/DECIMAL fall into the else branch and bind the raw String. InParam.java:16 holds only `private final String stringValue;` (no typed value).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/InParam/InParam.cs:54; InParams.cs:69; Helper/Data_Type_Helper.cs:158-161; QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:169-183,216-229` — InParam.cs:54 `_value = Data_Type_Helper.InputToTypedValue(_string_value, _type);`  Data_Type_Helper.cs:158-161 `case Data_Type.Int: return ConversionInvariante.ToInt(value); case Data_Type.Decimal: return ConversionInvariante.ToDecimal(value);`  InParams.cs:69 `return new QXPDataOracle.OraParameter(__paramName, __inParam.Value);` (binds the TYPED Value, not the string). ConversionInvariante.cs:174-177 ToInt uses `Decimal.TryParse(value.Replace(" ",""), NumberStyles.Any, InvariantCulture.NumberFormat,...)` then `(Int32)` cast, default int.MinValue; ToDecimal:221-227 same parse, default decimal.MinValue.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Bind typed numerics for INT/DECIMAL mirroring ConversionInvariante, but two corrections to the proposed fix: (1) .NET's ToInt casts the parsed decimal to Int32 (truncation toward zero) and produces a System.Int32 — bind an Integer with that truncation, not a Long. Concretely parse as BigDecimal(stringValue.replace(" ",""), with '.' decimal point via Locale.ROOT), then for INT take .toBigInteger().intValueExact()-equivalent truncation -> bind Integer; for DECIMAL bind the BigDecimal. (Long is acceptable as a tolerant variant since Oracle treats them the same, but to be exact use Integer to match the Int32 cast/truncation .NET performs.) (2) Do NOT skip empty/null like the DATE branch — that contradicts .NET. In .NET the InParam ctor ALWAYS calls InputToTypedValue, and for empty/null Validation.IsSet fails so ToInt returns int.MinValue and ToDecimal returns decimal.MinValue (the sentinels are bound, not a raw/empty string). So for INT/DECIMAL with null/empty/unparseable value, bind Integer.MIN_VALUE / new BigDecimal(Decimal.MinValue) respectively (catch parse failure, return sentinel) rather than raw string. Steps: else-if type==INT -> params.put(key, parseInt32Invariant(s)); else-if type==DECIMAL -> params.put(key, parseDecimalInvariant(s)); else raw string. parseInt32Invariant: if blank return Integer.MIN_VALUE; try new BigDecimal(s.replace(" ","")).intValue() (BigDecimal.intValue truncates and narrows like the .NET (Int32) cast); on NumberFormatException return Integer.MIN_VALUE. parseDecimalInvariant: if blank return BigDecimal sentinel; try new BigDecimal(s.replace(" ","")); on failure return BigDecimal sentinel. Also correct InParam.java:8-9 Javadoc which falsely claims the raw string is passed directly like OraParameter — .NET's OraParameter was built from the typed Value.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. .NET binds native int/decimal (InParam.cs:54 -> InParams.cs:69 uses the typed Value); Java binds raw String for INT/DECIMAL (InParamSqlMapper.java:32), reachable via DynamicQueryPortImpl/ProcessSqlBusiness feeding JDBC named params. Bug is real (divergence under non-default NLS or grouped/space values). The proposed fix's direction is right but two inaccuracies: it suggests Long (not the Int32 truncation .NET does) and suggests skipping empty/null like DATE — but .NET always converts and binds int.MinValue/decimal.MinValue sentinels for empty/unparseable, so the raw-string fallback for empties would itself diverge. Corrected in adjustedFix. Severity MEDIUM is right: common dot-decimal literals convert identically under standard NLS; impact is confined to locale/NLS edge cases.

### [#52] parseIndexLignes throws NumberFormatException on non-numeric INDEX_LIGNES token, aborting exception loading  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `mapper/TaskExceptionMapper.java:40-43`
- **Area:** mappers
- **Scenario:** A QXP_TACHE_EXCEPTION row whose INDEX_LIGNES contains a non-integer or malformed token (e.g. stray whitespace inside a token, or any non-digit). mapToTaskException is called per row inside LoadTasksServiceImpl.loadTaskExceptions.
- **Java is (verified):** TaskExceptionMapper.java:40-43: `return Arrays.stream(value.split("\\|")) .filter(s -> !s.isBlank()) .mapToInt(Integer::parseInt) .toArray();` and lines 29-31: `TaskExceptionTypeEnum type = (indexLignes.length > 0) ? TaskExceptionTypeEnum.LINE : TaskExceptionTypeEnum.TABLE;`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Task.cs:188; QXP.Framework/Core/Conversion.cs:178-211,440-453; QXP.Framework/Core/Validation.cs:233-236` — Proxy_Task.cs:188 `__index_Lignes = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToIntArray(__str_index_lignes.Split('|'));`. Conversion.cs:440-453 ToIntArray foreach token `__list.Add(Conversion.ToInt(__value));`. Conversion.cs:193-211 ToInt: `if (Validation.IsSet(value) && Decimal.TryParse(value.Replace(" ", ""), NumberStyles.Any, ...)) {...} else { return __defaultValue; }` with defaultValue int.MinValue. Validation.cs:233 IsSet(ICollection) `return (IsNotNull(collection) && collection.Count != 0);`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Mirror .NET exactly. .NET splits on '|', keeps every token (incl. blanks), and per token calls ToInt -> int.MinValue on empty/unparseable, using Decimal.TryParse(value.Replace(" ",""), NumberStyles.Any) so decimal/grouped strings (e.g. "12.0", "1,234") parse and cast to int rather than fail. The finding's primary fix (Integer.parseInt + catch, keep blanks) fixes the throw and the blank-drop but is still stricter than .NET (parseInt("12.0") -> fallback int.MinValue, whereas .NET -> 12), so it is not byte-identical. Use BigDecimal:\n\n```java\nprivate int[] parseIndexLignes(String value) {\n    if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n        return new int[0];\n    }\n    String[] tokens = value.split("\\\\|", -1); // -1 keeps trailing empties like .NET Split('|')\n    int[] result = new int[tokens.length];\n    for (int i = 0; i < tokens.length; i++) {\n        result[i] = toInt(tokens[i]);\n    }\n    return result;\n}\n\nprivate int toInt(String token) {\n    if (token == null) return Integer.MIN_VALUE;\n    String v = token.replace(" ", "");\n    if (v.isEmpty()) return Integer.MIN_VALUE;\n    try {\n        // NumberStyles.Any-equivalent: tolerate decimals/grouping, then truncate toward zero like (Int32)decimal\n        java.math.BigDecimal d = new java.math.BigDecimal(v.replace(",", ""));\n        return d.intValue();\n    } catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n        return Integer.MIN_VALUE;\n    }\n}\n```\n\nNotes: (1) guard at line 37 must check isEmpty (not isBlank) and must NOT short-circuit "|" because .NET produces a 2-element array for "|"; keep split with limit -1. (2) Classification at 29-31 should stay length>0 (matches .NET IsSet(ICollection) = Count!=0, which is element-value-independent). (3) Optionally wrap the per-row mapToTaskException call in LoadTasksServiceImpl in try/catch+log as defense-in-depth, but the parity fix belongs in the mapper.
- **Reconciler note:** Bug is real on two axes. (1) Throw: Java calls Integer::parseInt with no guard, so a non-numeric INDEX_LIGNES token throws NumberFormatException that propagates out of the mapper and aborts loadTaskExceptions for the whole run; .NET's ToInt never throws (returns int.MinValue). (2) Classification: Java's filter(!isBlank) drops blank tokens, so "|" -> empty array -> TABLE, while .NET keeps blanks (Split('|') + ToInt("")=int.MinValue) -> non-empty array -> LINE (IsSet=Count!=0). The finding's proposed fix addresses both but uses Integer.parseInt, which is stricter than .NET's Decimal.TryParse(NumberStyles.Any) for decimal/grouped tokens; BigDecimal-based parse and split(...,-1) are needed for true parity. Severity MEDIUM confirmed: real divergence with operational impact (aborted batch / mis-classification), gated on malformed data in a DB-controlled column.

### [#53] Audit DURATION computed in seconds vs .NET sub-second milliseconds component  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/AuditDaoImpl.java:51-53, 62`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Every audit row. Java sets p_duration = Duration.between(start,end).getSeconds(); .NET Audit.Duration = ((TimeSpan)(endDate-startDate)).Milliseconds.
- **Java is (verified):** AuditDaoImpl.java:53 `durationSeconds = Duration.between(run.getStartDate(), run.getEndDate()).getSeconds();` and AuditDaoImpl.java:62 `.addValue("p_duration", durationSeconds)`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Audit/Audit.cs:93-95; Proxy_Audit.cs:101` — Audit.cs:93-95 `public int Duration { get { return ((TimeSpan)(_endDate-_startDate)).Milliseconds;} }`; Proxy_Audit.cs:101 `.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_duration", audit.Duration)`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** For strict .NET parity (the stated goal of 100% functional parity), replace getSeconds() with the sub-second millisecond COMPONENT, matching .NET's TimeSpan.Milliseconds (0-999). In AuditDaoImpl.insertAuditRun, change the type to int and: `int durationMs = 0; if (run.getStartDate() != null && run.getEndDate() != null) { durationMs = Duration.between(run.getStartDate(), run.getEndDate()).toMillisPart(); } ... .addValue("p_duration", durationMs);`. Duration.toMillisPart() (Java 9+) returns the milliseconds within the sub-second part (0-999), exactly equal to .NET TimeSpan.Milliseconds. Do NOT use toMillis() (total ms) — that is the finding's "recommended" option (2) and does NOT match .NET; it deliberately deviates. Use toMillisPart() to preserve persisted-data parity. (If stakeholders explicitly approve correcting the latent .NET bug, toMillis() would be the sensible value, but that is a deviation, not parity.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed by reopening sources. Java writes whole seconds; .NET writes the sub-second ms component (0-999) via TimeSpan.Milliseconds, passed directly to the same QXP_AUDIT_RUN.DURATION column. Values are genuinely unrelated, so this is a real persisted-data divergence on every audit row. The fix is marked FIX_ADJUSTED: the finding's two options conflict, and its RECOMMENDED option (2, toMillis total) intentionally does NOT match .NET. For the project's strict-parity goal, only toMillisPart() reproduces .NET behavior; getSeconds() must go. Severity MEDIUM stands: affects a diagnostic/reporting column only (not engine control flow), and the .NET source value is itself effectively a latent bug, capping downstream impact.

### [#54] load() runs in-params/tasks/templates even in Mode_Degrade; .NET skips them  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/impl/ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:162-183 (load)`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Oversized gabarit sets modeDegrade during prepareGabarit (step 2). Java still executes getInParamsBusiness, loadTasksService.loadTasks, loadTemplatesBusiness unconditionally.
- **Java is (verified):** ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:171 run.prepareGabarit(getGabaritBusiness, filePoolPort, documentIdentityPort);  :174 getInParamsBusiness.execute(run);  :177 loadTasksService.loadTasks(run);  :180 loadTemplatesBusiness.execute(run); — all unconditional, no Mode_Degrade guard. Run.java:159-162: if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { ... this.runProperties.setModeDegrade(true); return; } sets the flag inside prepareGabarit (step 2).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:302-317` — 302 Execution_Helper.Universal_Executer<Exception_Run>(this.PrepareGabarit, ...);  305 if (!this.Mode_Degrade) {  308 ...(this.LoadInParams, ...);  310 this.InParams.InitOraParameters();  313 ...(this.LoadTasks, ...);  316 ...(this.LoadTemplates, ...); } 
- **Fix:** In ProcessRunServiceImpl.load(), guard steps 3-5 after prepareGabarit, mirroring .NET Run_Base.Load() lines 305-317:

run.prepareGabarit(getGabaritBusiness, filePoolPort, documentIdentityPort);

if (!run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade()) {
    getInParamsBusiness.execute(run);      // LoadInParams + InitOraParameters
    loadTasksService.loadTasks(run);       // LoadTasks
    loadTemplatesBusiness.execute(run);    // LoadTemplates
}

This keeps load() faithful to .NET (flag is set during prepareGabarit/PrepareGabarit which still runs in both modes) and the existing outer guard at line 69 remains correct for the Launch phase. No behavioral change for non-degraded runs.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified from both sources. .NET Run_Base.Load() wraps LoadInParams + InitOraParameters + LoadTasks + LoadTemplates in if(!Mode_Degrade) AFTER PrepareGabarit, the same point where the flag becomes available (Mode_Degrade derives from Gabarit/Gabarit_Template, Run_Base.cs:915-926). Java load() runs the equivalent three steps (getInParamsBusiness=LoadInParams+InitOraParameters, loadTasks, loadTemplates) unconditionally after prepareGabarit, which itself sets modeDegrade=true on oversized gabarit (Run.java:159-162). The proposed fix wraps exactly these three steps in if(!run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade()), matching .NET. Getter naming (run.getRunProperties().isModeDegrade()) is consistent with lines 69/114. Outer guard at line 69 is the Launch-phase guard, not the load-internal one, so it does not compensate. MEDIUM correct: wasted work plus risk of surfacing secondary template-load failures .NET never reaches; no corruption.

### [#55] NPE risk when gabarit content (Data) is null at the Mode_Degrade size check  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:159`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** A gabarit row whose 'contenu' BLOB is null reaches prepareGabarit; this.gabarit.getData().length throws NullPointerException.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:159 `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) {` and Run.java:161 `this.gabarit.getData().length, sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft, this.id);` — getData() dereferenced for .length with no null guard. DAOs populate data via rs.getBytes("contenu") (GetGabaritDaoImpl:87, GetGabaritDocumentDaoImpl:81, GetGabaritDocumentCertifieDaoImpl:82), which returns null on SQL NULL.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:228-239; Run.cs:92,94` — Document.cs:231-238 `if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(this.Data)) { return (_data.Length > SizeLimitBeforeFailSoft); } else { return false; }`. Run.cs:92 `QXPS_File_Manager.Addfile(this.Gabarit.FilePoolPath, this.Gabarit.Data);` (unconditional); Run.cs:94 `this.Gabarit.Evaluate_Document_Identity();` (DID skipped internally when Mode_Degrade per Document.cs:200).
- **Fix:** Guard the size check against null data so the null-data case behaves like .NET (not degraded, continue): replace line 159's condition with `if (this.gabarit.getData() != null && this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { ... }`. With this guard, when getData() is null the method falls through to the file-pool add (Step 1) and DID parsing, exactly matching .NET Run.cs which calls Addfile unconditionally and reports Mode_Degrade=false. Optionally store getData() in a local byte[] to avoid the double call. No need to add a Type==QXP check since the gabarit DAOs always set format "QXP".
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java unconditionally dereferences getData().length (Run.java:159,161); .NET Evaluate_Mode_Degrade returns false when !IsSet(Data), never touching _data.Length, so .NET cannot NPE on null Data. Null Data is reachable (DAOs use rs.getBytes("contenu")). The proposed fix (null-guard the size check, optionally cache getData() in a local) makes the null case fall through to addFile + DID parsing, matching .NET where Addfile (Run.cs:92) runs unconditionally even with null Data. Fix is sound for the stated NPE scope. Caveat (out of scope, not a defect of this fix): the degraded path's early-return at line 163 still skips addFile even for non-null oversized gabarits, whereas .NET always calls Addfile and only skips DID — a separate divergence. For THIS finding (null-data NPE), the fix correctly matches .NET. Severity MEDIUM correct: genuine reachable NPE on edge case (null contenu BLOB) within load chain.

### [#56] Missing template-ID is silently skipped instead of raising Bloquante; no dynamic-task gating  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `business/LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:35-38`
- **Area:** load-chain (Run_Base.Load port: properties → gabarit → in-params → tasks/exceptions/DID → templates)
- **Scenario:** Run has dynamic tasks in TODO but idGabaritTemplate is unset (Integer.MIN_VALUE). Java logs 'No gabarit template ID... skipping' and returns; .NET raises a Bloquante Exception_Run (MSG_Missing_ID_Gabarit_Template). Conversely, when there are NO dynamic tasks, .NET skips template loading entirely; Java loads them whenever an ID is present.
- **Java is (verified):** LoadTemplatesBusiness.java:35-38: `if (idGabaritTemplate == Integer.MIN_VALUE) { log.info("No gabarit template ID set for run [{}], skipping template loading", run.getId()); return; }` — then unconditionally loads when an ID is present (lines 40-54). No scan of run.getTasks() for any TaskDynamique, no Bloquante error path.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run.cs:132-165` — L137 `foreach(Task_Dynamique __task in this.Tasks.GetEnumerable<Task_Dynamique>(true)) { __load_templates = true; break; }`; L144 `if(__load_templates)`; L147 `if(QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(Properties.ID_Gabarit_Template))` -> loads gabarit_template + templates; L163 `else { Exception_Helper.Raise_Exception(new Exception_Run(Exception_Run.MSG_Missing_ID_Gabarit_Template, this.Gabarit.ID), Error_Type.Bloquante); }`.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Rewrite execute(run): (1) boolean hasDynamicTodo = run.getTasks().values().stream().anyMatch(t -> t instanceof TaskDynamique && t.isTodo()); — DO filter on todo, because .NET's GetEnumerable<Task_Dynamique>(true) filters Todo==true and TaskBase already exposes `boolean todo` (TaskBase.java:22, getter isTodo()); the finding's claim that no status field exists is incorrect. (2) if (!hasDynamicTodo) return; (do not load templates when no dynamic TODO task). (3) int idGabaritTemplate = run.getRunProperties().getIdGabaritTemplate(); if (idGabaritTemplate == Integer.MIN_VALUE) throw a RuntimeException carrying the missing-ID message (e.g. new RuntimeException("Identifiant du gabarit template manquant (gabarit " + run.getGabarit().getId() + ")")) — DO NOT merely run.getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.BLOQUANTE, ...)), because adding to the errors list does NOT interrupt load()/processing; .NET Raise_Exception THROWS. ProcessRunServiceImpl.load() runs inside the try at line 66, and the catch at lines 103-108 registers the thrown exception as new RunError(RunError.BLOQUANTE, ex.getMessage()) (note BLOQUANTE=3, not 1 as the original fix stated), setting status ERROR — exactly mirroring .NET Run_Base.Launch. (4) Only when hasDynamicTodo && id set, proceed to load gabarit template + templates as today, keeping existing success-path log lines.
- **Reconciler note:** Bug is real on both counts: (a) absent fail-fast Bloquante when a dynamic-TODO task exists but ID unset; (b) templates loaded even with no dynamic task. Reachable: load() step 5 runs after loadTasks (step 4), so tasks are populated. Proposed fix is directionally right but has three defects I corrected: it falsely claims TaskBase has no status field (it has `todo`, TaskBase.java:22), so TODO filtering MUST be reproduced; it cites BLOQUANTE as category 1 (actually 3, RunError.java:24); and it conflates adding a RunError with interrupting — only throwing interrupts the run (caught at ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:103-108). MEDIUM is correct: no data corruption, but a fail-fast misconfiguration error is silently swallowed.

### [#57] generateFileNames lowercases the format extension; .NET uses Format verbatim — persisted document name / pool file extension diverges  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/DocumentDomain.java:275-279 (generateFileNames, this.format.toLowerCase())`
- **Area:** enums-modes
- **Scenario:** Final result documents are created via the parameterized constructor in ProcessRunServiceImpl.buildRunResult (formats "JPEG","PDF","QXP"). generateFileNames produces DF_<id>.qxp / .pdf / .jpeg (lowercase). This fileName is persisted as p_nom_document by EndRunBusiness/InsertDocumentDao.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentDomain.java:277: `this.fileName = String.format(FILE_NAME_PREFIX_PATTERN, this.prefix, this.id, this.format.toLowerCase());` (pattern line 38 = "%s_%d.%s"). Corroborating: GetDocumentDaoImpl.java:94 builds the same name with `doc.getFormat()` VERBATIM (no toLowerCase); QxpsCallerBusiness.java:275-277 comment "Use the Format verbatim... Lowercasing diverged from the .NET-produced pool name." buildRunResult (ProcessRunServiceImpl.java:146-158) passes uppercase "JPEG"/"PDF"/"QXP"; InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:62 `params.put("p_nom_document", document.getFileName());`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:115; Constantes.cs:97-108; Run_Base.cs:513/518/523` — Document.cs:115: `_fileName = string.Format(FileNamePrefixePattern, _prefix, this.ID, format);` (pattern line 46 = "{0}_{1}.{2}", format used as-is, no ToLower). Constantes.cs:97/99/106: `public const string PDF = "PDF"; ... JPEG = "JPEG"; ... QXP = "QXP";`. Run_Base.cs:513: `new Document(this.ID, ..., Constantes.Document_Format.JPEG, Document.FileDocumentFinalPrefix, ...)` (and PDF/QXP at 518/523).
- **Fix:** In DocumentDomain.generateFileNames() (line 277) remove the .toLowerCase() call so the extension uses the verbatim format: this.fileName = String.format(FILE_NAME_PREFIX_PATTERN, this.prefix, this.id, this.format); This matches .NET Document.cs:115 and aligns generateFileNames() with the five DAO name-builders and QxpsCallerBusiness.getNewGabaritNameExt that already use doc.getFormat() verbatim.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple fully verified from source. Java DocumentDomain.generateFileNames() lowercases the format extension (line 277), while .NET Document.cs:115 uses the format string verbatim, and the format constants (Constantes.cs:97-108) plus the call sites (Run_Base.cs:513/518/523 -> Java buildRunResult) are uppercase. The fileName is persisted unchanged to p_nom_document (InsertDocumentDaoImpl:62 == Proxy_Document p_nom_document). So .NET stores DF_<id>.QXP/.PDF/.JPEG; Java stores lowercase — a genuine divergence on case-sensitive stores/filesystems. The fix (remove .toLowerCase()) compiles, matches .NET exactly, and aligns generateFileNames with the load-path DAOs and QxpsCallerBusiness which already use getFormat() verbatim. p_format column unaffected. Severity MEDIUM is correct: cosmetic on case-insensitive stores, breaks lookups/comparisons on case-sensitive ones.

### [#58] Missing process strategy is swallowed silently instead of being a controlled error  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/task/impl/TaskProcessServiceImpl.java:32-37`
- **Area:** process-tasks
- **Scenario:** A concrete TaskBase subclass is added (or mis-registered) without a matching TaskProcessStrategy bean; that task is Todo and not in error.
- **Java is (verified):** TaskProcessServiceImpl.java:31-37: `public void process(TaskBase task) { TaskProcessStrategy strategy = strategyMap.get(task.getClass()); if (strategy != null) { strategy.process(task); } else { log.warn("No process strategy registered for task type: {}", task.getClass().getSimpleName()); } }` — and ProcessTasksServiceImpl.java:115-119 reports a no-bloc task as RunError.UNSPECIFIED, not a hard error.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:414 (and catch at 428-432)` — Run_Base.cs:414: `Execution_Helper.Universal_Executer<Exception_Task>(__task.Process, Exception_Task.MSG_Process_Task, __task.ID, this.Debug_Info);` — polymorphic call on the abstract/virtual Task_Base.Process; no handler lookup. On unknown failure, lines 430-431: `__task.InError = true; this.Errors.Add(Error_Type.Critique, ...)`.
- **Fix:** In TaskProcessServiceImpl.process(), replace the WARN-and-return on a null strategy with a thrown exception so the gap fails loud: change the else branch to `throw new IllegalStateException("No process strategy registered for task type: " + task.getClass().getName());`. This propagates into ProcessTasksServiceImpl Pass 1's try/catch (line 86), which sets task.setInError(true) and records a CRITIQUE RunError — matching .NET's behavior where an unprocessable task is a hard failure rather than a silently-dropped one. Optionally also add a startup assertion that every concrete TaskBase subclass has a registered strategy to catch the misconfiguration at boot.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple. Java: process() only logs WARN and returns on a missing strategy (no exception, no inError); such a task produces zero blocs and is downgraded to a non-fatal UNSPECIFIED error in Pass 3, so the run "succeeds" while silently dropping that task's modifications. .NET dispatches via polymorphism (Run_Base.cs:414) — every Task_Base implements Process, so a task can never be silently skipped; any failure becomes InError=true + Critique error. The proposed fix (throw IllegalStateException) propagates into Pass 1's `catch (Exception ex)` (ProcessTasksServiceImpl:86-91), which sets setInError(true) and adds a CRITIQUE RunError — exactly matching .NET lines 430-431. Compiles cleanly (process() declares no checked exceptions; IllegalStateException is unchecked). Severity correctly LOW: latent, since all 7 registered strategies cover every instantiated TaskBase subclass today.

### [#59] Redundant RunTask creation before re-processing  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/impl/CheckServiceImpl.java:182`
- **Area:** CheckServiceImpl — overflow detection, re-processing, SQL/document data collection, gabarit XML refresh timing
