- **Scenario:** Overflow detected and tasksToReprocess non-empty: checkOverflow does run.setRunTask(new RunTask(run)) then calls processTasksService.processTasks(run).
- **Java is (verified):** CheckServiceImpl.java:181-184: "// Re-create RunTask for re-processing\n            run.setRunTask(new RunTask(run));\n\n            processTasksService.processTasks(run);" — and ProcessTasksServiceImpl.java:64-66: "RunTask runTask = new RunTask(run);\n        runTask.setSplitStepBoxNumber(stepLimit); // configurable step limit\n        run.setRunTask(runTask);" which overwrites the line-182 object.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:663-669 (Check overflow path) and Run_Base.cs:209-216 (Launch_Process) and Run_Base.cs:393 (Process)` — Run_Base.cs:663-669: "if (__tasks_to_reprocess.Count > 0)\n{\n    this.Launch_Process();\n    this.Launch_Process_Steps();\n}"; Run_Base.cs:215: "this.Process();"; Run_Base.cs:393: "_run_Task = new Run_Task(this);" — Check() never creates a Run_Task; Process() is the sole creator during reprocessing.
- **Fix:** Delete line 182 (`run.setRunTask(new RunTask(run));`) and its preceding comment. processTasksService.processTasks(run) at line 184 already creates and configures the RunTask (with splitStepBoxNumber(stepLimit)) as the single source of truth, matching .NET where Process() is the sole creator of _run_Task during re-processing and Check() never creates one. Optionally verify the RunTask import in CheckServiceImpl is no longer needed and remove it if unused.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified by reopening both sources. Java line 182 builds a RunTask that is unconditionally overwritten at ProcessTasksServiceImpl.java:64-66 before any read, so it is dead code. .NET Check() overflow path calls Launch_Process()->Process(), and Process() (Run_Base.cs:393) is the only creator of _run_Task during reprocessing; Check() never creates one. Deleting line 182 + its comment makes processTasks() the single creator, matching .NET exactly, and cannot break behavior since the object was never used. The import-removal step in the fix is correct as an optional cleanup. No behavioral/parity impact (object immediately discarded), so LOW severity is correct.

### [#60] Double gabarit XML refresh when overflow re-processing ran and DOCUMENT storage is enabled  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `service/impl/CheckServiceImpl.java:67-70`
- **Area:** CheckServiceImpl — overflow detection, re-processing, SQL/document data collection, gabarit XML refresh timing
- **Scenario:** Overflow re-processing occurs (checkOverflow already calls refreshGabaritXml at line 188), then storeType has DOCUMENT so Phase 4 calls refreshGabaritXml again at line 69 before collectDocumentData.
- **Java is (verified):** CheckServiceImpl.java:53 `refreshGabaritXml(run);` (Phase 1); :188 `refreshGabaritXml(run);` (after processTasksService.processTasks + qxpsCallerService.process inside checkOverflow); :67-69 `if (storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.DOCUMENT)) { // Refresh XML again ... refreshGabaritXml(run);` (Phase 4, unconditional). On the overflow-reprocess + DOCUMENT path, line 188 and line 69 each call getGabaritXmlBusiness.fetchXml -> a duplicate QXPS round-trip for byte-identical XML.
- **.NET is (verified):** `Run_Base.cs:616,701; Document.cs:421-445,172-188` — Run_Base.cs:616 `__overflowBoxes = this.Gabarit.XML.GetOverflowBoxes();`; :701 `__docNamesValues = this.Gabarit.XML.GetNamesValuesBoxes();`. Document.cs:423-444 lazy getter: `if(this.Type==QXP && IsNull(_xml)){ ... _xml = QXPS_File_Manager.Get_XML(this); } return _xml;`. Document.cs:179 `_xml = null;` ONLY inside Change_Document (never called during the run's Process/Process_Steps). So Check() fetches XML at most ONCE and the DOCUMENT phase reads the cached pre-reprocess (stale) XML.
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The duplicate fetch is real, but the proposed guarded-flag fix does NOT achieve .NET parity: .NET does exactly ONE Get_XML in Check() (lazy _xml, never invalidated after Launch_Process/Launch_Process_Steps) and the DOCUMENT phase reads the cached PRE-reprocess (stale) XML. The proposed fix still leaves Java doing 2 fetches (line 53 + line 188) AND reading FRESH post-reprocess XML, which diverges from .NET in both fetch count and output content. For true 100% parity: fetch the gabarit XML lazily/once and do NOT re-fetch after reprocess. Concretely: (1) DELETE the line-188 refresh inside checkOverflow and the line-69 refresh in Phase 4; (2) keep only the single Phase-1 refresh at line 53 (which fetches before any reprocess, mirroring .NET reading the cached XML that predates Launch_Process). This makes both the overflow access (xml.getOverflowBoxes via checkOverflow) and the document access (collectDocumentData) read the same single cached XML, matching .NET's stale-read semantics exactly. NOTE: if intentional fresh-XML behavior is desired over byte-parity, then merely de-duplicating per the original fix is acceptable as a latency optimization — but it is NOT .NET-identical. Severity remains LOW since current output is arguably more correct than .NET; this is a parity/latency nuance, not a correctness regression.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java performs up to 3 refreshGabaritXml calls (lines 53, 188, 69); on the overflow-reprocess + DOCUMENT path lines 188 and 69 are an identical back-to-back fetch (nothing mutates QXPS between them) — a genuine redundant round-trip. .NET Document.XML is lazy-cached and only invalidated in Change_Document (never during the run), so Check() does ONE fetch total and the DOCUMENT phase reads stale pre-reprocess XML. The finding's proposed flag-guard fix removes the duplicate but keeps Java's deliberate divergence (extra Phase-1 fetch + fresh post-reprocess content), so it does not make Java behave identically to .NET — hence FIX_ADJUSTED. Bug is real but trivial (LOW): output is correct/arguably better; only network/latency cost.

### [#61] Early return on zero overflow boxes skips the 'set non-reprocess tasks Todo=false' loop done by .NET  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `service/impl/CheckServiceImpl.java:136-138`
- **Area:** CheckServiceImpl — overflow detection, re-processing, SQL/document data collection, gabarit XML refresh timing
- **Scenario:** Control_Overflow is true but the document has zero overflow boxes (or overflow boxes that belong to no controlled task).
- **Java is (verified):** CheckServiceImpl.java:136-138 `if (overflowBoxes.isEmpty()) { return; }` and CheckServiceImpl.java:171-176 `if (!tasksToReprocess.isEmpty()) { for (TaskBase task : run.getTasks().values()) { if (!tasksToReprocess.contains(task) && !task.isAllwaysReprocess()) { task.setTodo(false); } } ...` — both the early-return and the Todo sweep being gated behind the non-empty check are present.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Base.cs:622, 653-660, 663-670` — L622 `if (__overflowBoxes.Count >= 0)` (always true); L653-659 unconditional sweep inside that block `foreach (Task_Base __t in this.Tasks.Values){ if (!__tasks_to_reprocess.Contains(__t) && !__t.AllwaysReprocess){ __t.Todo = false; }}`; L663-670 only Launch_Process/Launch_Process_Steps gated by `if (__tasks_to_reprocess.Count > 0)`.
- **Fix:** Restructure checkOverflow to mirror .NET's unconditional Todo sweep. Remove the early `if (overflowBoxes.isEmpty()) return;` at lines 136-138 (keep the log line). Keep the per-task overflow-box matching loop as-is (it harmlessly produces an empty tasksToReprocess when nothing matches). Then move the Todo=false sweep OUT of the `if (!tasksToReprocess.isEmpty())` guard so it always runs inside the Control_Overflow branch: `for (TaskBase task : run.getTasks().values()) { if (!tasksToReprocess.contains(task) && !task.isAllwaysReprocess()) task.setTodo(false); }`. Keep ONLY the reprocess work (new RunTask, processTasks, qxpsCaller.process, refreshGabaritXml) gated behind `if (!tasksToReprocess.isEmpty())`. This matches Run_Base.cs:653-670 exactly. Given zero current impact, this can be deferred until a future step reads Todo after Check; if applied, add a comment noting the .NET `>=0` guard is intentionally always-true.
- **Reconciler note:** Confirmed by re-reading both sources. .NET guards the section with `Count >= 0` (always true) and runs the Todo=false sweep UNCONDITIONALLY inside it; only the reprocess (Launch_Process/Steps) is gated on Count>0. Java diverges twice: (1) early-return at 136-138 on empty overflowBoxes, (2) sweep gated behind !tasksToReprocess.isEmpty() at 171. The proposed fix — drop the early return, move the Todo sweep out of the guard, keep only RunTask/processTasks/qxpsCaller/refreshGabaritXml gated — reproduces Run_Base.cs:653-670 exactly. The per-task matching loop (Java 143-168) already mirrors .NET's GetEnumerable<Task_Dynamique>(true)+Control_Overflow filter (true=onlyTodo, contrast L680 (false)). LOW severity correct: no code after check() reads Task.Todo, so divergence is latent today; fix is for fidelity/future-proofing.

### [#62] Modify file timestamp format HHmmssSSS instead of .NET HHmmssff  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/QxpsCallerBusiness.java:43,150-151 (MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN / TIMESTAMP_FORMAT)`
- **Area:** qxps-caller (QxpsCallerBusiness / QxpsCallerServiceImpl / GetGabaritXmlBusiness)
- **Scenario:** Cosmetic: every directCall step with modifications.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsCallerBusiness.java:43 `private static final DateTimeFormatter TIMESTAMP_FORMAT = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("HHmmssSSS");`; line 40 `private static final String MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN = "Modify_%s.xml";`; lines 150-151 `String modifyFileName = String.format(MODIFY_NAME_PATTERN, LocalDateTime.now().format(TIMESTAMP_FORMAT));`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/QXPS/QXPS_Caller.cs:35 (and usage :142)` — Line 35: `const string ModifyNamePattern = "Modify_{0:HHmmssff}.xml";`  Line 142: `string __modify_Name = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(string.Format(ModifyNamePattern, DateTime.Now));`
- **Fix:** Optional, parity-only. To match .NET `{0:HHmmssff}` (2 fractional digits), change line 43 to DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("HHmmssSS"). Java's SS yields exactly 2 fraction-of-second digits, matching .NET ff. Otherwise leave HHmmssSSS as-is — finer (millisecond) resolution is harmless since the name is internally self-referenced within a single call and never matched against a .NET-generated filename.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified the triple directly in source. Java uses "HHmmssSSS" (3 fractional-second digits = milliseconds); .NET uses {0:HHmmssff} where ff = 2 fractional digits (centiseconds). Note: my first greps of the .cs returned nothing because the file is Latin-1 encoded (accented French comments); re-running with grep -a found the constant verbatim at line 35, used at line 142 via string.Format(..., DateTime.Now). So the deviation is real but purely cosmetic: the modify filename is generated, uploaded, then referenced within the same call and is never matched against an external .NET-produced name; finer resolution adds no collision risk. Proposed fix changes the pattern to "HHmmssSS"; in Java DateTimeFormatter the letter S is fraction-of-second and a count of 2 yields exactly 2 digits, matching .NET ff. Fix compiles and achieves parity. LOW severity confirmed.

### [#63] Response text/binary classification uses startsWith instead of .NET's exact content-type equality  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/client/QxpsHttpClient.java:124,163-167`
- **Area:** qxps-http (URL build/encoding, multipart POST, response parsing, error handling)
- **Scenario:** QXPS returns a Content-Type whose value is not exactly one of "", text/plain, text/xml, text/html — e.g. with a charset parameter (text/plain;charset=utf-8) or a binary type whose name happens to start with a text prefix.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsHttpClient.java:163-167 `private boolean isTextContentType(String contentType) { if (contentType == null || contentType.isEmpty()) return true; String lower = contentType.toLowerCase(); return TEXT_CONTENT_TYPES.stream().anyMatch(t -> !t.isEmpty() && lower.startsWith(t)); }` ; line 121 `String contentTypeValue = contentType != null ? contentType.toString() : "";` (MediaType.toString() includes parameters)
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Interop/QuarkServer/BusinessObject/QXPS_SDK/QXPS/QXPS_Call.cs:89-103` — _response_Message.ContentType = __response.ContentType; switch(__response.ContentType){ case "": case "text/plain": case "text/xml": case "text/html": ... default: binary }
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Replace the startsWith logic with EXACT equality on the full content-type value (no parameter stripping), to mirror .NET's `switch(__response.ContentType)` which compares the raw header verbatim. Implementation: `private boolean isTextContentType(String contentType){ if (contentType == null || contentType.isEmpty()) return true; return TEXT_CONTENT_TYPES.contains(contentType.toLowerCase()); }` where TEXT_CONTENT_TYPES keeps {"text/plain","text/xml","text/html",""}. Do NOT strip the ';'-parameter as the finding's fix proposes: .NET does exact equality on the raw header, so `text/plain;charset=utf-8` falls to default and is read as BINARY in .NET; the finding's parameter-stripping would instead classify it as TEXT, producing a NEW divergence from .NET rather than parity. (Note: .NET's switch is case-sensitive on the raw header; Java already lowercases via MediaType.toString(), and the deployed QXPS emits bare lowercase types, so equality on the lowercased value is functionally equivalent for the actual call set.)
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java isTextContentType uses lower.startsWith(t) (over-matches text/plain-foo) and contentTypeValue from MediaType.toString() includes parameters; .NET (QXPS_Call.cs:90-103) does an EXACT switch on the raw __response.ContentType header, default->binary. Genuine divergence. But the finding's proposed fix is wrong: it strips the ';' parameter before comparing, so text/plain;charset=utf-8 would be TEXT in the fixed Java while .NET reads it as BINARY (default). That is the opposite of .NET and introduces a fresh mismatch. Correct fix is exact equality on the full value (no stripping). Severity LOW stands: deployed QXPS returns bare types (text/xml for gabarit, application/* and image/* for binary), so neither over-match nor parameter cases actually fire on the known call set.

### [#64] HTTP protocol version not forced to 1.0 as in .NET  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxps/client/QxpsHttpClient.java:51-56`
- **Area:** qxps-http (URL build/encoding, multipart POST, response parsing, error handling)
- **Scenario:** Any QXPS call; reactor-netty defaults to HTTP/1.1.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsHttpClient.java:51-56 — `HttpClient httpClient = HttpClient.create() .option(ChannelOption.CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS, timeout) .doOnConnected(conn -> conn.addHandlerLast(new ReadTimeoutHandler(timeout, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS))) .responseTimeout(Duration.ofMillis(timeout));` — no protocol-version override; reactor-netty defaults to HTTP/1.1 with keep-alive and allows chunked transfer.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Interop/QuarkServer/BusinessObject/QXPS_SDK/QXPS/QXPS_Call.cs:78` — QXPS_Call.cs:78 — `__wr.ProtocolVersion = HttpVersion.Version10;` (inside SDKCall, after `HttpWebRequest __wr = (HttpWebRequest)WebRequest.Create(__uri);`). WriteMultipart at line 154 also sets `request.ContentLength = __data.Count;` (fixed length, no chunking).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The proposed fix is "document only / no change," which leaves Java on HTTP/1.1 and therefore NOT identical to .NET's pinned HTTP/1.0. Two real, achievable mitigations that move Java closer to .NET semantics should be applied rather than relying solely on a comment: (1) Disable persistent connections to mimic HTTP/1.0 connection-close behavior: `HttpClient.create().keepAlive(false)...`. (2) Avoid chunked transfer encoding so the upload matches .NET's fixed ContentLength: the body is already a fully-built byte[], so set `Content-Length` explicitly on the POST (.header(HttpHeaders.CONTENT_LENGTH, String.valueOf(multipartBody.length))) — bodyValue(byte[]) generally already produces a fixed Content-Length, but pinning it guarantees no chunking. A literal HTTP/1.0 request line is not expressible in reactor-netty, so exact protocol-version parity is unachievable; the above keep-alive-off + fixed-Content-Length pair is the closest functional match and SHOULD accompany the documentation comment. Severity remains LOW.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java QxpsHttpClient.init() (51-56) builds reactor-netty client with only timeouts -> HTTP/1.1, keep-alive, chunked allowed. .NET QXPS_Call.SDKCall() line 78 pins HttpVersion.Version10 and WriteMultipart sets a fixed ContentLength (154). Real divergence. The finding's proposed fix is "document, no change," which does not make Java behave identically to .NET, so fixCorrect=false. True 1.0 cannot be forced in reactor-netty, but keepAlive(false) + explicit Content-Length are the realistic parity-hardening steps and should be applied alongside the comment. Impact is benign on any modern QXPS deployment (no observed failure), so LOW is correct; not WITHDRAWN because the behavioral difference genuinely exists.

### [#65] Missing run-error registration when a bloc has no layout/spread (bloc silently dropped)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/modifier/QxpsModifier.java:180-185 (addGetSpread)`
- **Area:** qxps-messages (infra/interop/qxps/message + domain/modifier QxpsModifier/ModifierSpread)
- **Scenario:** A modify step processes a conditional bloc (CondName set) while the run gabarit's full XML is not yet loaded — getQxpXml() returns QxpXml.EMPTY, getLayoutName() returns "" (QxpXml.java:342-345), so addGetLayout returns null and addGetSpread returns null. The bloc is dropped from the modify project with only a log.warn; the modify XML sent to QuarkXPress is missing that bloc and the run completes with WRONG output and no recorded error.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsModifier.java:180-185: `private ModifierSpread addGetSpread(BlocBase bloc) { ModifierLayout layout = addGetLayout(bloc); if (layout == null) { log.warn("No layout found for bloc [{}]", bloc.getName()); return null; } }` — only logs, no error registration.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Modifier/QXPS_Modifier.cs:406-410` — if(QXPFrk.Validation.IsNull(__layout)) { bloc.Task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.Empty_Layout_Or_Spread_For_Bloc, bloc.Name); return null; }
- **Fix:** In addGetSpread, before returning null when layout==null, register an UNSPECIFIED run error to match .NET parity (audit-trail record only — do NOT change run status, since .NET does not fail the run for this):

private ModifierSpread addGetSpread(BlocBase bloc) {
    ModifierLayout layout = addGetLayout(bloc);
    if (layout == null) {
        bloc.getTask().getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,
                "Empty_Layout_Or_Spread_For_Bloc: " + bloc.getName()));
        log.warn("No layout found for bloc [{}]", bloc.getName());
        return null;
    }
    ...
}

This mirrors the exact pattern already used across the Java port (e.g. ProcessTasksServiceImpl, DocumentTaskProcessStrategy) and the .NET AddGetSpread. Guard bloc.getTask()/getRun() only if those can be null in this context (they are non-null on the modify path, matching .NET which dereferences them unconditionally). Do not promote to CRITIQUE/BLOQUANTE and do not set run status to Error — that would diverge from .NET, where this is an Unspecified, non-status-affecting reporting entry.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java addGetSpread (QxpsModifier.java:182-185) returns null with only log.warn; .NET AddGetSpread (QXPS_Modifier.cs:408) adds a run error before returning null. The Errors.Add(string,...) overload (Errors.cs:98) builds Error via Error(string,...) which delegates to Error_Type.Unspecified (Error.cs:56), and adding to the list does NOT change run status — Run_Base.Launch sets Generated on success (line 126) and Error only in catch blocks (lines 134,141); sibling TaskSansBloc (line 479) uses the same non-status-affecting Unspecified add. So the only real gap is the missing audit-trail entry; run outcome is identical. The proposed fix (RunError.UNSPECIFIED, getRun().getErrors().add, no status change) is the established idiom (List<RunError> getErrors, Run.java:54) and compiles. Exact message-constant text not load-bearing. Severity correctly LOW.

### [#66] userName/userPassword never set on context — serialized as null/nil instead of empty string  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxpsm/QxpsmSoapClient.java:86-96 (QRequestContext build)`
- **Area:** qxpsm-soap
- **Scenario:** Every SOAP processRequest call to a QXPSM server that distinguishes a missing element from an empty string.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsmSoapClient.java:86-96 builds the context: setDocumentName, setRequest, setResponseAsURL(false), setUseCache(false), setBypassFileInfo(false), setMaxRetries(...), setRequestTimeout(...) — no setUserName/setUserPassword call. QRequestContext.java:31-33 `private java.lang.String userName; private java.lang.String userPassword;` (default null); :450 `elemField.setNillable(true)` for userName, :455 nillable(true) for userPassword. Setters exist at :281 setUserName / :301 setUserPassword.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXPSM_Call.cs:76-77 (InitContext); QXPS_Call_Info.cs:26-27` — QXPSM_Call.cs:76 `_sdk_context.userName = _call_Info.UserName;` and :77 `_sdk_context.userPassword = _call_Info.UserPassword;` (both unconditional inside InitContext). QXPS_Call_Info.cs:26 `string _userName = string.Empty;` and :27 `string _userPassword = string.Empty;`
- **Fix:** In QxpsmSoapClient.executeStep, after building the context (around line 88-92), add the two empty-string defaults to mirror .NET's string.Empty credentials exactly:
    context.setUserName("");
    context.setUserPassword("");
This makes Axis emit `<userName></userName>` / `<userPassword></userPassword>` matching the .NET wire format. Trivial and risk-free; apply for exact parity even though behavior is unlikely to change.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple. Java omits setUserName/setUserPassword so the nillable String fields stay null and Axis emits xsi:nil/absent elements; .NET InitContext unconditionally assigns both from _call_Info, which defaults to string.Empty, so .NET always sends empty-element credentials. Genuine wire-format divergence. The proposed fix (context.setUserName(""); context.setUserPassword("");) compiles — setters exist taking java.lang.String — and produces empty-string elements matching .NET's string.Empty. SOUND. Severity LOW is right: .NET source comments mark these "pas utiliser dans notre cas" (unused) and the schema marks both nillable/optional, so a server distinguishing empty vs nil for unused credentials is highly unlikely; this is a faithful-parity nicety, not a functional break.

### [#67] getProject does not set the document to mode-degrade on DOM-fetch failure, no already-degraded short-circuit, and no caching  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `business/GetDocumentProjectBusiness.java:31-39`
- **Area:** qxpsm-soap
- **Scenario:** Compartiment incorporation: getXPressDOM fails for a child run's pooled QXP (server error/empty), or the same document's project is requested again.
- **Java is (verified):** GetDocumentProjectBusiness.java:31-38: `public QxpProject getProject(String documentName) { try { Project project = qxpsmSoapClient.getProject(documentName); return project != null ? new QxpProject(project) : QxpProject.EMPTY; } catch (Exception e) { log.error(...); return QxpProject.EMPTY; } }`. LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:204-208: `private void loadQxpSource(DocumentDomain doc, boolean conserverStyle){ doc.initXmlFromContent(...); if (conserverStyle){ doc.setQxpProject(getDocumentProjectBusiness.getProject(doc.getFilePoolPath())); } }` — never calls doc.setModeDegrade(true) on failure.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:389-412` — `if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXP.Framework.Validation.IsNull(_project)) { if (!this.Mode_Degrade) { try { QXPSMSDK.Project __qxp_Project = QXPS_File_Manager.Get_Project(this); _project = new QXP_Project(__qxp_Project); } catch { _project = QXP_Project.Empty; _mode_degrade = true; } } else { _project = QXP_Project.Empty; } } return _project;` Run_Base.cs:919-924 propagates: `return ((IsNotNull(this.Gabarit) && this.Gabarit.Mode_Degrade) || (IsNotNull(this.Gabarit_Template) && this.Gabarit_Template.Mode_Degrade));`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The bug (no mode-degrade flip on DOM-fetch failure) is real, and locating the flip at the caller (not the stateless bridge) is correct. But the proposed snippet degrades on QxpProject.EMPTY, which in the current Java conflates two distinct cases: getProject returns EMPTY on BOTH an exception AND a non-throwing null server response, whereas .NET sets _mode_degrade=true ONLY in the catch block (a non-throwing null builds new QXP_Project(null), NOT Empty, and does NOT degrade). To match .NET exactly, the degrade must be exception-driven. Refactor so the bridge signals failure vs empty distinctly. Minimal faithful version: in GetDocumentProjectBusiness.getProject, on the catch path return a distinct failure sentinel (or rethrow), and in LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.loadQxpSource catch that failure and call doc.setModeDegrade(true). Concretely, change loadQxpSource to: `if (conserverStyle) { try { Project p = qxpsmSoapClient.getProject(doc.getFilePoolPath()); doc.setQxpProject(p != null ? new QxpProject(p) : QxpProject.EMPTY); } catch (Exception e) { doc.setQxpProject(QxpProject.EMPTY); doc.setModeDegrade(true); } }` — i.e. flip mode-degrade only on the thrown-exception path, mirroring .NET's catch. Apply this inside loadQxpSource (shared by both the FILE_QXP_DATA and Task_QXP_Previous callers) rather than only the FILE_QXP_DATA branch as the finding's snippet shows. Leave CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy:332-349 unchanged (it already guards EMPTY via EmptyRunChildProject). No caching change needed (already cached via doc.setQxpProject + downstream doc.getQxpProject() reads).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java: GetDocumentProjectBusiness.java:31-38 returns EMPTY on null OR exception with no degrade; LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:204-208 stores it without flipping degrade. .NET Document.cs:399-403 sets _mode_degrade=true only in catch, propagating via Run_Base.cs:919-924 to fail-soft all tasks. Caching/short-circuit claims are correctly withdrawn by the verifyNote (cache relocated to doc.getQxpProject(); bridge called once per doc). The one real gap is the degrade flip. The proposed fix is directionally right (caller, not bridge) but degrades on EMPTY, over-degrading vs .NET which degrades only on exception (a non-throwing null does NOT degrade in .NET). adjustedFix moves the flip to the exception path inside the shared loadQxpSource. Severity LOW: error/edge path only, no crash; differs only in error category and run-wide degrade flag.

### [#68] Configured connectionTimeout, retryBackoffInterval and namespace are never applied by the client  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/interop/qxpsm/QxpsmProperties.java:18-22`
- **Area:** qxpsm-soap
- **Scenario:** Operators tuning qxpsm.soap.connection-timeout / retry-backoff-interval / namespace expect them to take effect.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpsmProperties.java:18 `private String namespace = "http://webservice.manager.quark.com";`, :19 `private int connectionTimeout = 10000;`, :22 `private int retryBackoffInterval = 1000;`. QxpsmSoapClient.java only reads getEndpoint() (:54,:127), getMaxRetries() (:93), getTimeout() (:95); processRequest called once (:102) with no retry loop. application.yaml:64 `connection-timeout: 10000`, :67 `retry-backoff-interval: 1000`, :63 `namespace: ...`. grep confirms namespace/connectionTimeout getter hits are only in generated stubs (RequestParameters.java, HTTPPreferences.java), not property consumers.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Interop/QuarkServer/BusinessObject/QXPS_SDK/QXPS_Call_Info.cs:29-30; QXPSM_Call.cs:79-91,130` — QXPS_Call_Info.cs:29 `int _maxRetries;`, :30 `int _requestTimeout;` (the ONLY two tuning fields; no ConnectionTimeout/RetryBackoff/Namespace). QXPSM_Call.cs:80 `_sdk_context.maxRetries = _call_Info.MaxRetries;`, :84 `_sdk_context.requestTimeout = ... _call_Info.RequestTimeout;`, :89-90 else `requestTimeout = 3600*1000; _sdk_service.Timeout = Timeout.Infinite;`, :130 `_sdk_response = _sdk_service.processRequest(_sdk_context);` — single call, no retry-backoff loop, no connection-timeout, no namespace knob.
- **Fix:** Pick one of two clean resolutions. (A) Remove the unused keys: delete namespace, connectionTimeout, and retryBackoffInterval from QxpsmProperties and the corresponding entries in application.yaml so the surface matches what is honored. This is the lowest-risk fix since namespace is already fixed by the WSDL/generated locator and there is no retry-with-backoff loop to feed. (B) If operators truly need them: wire connectionTimeout via the Axis stub by casting to org.apache.axis.client.Stub and calling setTimeout(...) / setProperty for connection timeout, and implement an actual retry loop around stub.processRequest(...) that sleeps retryBackoffInterval ms between up to maxRetries attempts (currently maxRetries is only passed into QRequestContext, not enforced client-side). Leave namespace removed regardless, since it duplicates the generated binding. Given LOW severity and zero functional impact, option (A) is recommended.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified triple. Java defines/configures three properties (namespace, connectionTimeout, retryBackoffInterval) that QxpsmSoapClient never reads — confirmed real dead/misleading config. .NET QXPS_Call_Info has ONLY MaxRetries and RequestTimeout; QXPSM_Call calls processRequest exactly once with no retry-backoff loop, no separate connection timeout, and namespace fixed by the generated WSDL binding. So .NET has no counterpart to the three Java knobs — the Java port already mirrors .NET runtime behavior; this is pure hygiene, no functional/parity impact, LOW is correct. The recommended fix (option A: delete the three unused keys from QxpsmProperties and application.yaml) is correct and aligns with .NET. Caveat: the finding's option B (add a retry-backoff loop + connection timeout) would INTRODUCE behavior .NET does not have and would diverge from parity; only option A should be applied. Since the finding recommends A, the resolution is sound.

### [#69] Missing destinationBlocName does not register a run Error (only logs)  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `service/task/impl/SystemTaskProcessStrategy.java:29-31`
- **Area:** system-task
- **Scenario:** A TaskSystem (or TaskDid via post-process) reaches process() with a null/blank destinationBlocName.
- **Java is (verified):** SystemTaskProcessStrategy.java:29-32:
        if (task.getDestinationBlocName() == null || task.getDestinationBlocName().isBlank()) {
            log.warn("Missing destinationBlocName for task [{}], skipping", task.getId());
            return;
        }
(no task.getRun().getErrors().add(...) call)
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Business/Task/Process_System.cs:44-48; Errors.cs:98-110; Error.cs:56` — Process_System.cs:44-48: if (QXPFrk.Validation.IsNotSet(task.DestinationBlocName)) { task.Run.Errors.Add(Constantes.Error_Messages.MissingBlocNameInTask, task.Debug_Info); return; }
Errors.cs:98 public void Add(string message, params object[] args) { ... new Error(message, args) ... }
Error.cs:56 public Error(string message, params object[] args): this(Error_Type.Unspecified, message, args){}
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** In SystemTaskProcessStrategy.process(), the missing/blank branch should record the run error with Error_Type.Unspecified (RunError.UNSPECIFIED=1), matching .NET's Add(string,params)->Error(string,args)->Unspecified resolution. But the proposed fix references ErrorMessages.MISSING_BLOC_NAME_IN_TASK and adds an import for it; NO such class/constant (and no Constantes/ErrorMessages class) exists in the Java codebase, so it would not compile. Inline a literal message instead:

    if (task.getDestinationBlocName() == null || task.getDestinationBlocName().isBlank()) {
        log.warn("Missing destinationBlocName for task [{}], skipping", task.getId());
        task.getRun().getErrors().add(new RunError(RunError.UNSPECIFIED,
                "Le nom du bloc de destination n'est pas renseigne dans la tache " + task.getDebugInfo()));
        return;
    }

Add import com.socgen.sgs.api.quark.engine.domain.RunError. task.getRun() and task.getDebugInfo() exist on TaskBase; RunError(int,String) ctor exists. Note: .NET passes Debug_Info as a string.Format arg, so it substitutes a {0} placeholder in the (unavailable in snapshot) MissingBlocNameInTask template rather than being appended; appending is a faithful approximation given the constant text is absent from the source snapshot. If/when a shared error-message constant is introduced, replace the literal with it.
- **Reconciler note:** Bug is real and reachable: Java only log.warn+return (SystemTaskProcessStrategy.java:29-32); .NET adds task.Run.Errors with the missing-bloc message (Process_System.cs:46). Overload/ctor chain confirms Error_Type.Unspecified (=1), so the verifyNote's correction to RunError.UNSPECIFIED (not CRITIQUE/BLOQUANTE) is right. Reachable directly for TaskSystem and via DidTaskPostProcessStrategy.java:110. Run.errors (Run.java:54) feeds reporting, so .NET records an auditable error Java drops; no output difference (no bloc created either way), hence LOW. Fix marked FIX_ADJUSTED because it cites a nonexistent ErrorMessages.MISSING_BLOC_NAME_IN_TASK constant/import that would not compile; must inline a literal message. Category/method/severity otherwise correct.

### [#70] Date/DateTime patterns hardcoded instead of run-configurable  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DataTypeHelper.java:17-18`
- **Area:** system-task
- **Scenario:** VALUE mode DATE/DATE_TIME where the deployment overrides Run_DatePattern/Run_DateTimePattern in config.
- **Java is (verified):** DataTypeHelper.java:17-18: `private static final DateTimeFormatter DATE_FMT = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("dd/MM/yyyy");` / `private static final DateTimeFormatter DATETIME_FMT = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss");`. Used unconditionally: line 86 `return date.format(DATE_FMT);`, line 95 `return dt.format(DATETIME_FMT);`. Grep for DatePattern/DateTimePattern config keys in the Java src returns nothing.
- **.NET is (verified):** `Data_Type_Helper.cs:517,532; Run_Properties.cs:73-74; EngineCoreSetting.cs:139-156` — Data_Type_Helper.cs:517 `string _str = __date.ToString(_run_Properties.DatePattern);`; :532 `string _str = __date.ToString(_run_Properties.DateTimePattern);`. Run_Properties.cs:73-74 `_datePattern = EngineCoreSetting.Current.Run_DatePattern;` / `_dateTimePattern = EngineCoreSetting.Current.Run_DateTimePattern;`. EngineCoreSetting.cs:143 `return this.GetString(RUN_DATEPATTERN_KEY, "dd/MM/yyyy");`; :154 `return this.GetString(RUN_DATETIMEPATTERN_KEY, "dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss");`.
- **Fix:** Restore run-configurability to match .NET. Add Run_DatePattern / Run_DateTimePattern keys to the Java EngineCoreSetting/config (defaults "dd/MM/yyyy" and "dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss" so existing deployments are unchanged), thread them through the run properties object that the SQL task processing already has, and have DataTypeHelper.formatDate/formatDateTime build their DateTimeFormatter from those configured patterns instead of the static DATE_FMT/DATETIME_FMT constants. If run properties are not readily threadable to this static helper, at minimum read the two keys from config to construct the formatters so a non-default deployment override is honored. Keep the existing fallback-to-raw-on-parse-failure behavior. Low priority since all shipped configs use the hardcoded defaults.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java hardcodes the two patterns (DataTypeHelper.java:17-18, used at 86/95) with no DatePattern config keys anywhere in src. .NET derives them from Run_Properties -> EngineCoreSetting config keys (Run_DatePattern/Run_DateTimePattern) whose defaults exactly equal the Java hardcoded values, so output is identical for every shipped config and diverges only when an operator overrides the keys. Real lost-configurability parity gap; LOW is correct. The proposed fix (add the two config keys with the same defaults and build the formatters from them, keeping the parse-failure fallback) restores .NET behavior; it provides an implementable fallback of reading config directly since the helper is static. Minor caveat: .NET vs Java pattern token semantics differ for exotic tokens, but for the defaults and ordinary dd/MM/yyyy-style overrides parity holds. Marked SOUND.

### [#71] Due-date field emitted empty when dateEcheance is null, where .NET always emits a date string  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/DocumentIdentityHelper.java:50-51`
- **Area:** did-task
- **Scenario:** A run whose RunProperties.dateEcheance is null produces an identity with an empty Due_Date field (||). In .NET Date_Echeance is a non-nullable DateTime, so Get_New_Identity always emits a date (DateTime.MinValue -> '01/01/0001 00:00:00' at worst), never an empty field.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentIdentityHelper.java:50-51: joiner.add(props.getDateEcheance() != null ? props.getDateEcheance().atStartOfDay().format(DATE_TIME_FORMATTER) : ""); | RunProperties.java:30: private LocalDate dateEcheance; | RunPropertiesMapper.java:41-42: if (dateEcheance != null) { props.setDateEcheance(dateEcheance.toLocalDate());
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/Document_Identity_Helper.cs:56; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Run_Properties.cs:217; QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:51-54` — Document_Identity_Helper.cs:56: __values.Add(QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToString(run.Properties.Date_Echeance)); | Run_Properties.cs:217: public DateTime Date_Echeance | ConversionInvariante.cs:53: return Convert.ToString(value, InvariantCulture);
- **Fix:** For byte-exact .NET fidelity, emit a canonical date for the null case instead of "". Replace the conditional with an unconditional date render that substitutes the min-date when null, matching .NET's DateTime.MinValue behavior: joiner.add((props.getDateEcheance() != null ? props.getDateEcheance() : LocalDate.of(1,1,1)).atStartOfDay().format(DATE_TIME_FORMATTER)); This produces "01/01/0001 00:00:00" exactly like .NET's default DateTime. If positional round-trip is the only contract that matters and byte-identity is not required, instead document the intentional divergence in the method Javadoc. Given LOW severity and that downstream parsing is positional, the documented-divergence option is acceptable, but the min-date substitution is the safer parity choice.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java emits an empty 5th DID field when dateEcheance is null (nullable LocalDate; mapper leaves it null when DB column is null). .NET Date_Echeance is a non-nullable DateTime value type, and Document_Identity_Helper.cs:56 unconditionally renders it via ConversionInvariante.ToString -> Convert.ToString(value, InvariantCulture), which for a DateTime always yields a non-empty invariant-culture string (MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss), worst case DateTime.MinValue -> "01/01/0001 00:00:00". So a NULL-due-date run produces a non-byte-identical DID (...||...). The proposed fix substitutes LocalDate.of(1,1,1).atStartOfDay() and formats with the existing DATE_TIME_FORMATTER, producing "01/01/0001 00:00:00" — exactly matching .NET. Compiles cleanly and uses existing imports/formatter. Impact is cosmetic (positional split still yields 7 fields), so LOW is correct.

### [#72] Default previous report type loses any DB id_type_rapport value not in TypeRapportEnum (and rejects negative explicit values)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:164-181 (resolvePreviousTypeRapport, default at 165)`
- **Area:** load-documents
- **Scenario:** TaskQxpPrevious with previousTypeRapport null/blank/"same" while the run's id_type_rapport is a DB code outside {0..5}; or an explicit negative numeric previousTypeRapport string.
- **Java is (verified):** LoadTaskDocumentsBusiness.java:165 `int type = props.getTypeRapport() != null ? props.getTypeRapport().getCode() : 0;`; line 172 `if (parsed > 0) { type = parsed; }`. RunPropertiesMapper.java:35 `props.setTypeRapport(rs.wasNull() ? TypeRapportEnum.UNKNOWN : TypeRapportEnum.fromCode(intVal));`. TypeRapportEnum.java:67 `return UNKNOWN;` (for out-of-range codes). RunProperties.java:24 stores only `private TypeRapportEnum typeRapport` (no raw int).
- **.NET is (verified):** `Task_QXP_Previous.cs:58,69-72; Validation.cs:67-77; Run_Properties.cs:148-159` — Task_QXP_Previous.cs:58 `int __previousTypeRapport = this.Run.Properties.ID_Type_Rapport;` (raw int default). Lines 69-72 `int __parsingResult = QXPFrk.Conversion.ToInt(this.Previous_Type_Rapport, 0); if (QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(__parsingResult, false)) { __previousTypeRapport = __parsingResult; }`. Validation.cs:75 `return !(val == int.MinValue || val == 0);` (accepts negatives). Run_Properties.cs:152 getter `return _id_Type_Rapport;` is a plain int, distinct from the Type_Rapport enum getter at 164.
- **Fix:** Two-part fix to restore .NET parity:

1) Preserve the raw int. Add a field to RunProperties, e.g. `private int idTypeRapportRaw;`, and in RunPropertiesMapper set it from the same `rs.getInt("id_type_rapport")` (use the raw value even when not null; when null .NET would have its int default — keep current UNKNOWN/0 behavior). Then in resolvePreviousTypeRapport use `int type = props.getIdTypeRapportRaw();` as the default instead of `props.getTypeRapport().getCode()`.

2) Match IsSet(parsed,false): change the explicit-parse guard from `if (parsed > 0)` to `if (parsed != 0)` (Integer.MIN_VALUE cannot occur here because Integer.parseInt throws on overflow and on the unparseable case we already fall into the catch block, so `!= 0` is sufficient). This lets negative explicit values through as .NET does.

If carrying the raw int is deemed not worth it, at minimum apply part (2) (`parsed != 0`), since that is a localized one-line change; but part (1) is required for full parity on out-of-range DB codes.
- **Reconciler note:** Both parity deltas verified from source. (1) Default: .NET uses the raw int Run.Properties.ID_Type_Rapport; Java derives it from the enum, which collapses any DB code outside {0..5} to UNKNOWN(0) via fromCode at mapper line 35. (2) Negatives: .NET IsSet(parsed,false) accepts negative parsed values; Java's `parsed > 0` drops them. Fix is correct: part 2 (`parsed != 0`) exactly matches IsSet semantics (MinValue is impossible since parseInt throws on overflow→catch). Part 1 (carry raw int + use it as default) restores out-of-range parity. Minor clarification: on rs.wasNull() the raw field should stay 0 to mirror .NET's int default (current null→UNKNOWN/0 behavior preserved); the finding already notes keeping UNKNOWN/0 when null, so intent is right. LOW severity correct: normal data is within {0..5} and negative explicit config is an edge input.

### [#73] Returned DocumentDomain.idLangue is left null; .NET sets Document.ID_Langue = id_langue  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/GetDocumentDaoImpl.java:86-96 (mapFromResultSet); also GetLastQxpCertifieDaoImpl.java:74-84`
- **Area:** load-documents
- **Scenario:** Any Get_Document / Get_Last_Qxp_Certifie load.
- **Java is (verified):** GetDocumentDaoImpl.java:86-95 mapFromResultSet: `DocumentDomain doc = new DocumentDomain();` then setId/setName/setFormat/setPrefix/setData/setFileName — no setIdLangue. Same in GetLastQxpCertifieDaoImpl.java:74-83. The Lombok @NoArgsConstructor (DocumentDomain.java:24) leaves `private Integer idLangue;` (line 46) null (only the 5-arg ctor sets `this.idLangue = 1;` at line 87).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Document.cs:115 and :211; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:24` — Proxy_Document.cs:115 `__document.ID_Langue = id_langue;` (Get_Document 6-arg); :211 `__document.ID_Langue = id_langue;` (Get_Last_Qxp_Certifie 3-arg). Document.cs:24 `int _id_langue = 1;` so .NET ID_Langue is never null (defaults to 1, then set to the param).
- **Fix:** In GetDocumentDaoImpl: the langue is available as a method param. Thread it into the mapper and call doc.setIdLangue(idLangue) — e.g. change the SqlOutParameter row mapper to a closure capturing idLangue, or set it in getDocument() on rows.get(0) before returning, matching .NET line 115. For GetLastQxpCertifieDaoImpl: the Java DAO interface only receives idSuivi/idTypeRapport (no langue, unlike .NET's 3-arg overload), so either (a) read it from the cursor if the column exists, or (b) call doc.setIdLangue(1) to match the DocumentDomain default and avoid a null. Given zero functional impact today, treat as low-priority consistency cleanup rather than a required fix.
- **Reconciler note:** Real parity gap confirmed: Java leaves idLangue null; .NET defaults to 1 and overwrites with the id_langue param. For GetDocumentDaoImpl the fix is exact — idLangue is a method param (ora.txt:8894-8902 cursor has no id_langue column, so the param is the only source), so capture it in the row mapper / set doc.setIdLangue(idLangue) on the returned row, matching Proxy_Document.cs:115. For GetLastQxpCertifie the SQL cursor (ora.txt:8987-9012) exposes only ID_DOCUMENT, CONTENU, NOM, FORMAT — NO id_langue — so the fix's option (a) "read from cursor" is infeasible; the correct path is option (b) setIdLangue(1), matching the .NET Document default (the 3-arg overload that sets the param is dead; live SQL pkg is 2-arg). Fix already offers (b), so it is adequate. Impact LOW/nil: InsertDocumentDaoImpl:60 null-defaults to 1 and no consumer reads doc.getIdLangue() on these loaded docs (DocumentIdentityService uses a separate idLangue). Optional hardening; sibling DAOs (GetGabaritDaoImpl:88, GetDocumentByIdDaoImpl:76) already set it.

### [#74] newBlocName() omits the surrounding square brackets that .NET's HookString applies  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/TElementHelper.java:249-252`
- **Area:** telement-helper
- **Scenario:** Any non-data box inside a group (boxType != CT_NONE, name not starting with C_, or extra boxes beyond the name count) is passed to renameBloc, which assigns newBlocName().
- **Java is (verified):** TElementHelper.java:249-252: public static String newBlocName() { String uuid = UUID.randomUUID().toString().replace("-", ""); return uuid.length() > NEW_BLOC_NAME_SIZE ? uuid.substring(0, NEW_BLOC_NAME_SIZE) : uuid; } — and line 56: private static final int NEW_BLOC_NAME_SIZE = 29; (returns bare 29-char hex, no brackets)
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/TElement_Helper.cs:430-433, :26, :28; QXP.Framework/Core/StringHelper.cs:158-161; QXP.Framework/Core/StringHelper_.cs:14` — TElement_Helper.cs:432 `return QXPFrk.StringHelper.HookString(QXPFrk.StringHelper.Left(string.Format(NewBlocPattern, Guid.NewGuid()), NewBlocNameSize));` with :26 `const string NewBlocPattern = "{0:N}";` and :28 `const int NewBlocNameSize = 29;`. StringHelper.cs:160 `return string.Format(StringFormat.HookFormat, param1);`. StringHelper_.cs:14 `public const string HookFormat = "[{0}]";`
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Apply the bracketed form so the name byte-matches .NET. Replace line 251 with: `String body = uuid.length() > NEW_BLOC_NAME_SIZE ? uuid.substring(0, NEW_BLOC_NAME_SIZE) : uuid; return "[" + body + "]";`. Both runtimes produce exactly 32 lowercase hex chars before truncation ({0:N} GUID vs UUID.replace("-","")), so the body is always 29 lowercase hex chars and the wrapped result is the 31-char "[<29 hex>]", identical to .NET's HookString(Left(...,29)).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple fully verified from real source bodies (not just convention): .NET NewBlocName wraps a 29-char truncated GUID-N in HookFormat "[{0}]" yielding a 31-char "[...]" string; Java returns the bare 29-char hex. Divergence is real but cosmetic — names are throwaway unique IDs, never re-parsed by getIndexBox (handles only C_X_) nor matched by DATA_BLOC_PREFIX; uniqueness (provided by both) is all that matters. Severity LOW is correct. Marked FIX_ADJUSTED because the finding's "fix" is ambivalent and explicitly recommends leaving Java as-is, which does NOT achieve the byte-for-byte .NET parity that is the goal here; the adjustedFix mandates the bracketed form, which is the only variant that makes Java identical to .NET. The bracketed code compiles and is correct.

### [#75] updatePosition(TGroup) returns ZERO for an unparseable position string where .NET returns decimal.MinValue (different shifted result)  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/helper/TElementHelper.java:406-421`
- **Area:** telement-helper
- **Scenario:** A group box whose geometry.position.left/top/right/bottom is empty/blank/non-numeric (malformed template), then shifted by relativeLeft/relativeTop.
- **Java is (verified):** TElementHelper.java:418-421 p.setLeft(parseDecimal(p.getLeft()).add(relativeLeft).toPlainString()); ... and TElementHelper.java:512-521 private static BigDecimal parseDecimal(String value){ if(value==null||value.isBlank()) return BigDecimal.ZERO; try{return new BigDecimal(value.trim());}catch(NumberFormatException e){return BigDecimal.ZERO;}}
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Helper/TElement_Helper.cs:295-299; QXP.Framework/Core/ConversionInvariante.cs:216-229` — ConversionInvariante.cs: public static decimal ToDecimal(string value){ decimal __defaultValue = decimal.MinValue; ... if(Validation.IsSet(value) && Decimal.TryParse(...)) return __newDecimalValue; else return __defaultValue; } | TElement_Helper.cs:295 __box.geometry.position.left = ToString(ToDecimal(__box.geometry.position.left) + __relative_left);
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** For strict .NET parity, make parseDecimal mirror ConversionInvariante.ToDecimal: return decimal.MinValue (new BigDecimal("-79228162514264337593543950335")) instead of BigDecimal.ZERO on null/blank/parse failure. parseDecimal is used ONLY by updatePosition's four ToDecimal-equivalent calls, so this is a safe, scoped change: private static final BigDecimal DECIMAL_MIN_VALUE = new BigDecimal("-79228162514264337593543950335"); ... if(value==null||value.isBlank()) return DECIMAL_MIN_VALUE; try{return new BigDecimal(value.trim());}catch(NumberFormatException e){return DECIMAL_MIN_VALUE;} Note: the finding's own 'fix' text recommends NOT doing this (keep ZERO or throw IllegalStateException), which directly contradicts the 100%-parity mandate; the throw alternative would also diverge (.NET emits geometry, never throws on numeric parse failure).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java parseDecimal returns ZERO; .NET ToDecimal returns decimal.MinValue; both feed the relative shift in Update_Position(TGroup). Divergence is real but only reachable with non-numeric/blank position strings, which valid templates never produce; both outcomes are garbage-in-garbage-out, so LOW is correct. Status is FIX_ADJUSTED, not SOUND: the proposed 'fix' explicitly declines to match .NET (it keeps ZERO or throws), so applying it would NOT yield parity. Given the stated goal of 100% functional parity, the correct fix is to return decimal.MinValue; parseDecimal has a single caller scope (updatePosition), making the change safe.

### [#76] BlocPage.getNbBox() REMOVE path swallows all exceptions and returns 1, silently corrupting box-capacity accounting  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/bloc/BlocPage.java:44-58`
- **Area:** bloc-model (domain/bloc: BlocBase, BlocBox, BlocTable, BlocPage, BlocLigne, BlocGroup)
- **Scenario:** A page-REMOVE bloc is evaluated in RunTaskStep.evaluateLimitation() (getNbBox called at RunTaskStep.java:127) while the run gabarit's QxpXml/ProjectInfo chain is not yet fully loaded (the documented 'gabarit XML may not be loaded before Process' situation). getTask().getRun().getGabarit().getQxpXml().getProjectInfo() NPEs, the catch(Exception) fires, and the method returns super.getNbBox()=1.
- **Java is (verified):** BlocPage.java:46-55: `if (getAction() == BlocActionEnum.REMOVE) { try { return getTask().getRun().getGabarit().getQxpXml().getProjectInfo().getNbBoxOnPage(getPageId()); } catch (Exception e) { return super.getNbBox(); } }`. super.getNbBox() resolves to BlocBase.java:39-41 `public int getNbBox() { return 1; }`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Bloc/Bloc_Page.cs:132-146; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Dynamique/Report/DProject_Info.cs:54-64` — Bloc_Page.cs:136-143: `if (this.Action == Bloc_Action.REMOVE) { return this.Task.Run.Gabarit.XML.Project_Info.GetNbBoxOnPage(this.Page_ID); } else { return base.Nb_Box; }` — NO try/catch. DProject_Info.cs:56-63: `if (_pageBoxes.ContainsKey(page)) { return _pageBoxes[page]; } else { return 0; }` — never throws (doc says "1" but code returns 0).
- **Fix:** Remove the try/catch so behavior mirrors .NET. Since getProjectInfo() is lazy-non-null and getNbBoxOnPage already returns 0 for a missing page (parity-faithful), simply return the chain directly: if (getAction()==REMOVE) return getTask().getRun().getGabarit().getQxpXml().getProjectInfo().getNbBoxOnPage(getPageId()); else return super.getNbBox(). If defensiveness is desired for the gabarit/qxpXml-null window, add explicit null guards that surface the precondition error (e.g. throw IllegalStateException) rather than masking it as 1 — but do NOT default to 1, since that silently miscounts REMOVE of a content-bearing page. The 0-for-missing-page path is already correct and needs no change.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java DProjectInfo.getNbBoxOnPage uses getOrDefault(page,0) (DProjectInfo.java:44) on a non-null HashMap, and getProjectInfo() (QxpXml.java:439-445) is lazy and never returns null — so for a genuinely-missing page Java already returns 0, matching .NET exactly in the normal XML-loaded flow (including the 0 case). The sole divergence is the catch-and-return-1 in the abnormal window where gabarit/qxpXml are null. The caller RunTaskStep.evaluateLimitation (line 121 guard) only protects the nbBoxDoc assignment at 122; the modify-bloc loop at 126-127 calls bloc.getNbBox() unguarded, so the NPE-then-catch path is reachable when gabarit/qxpXml are null. There .NET propagates the NPE while Java silently returns 1 — a real but very narrow masking divergence, correctly rated LOW (finding already downgraded from MEDIUM). The proposed fix (remove try/catch to mirror .NET, or null-guard that throws rather than defaulting to 1) makes Java throw exactly where .NET throws and return identical counts otherwise: it achieves parity. Fix is SOUND; option-1 (drop try/catch) is the cleanest parity-faithful form.

### [#77] Pastboard / page-0 boxes excluded from pageBoxes and nbBox (currentPage>0 vs .NET IsSet)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/xml/QxpXml.java:472-499`
- **Area:** xml-xpath (domain/xml/QxpXml.java)
- **Scenario:** A box or table whose GEOMETRY/@PAGE is 0 (pastboard) or non-numeric. .NET ToInt returns int.MinValue on non-numeric and the real value (incl. 0/negative) otherwise; IsSet(currentPage) with isZeroValid=true is `val != int.MinValue`, so .NET DOES record page 0 boxes in PageBoxes and counts them in NbBox (for tables). Java's parseIntSafe returns 0 on failure and guards with `currentPage > 0`, so page-0 boxes are dropped from pageBoxes (box loop) and from both pageBoxes AND nbBox (table loop).
- **Java is (verified):** QxpXml.java:475-479 box loop: `int currentPage = parseIntSafe(pageStr); if (currentPage > 0) { info.getPageBoxes().merge(currentPage, 1, Integer::sum); } totalBoxes++;` (totalBoxes unconditional). QxpXml.java:486-499 table loop: `int currentPage = parseIntSafe(pageAttr.getNodeValue()); if (currentPage > 0) { ... for cellIds: info.getPageBoxes().merge(...); totalBoxes++; }` (both inside `>0` guard). QxpXml.java:666-675 parseIntSafe returns 0 on null/blank/NumberFormatException, strips trailing `*`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP_XML.cs:403-444; Validation.cs:67-77; ConversionInvariante.cs:164-184` — QXP_XML.cs box loop L403-415: `__currentPage = ConversionInvariante.ToInt(...); if (Validation.IsSet(__currentPage)) { ...PageBoxes... } __dProjet_Info.NbBox++;` (NbBox++ outside IsSet). Table loop L428-444: `__currentPage = ToInt(...); if (IsSet(__currentPage)) { while(subIter) { ...PageBoxes...; __dProjet_Info.NbBox++; } }` (NbBox++ inside IsSet). Validation.cs L72 `return val != int.MinValue;` (isZeroValid). ConversionInvariante.cs L166 `return ToInt(value, int.MinValue);` so non-numeric->MinValue.
- **Fix:** Make parseIntSafe return a sentinel on failure rather than conflating failure with 0, then mirror .NET's per-loop placement. Add `private static final int UNSET = Integer.MIN_VALUE;` and have a parse helper return UNSET on null/blank/NumberFormatException (keep the trailing-`*` strip). Box loop: guard PageBoxes with `if (currentPage != UNSET)` and keep `totalBoxes++` UNCONDITIONAL (already correct). Table loop: guard the whole cell-counting block (both PageBoxes merge and totalBoxes++) with `if (currentPage != UNSET)`. This includes page 0 (and negatives) exactly as .NET IsSet(int) does, while still excluding non-numeric/blank PAGE, restoring full parity. Do NOT use `>= 0`, which would wrongly fold Java's 0-for-non-numeric back in and break the non-numeric exclusion. Impact is small (only page-0 tables change NbBox; the PageBoxes map is unused), so this is optional hardening rather than urgent.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. .NET: ToInt->int.MinValue on non-numeric; IsSet(isZeroValid=true)=`val!=int.MinValue` so page 0/negatives are valid. Box-loop NbBox++ unconditional (matches Java totalBoxes++); table-loop NbBox++ inside IsSet. Java's `>0` guard wrongly drops page-0/negative entries from PageBoxes (both loops) and from NbBox (table loop only). getPageBoxes() is write-only/dead (only QxpXml.java writes it), so PageBoxes divergence is inert. Only real effect: table-loop NbBox under-counts page-0 tables; getNbBox() feeds getRatioSizeBox (DocumentDomain.java:250-251). Fix (UNSET sentinel = Integer.MIN_VALUE, guard PageBoxes-in-box-loop and whole-cell-block-in-table-loop with `!=UNSET`, keep box totalBoxes++ unconditional) mirrors .NET exactly. parseIntSafe used only in these two spots so changing it is safe. Trailing-`*` strip is a separate pre-existing divergence, out of scope. LOW is correct given dead PageBoxes and rare page-0 tables.

### [#78] Page parsing rejects decimal/spaced values that .NET ConversionInvariante.ToInt accepts  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/xml/QxpXml.java:316-317,666-675`
- **Area:** xml-xpath (domain/xml/QxpXml.java)
- **Scenario:** A PAGE / pageID value like "10.0", "1 0", or a value with a thousands separator. .NET ToInt does Decimal.TryParse(value.Replace(" ",""), NumberStyles.Any, InvariantCulture) then casts to int, so "10.0"->10 and spaces are stripped. Java getPageNum/parseIntSafe call Integer.parseInt after only stripping trailing '*', so "10.0" or "1 0" throws NumberFormatException and yields 0.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpXml.java:316-317 getPageNum: `String cleaned = pageId.replaceAll("\\*+$", ""); return Integer.parseInt(cleaned);` (catch -> return 0, line 320). QxpXml.java:671-674 parseIntSafe: `String cleaned = value.replaceAll("\\*+$", "").trim(); return Integer.parseInt(cleaned);` (catch -> return 0). Call sites: line 475 `int currentPage = parseIntSafe(pageStr);` then 476 `if (currentPage > 0)`; line 486 `int currentPage = parseIntSafe(pageAttr.getNodeValue());` then 487 `if (currentPage > 0)`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `ConversionInvariante.cs:164-183; QXP_XML.cs:289,403,428; Validation.cs:67-77` — ConversionInvariante.cs:166 `return ToInt(value, int.MinValue);` and :174-177 `if (Validation.IsSet(value) && Decimal.TryParse(value.Replace(" ", ""), NumberStyles.Any, InvariantCulture.NumberFormat, out __newDecimalValue)) { int __newvalue = (Int32)__newDecimalValue; return __newvalue; }`. QXP_XML.cs:289 `return QXPFrk.ConversionInvariante.ToInt(__pageID.TrimEnd('*'));`; :403 `__currentPage = ...ToInt(...GetAttribute("PAGE",...));` :404 `if (QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(__currentPage))`; :428 `...ToInt(__iterator.Current.Value)` :429 `if (QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(__currentPage))`. Validation.cs:69-71 `if (isZeroValid) { return val != int.MinValue; }` (IsSet(int) defaults isZeroValid=true).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Add a private helper mirroring ConversionInvariante.ToInt and use it in getPageNum, and at lines 475 and 486 (replacing parseIntSafe for the PAGE-value sites). Helper: `private int toInt(String value){ if(value==null) return Integer.MIN_VALUE; String s=value.replace(" ","").replace(',','.'); if(s.isEmpty()) return Integer.MIN_VALUE; try { return new java.math.BigDecimal(s).toBigInteger().intValue(); } catch(NumberFormatException e){ return Integer.MIN_VALUE; } }`. (Strips ALL spaces like .NET, normalizes decimal comma, truncates toward zero via toBigInteger() matching the (Int32)decimal cast, and returns Integer.MIN_VALUE as the .NET default.) For getPageNum: `return toInt(pageId.replaceAll("\\*+$",""));` — but note .NET GetPageNum/ToInt does NOT trim; it uses TrimEnd('*') only, so keep the trailing-* strip. IMPORTANT — the finding's fix is incomplete: the box-counting call sites also diverge in the FILTER. .NET counts when `Validation.IsSet(currentPage)` which (isZeroValid=true) is `currentPage != int.MinValue`, i.e. it counts page 0 and NEGATIVE pages; Java uses `currentPage > 0`, which drops page 0 and negatives. To match .NET, change lines 476 and 487 from `if (currentPage > 0)` to `if (currentPage != Integer.MIN_VALUE)`. Without this filter change, parity is still broken for PAGE="0" (and negative/pasteboard) boxes regardless of the parse helper. getPageNum has no such filter so the helper alone suffices there.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java uses Integer.parseInt after only stripping trailing '*' (+trim in parseIntSafe), throwing on "10.0"/"1 0"/separators and degrading to 0. .NET ToInt uses Decimal.TryParse with space-stripping + NumberStyles.Any + (Int32) truncation, default int.MinValue. Bug is real. The proposed fix correctly handles parse leniency, but is INCOMPLETE: it ignores a second confirmed divergence at the box-count sites — .NET's Validation.IsSet(int) (isZeroValid=true) accepts page 0 and negatives (!= int.MinValue), whereas Java's `currentPage > 0` rejects them. Full parity also requires changing the `> 0` checks at lines 476/487 to `!= Integer.MIN_VALUE`. Severity LOW: real QXP @PAGE/@GEOMETRY values are plain non-negative integers, so decimal/space/page-0 inputs are rare; treat as parity hardening.

### [#79] getBlocInfo reads only direct children of POSITION; .NET reads all descendant elements  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/xml/QxpXml.java:375-399`
- **Area:** xml-xpath (domain/xml/QxpXml.java)
- **Scenario:** If a POSITION element nests LEFT/TOP/RIGHT/BOTTOM below a wrapper element (not as immediate children). .NET uses SelectDescendants(Element) (recursive over all descendant elements), Java uses positionNode.getChildNodes() (immediate children only), so deeper LEFT/TOP/RIGHT/BOTTOM would be missed and left at their DBlocInfo defaults.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpXml.java:375 `NodeList children = positionNode.getChildNodes();` then loops only over immediate children (lines 376-399), guarding `child.getNodeType() != Node.ELEMENT_NODE` and switching on LEFT_KEY/TOP_KEY/RIGHT_KEY/BOTTOM_KEY.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/QXP_XML.cs:341` — QXP_XML.cs:341 `XPathNodeIterator __iterator_childs = __iterator.Current.SelectDescendants(XPathNodeType.Element, false);` then `while(__iterator_childs.MoveNext()) switch(__iterator_childs.Current.Name){ case LeftKey ... TopKey ... RightKey ... BottomKey }` (lines 342-360).
- **Fix:** Replace the immediate-children loop with a descendant traversal to mirror SelectDescendants(Element). Simplest: change the XPath to select the descendant elements directly, e.g. query `.//*` relative to positionNode (or evaluate `string(...POSITION//LEFT)` etc.), then iterate element nodes. Concretely, keep the same switch but iterate descendant elements instead of direct children: e.g. `NodeList descendants = (NodeList) xpath.evaluate(".//*", positionNode, XPathConstants.NODESET);` and loop over that (all returned nodes are already elements, so the ELEMENT_NODE guard can stay as a no-op safeguard). This makes the four-coordinate extraction recursive and behaviorally identical to the .NET version regardless of nesting depth, while remaining a no-op for the common flat POSITION structure.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java uses getChildNodes() (immediate children only); .NET uses SelectDescendants(XPathNodeType.Element, false) — a recursive walk over all descendant elements, excluding self. PositionBlocPattern is identical (/PROJECT/LAYOUT/SPREAD/BOX[ID[@NAME='{0}']]/GEOMETRY/POSITION) in both. Constants LEFT_KEY/TOP_KEY/RIGHT_KEY/BOTTOM_KEY map to .NET LeftKey etc. Real divergence: any LEFT/TOP/RIGHT/BOTTOM nested under a wrapper below POSITION is matched by .NET, missed by Java, leaving DBlocInfo default. Proposed fix using xpath.evaluate(".//*", positionNode, NODESET) is correct: ".//*" selects all descendant elements excluding the context node, exactly mirroring SelectDescendants(Element, false). The xpath field exists in scope; ".//*" returns only element nodes so the ELEMENT_NODE guard stays as harmless safeguard; switch consumes only the four unique keys so document-order is irrelevant. LOW is right: normal QXP keeps these as direct children where both paths are equivalent; divergent nesting is plausible but unproven, and the fix is cheap/risk-free.

### [#80] evaluateNodeListAsStrings drops blank/whitespace names; .NET keeps all matches  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/xml/QxpXml.java:610`
- **Area:** xml-xpath (domain/xml/QxpXml.java)
- **Scenario:** getListBoxNameStartWith / getListBoxNameContains / getListBoxNameEndWith / getOverflowBoxes on a document where a matching @NAME is empty or whitespace. .NET's GetListBoxName* and GetOverflowBoxes add every iterator value unconditionally; Java skips entries failing `value != null && !value.isBlank()`, producing a shorter list.
- **Java is (verified):** QxpXml.java:610 `if (value != null && !value.isBlank()) {` inside evaluateNodeListAsStrings (lines 600-618); reached by getListBoxNameStartWith (239), getListBoxNameEndWith (251), getListBoxNameContains (269), getOverflowBoxes (278).
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/QXP_XML.cs:200, 220, 239, 464` — L200 `__list.Add(__iterator.Current.Value);` (StartWith); L237-239 `while(__iterator.MoveNext()){ __list.Add(__iterator.Current.Value); }` (Contains); L219-220 `if(__iterator.Current.Value.EndsWith(suffixe)) __list.Add(__iterator.Current.Value);` (EndWith, suffix-only filter); L462-464 `while(__iterator.MoveNext()){ __list.Add(__iterator.Current.Value); }` (GetOverflowBoxes). No null/blank guard anywhere.
- **Fix:** Because the same private helper is shared by callers that may legitimately want blank-filtering elsewhere in the future, prefer fixing parity at the helper by dropping only the blank check: change line 610 from `if (value != null && !value.isBlank())` to `if (value != null)` so blank/whitespace names are kept (matching .NET, which never null-checks but cannot get a null Value from an attribute iterator). This makes getListBoxNameStartWith/Contains/EndWith/getOverflowBoxes return the same counts/contents as .NET. If any non-cited future caller needs blank-filtering, do it at that call site rather than in the shared helper. Add a brief comment noting the null-only guard is intentional parity with .NET's unconditional __list.Add(__iterator.Current.Value).
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java's shared helper adds the extra `!value.isBlank()` filter that .NET never applies; .NET's four equivalents (StartWith/Contains/Overflow add every Current.Value; EndWith adds when suffix matches) keep blank/whitespace @NAME values. Java thus returns shorter lists when a matched box @NAME is empty/whitespace. The proposed fix (line 610 -> `if (value != null)`) removes the blank filter while retaining a null guard. Only the 4 cited methods call this private helper (confirmed via grep), so the change is fully scoped and produces identical counts/contents to .NET; the EndWith suffix check lives at the call site (line 254) and is unaffected. .NET attribute Current.Value is never null, so keeping the null guard is harmless parity. Compiles cleanly. Severity LOW is accurate: requires a degenerate empty/whitespace @NAME, rare in real QuarkXPress XML.

### [#81] Null element name produces a null map key instead of aborting, diverging from .NET Dictionary semantics  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `domain/project/QxpProject.java:135 / 162 / 192 (TBox/TTable/TGroup construction with null element name)`
- **Area:** qxp-project (QxpProject.analyse / Elements map building)
- **Scenario:** A SOAP Box/Table/Group is non-null but its name is null (malformed gabarit). new TBox(box) -> super(box.getName()) -> name=null, then elements.put(null, tBox).
- **Java is (verified):** QxpProject.java:85 `elements = new LinkedHashMap<>();`; :135 `TBox tBox = new TBox(box);`; :138-139 `if (!elements.containsKey(tBox.getName())) { elements.put(tBox.getName(), tBox);` (mirrored at :162-165 tables, :192-195 groups). TBox.java:51 `super(box.getName());` (no null guard). LinkedHashMap permits a null key, so a null name is stored silently.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/QXP_Project.cs:65, :86-88` — :65 `_elements = new Dictionary<string, TElement>();`  :86 `if (!_elements.ContainsKey(__tBox.Name))`  :88 `_elements.Add(__tBox.Name, __tBox);` — Dictionary<string,T>.ContainsKey(null)/Add(null,...) throws ArgumentNullException; box/table loops (86/104) have no try/catch, so it propagates out of Analyse().
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** The divergence is real (Java silently keeps a null-keyed entry; .NET throws ArgumentNullException and aborts Analyse). The proposed fix (a `continue` skip) does NOT make Java behave identically to .NET — it deliberately picks graceful-continue over .NET's full abort, so for strict parity it is incorrect. For true .NET parity, replicate the throw: in processBoxes/processTables/processGroups, before the containsKey/put, add `if (tBox.getName() == null) throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"element name is null\");` (mirrored for TTable/TGroup) so the unchecked exception propagates out of analyse() and aborts it, matching ArgumentNullException from Dictionary. NOTE: given severity is genuinely LOW (lookups are by real names; only malformed gabarits reach this; null-keyed entry is harmless to happy-path output), if the team prefers robustness over literal parity, the finding's continue-skip is an acceptable conscious deviation — but it must be flagged as a deliberate divergence, not parity. Per the 100%-parity goal, the throw variant is the correct fix.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified by re-reading source. Java: LinkedHashMap (QxpProject.java:85) + no null guard in TBox.java:51, so null names are silently stored/deduped. .NET: Dictionary (QXP_Project.cs:65) throws on null key at ContainsKey/Add (86-88, no surrounding try/catch in box/table loops), aborting Analyse. The proposed fix intentionally diverges from .NET (continue vs abort), so it does not achieve identical behavior; hence FIX_ADJUSTED with a throw-based adjustedFix that matches .NET, while noting the LOW impact makes the deviation defensible if documented. Severity LOW confirmed: real-name lookups make the stray null entry harmless; only malformed input triggers it.

### [#82] Audit duration computed in SECONDS; .NET Audit.Duration returns the milliseconds component (TimeSpan.Milliseconds)  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/AuditDaoImpl.java:51-53, 62`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** Every audit row: durationSeconds = Duration.between(start,end).getSeconds(); written to QXP_AUDIT_RUN.DURATION.
- **Java is (verified):** AuditDaoImpl.java:50-53 `// Duration in seconds between start and end (.NET audit.Duration).` / `long durationSeconds = 0L;` / `if (run.getStartDate() != null && run.getEndDate() != null) {` / `durationSeconds = Duration.between(run.getStartDate(), run.getEndDate()).getSeconds();` and line 62 `.addValue("p_duration", durationSeconds)`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Audit/Audit.cs:93-96; Proxy_Audit.cs:101; ora.txt:192,199-200` — Audit.cs:93-96 `public int Duration { get { return ((TimeSpan)(_endDate-_startDate)).Milliseconds;} }`; Proxy_Audit.cs:101 `.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_duration", audit.Duration)`; ora.txt:200 `VALUES ( ...,p_duration,... )` (stored verbatim into QXP_AUDIT_RUN.DURATION).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** Replace the seconds computation with the .NET TimeSpan.Milliseconds-component semantics for exact parity. Since startDate/endDate are LocalDateTime (Run.java:28,57) and the target is 100% .NET parity:

  int durationValue = 0;
  if (run.getStartDate() != null && run.getEndDate() != null) {
      durationValue = Duration.between(run.getStartDate(), run.getEndDate()).toMillisPart();
      // .NET parity: Audit.cs Duration getter returns ((TimeSpan)(end-start)).Milliseconds,
      // i.e. the 0..999 sub-second millisecond COMPONENT (NOT total ms, NOT seconds).
  }
  ...
  .addValue("p_duration", durationValue)

Replace getSeconds()/long with the toMillisPart() int. If the project targets Java 8 (no toMillisPart), use: (int)(Duration.between(run.getStartDate(), run.getEndDate()).toMillis() % 1000). Update the misleading line-50 comment accordingly. Do NOT use the finding's recommended option (2) (toMillis total elapsed) — that intentionally diverges from .NET and would break parity.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified in source. Java writes whole truncated seconds (getSeconds, line 53); .NET Audit.Duration returns ((TimeSpan)(end-start)).Milliseconds — the 0..999 sub-second component only (Audit.cs:95). Proxy_Audit.cs:101 passes audit.Duration verbatim as p_duration, and the proc inserts it unchanged into QXP_AUDIT_RUN.DURATION (ora.txt:200). So the persisted magnitude/unit genuinely differs every audit row (65.123s run: .NET=123, Java=65). Bug is real. The proposed fix is ambiguous (three options) and recommends option 2 (total ms) which does NOT match .NET; for the stated goal of 100% parity the correct choice is the .Milliseconds component via toMillisPart() (or toMillis()%1000 on Java 8). Hence FIX_ADJUSTED. Severity LOW confirmed: diagnostic/audit-only column, no engine-logic, scheduling, or downstream impact.

### [#83] Audit p_end_status uses Java enum name() (e.g. ERROR, TO_GENERATE) vs .NET enum names (Error, ToGenerate)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/AuditDaoImpl.java:63`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** p_end_status bound from run.getStatus().name() → 'TO_GENERATE'/'GENERATED'/'ERROR'/'RUNNING'.
- **Java is (verified):** AuditDaoImpl.java:63: `.addValue("p_end_status", run.getStatus() != null ? run.getStatus().name() : null)`. RunStatus.java:12-15: `TO_GENERATE(1), GENERATED(2), ERROR(3), RUNNING(4);` so name() yields TO_GENERATE/GENERATED/ERROR/RUNNING.
- **.NET is (verified):** `Proxy_Audit.cs:102; Run_Status.cs:15-27; Audit.cs:117-120,57` — Proxy_Audit.cs:102: `.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_end_status", audit.EndStatus.ToString())`. Audit.cs:117-120: `public Run_Status EndStatus { get{return _endStatus;} }` (set from `_run.Status`, line 57). Run_Status.cs:15-27: `ToGenerate = 1, Generated = 2, Error = 3, Running = 4,`. C# enum ToString() returns the member name → ToGenerate/Generated/Error/Running.
- **Fix:** Map RunStatus to the legacy .NET display strings instead of name(). Add a method on RunStatus returning the legacy label, e.g. add a field per constant: TO_GENERATE("ToGenerate",1), GENERATED("Generated",2), ERROR("Error",3), RUNNING("Running",4) with a getLegacyStatusName() accessor, then bind .addValue("p_end_status", run.getStatus() != null ? run.getStatus().getLegacyStatusName() : null). This reproduces .NET Run_Status.ToString() output (ToGenerate/Generated/Error/Running) and restores audit/reporting parity. Keep the existing null guard.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java binds run.getStatus().name() producing SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE (TO_GENERATE/GENERATED/ERROR/RUNNING); .NET binds Run_Status.ToString() producing PascalCase (ToGenerate/Generated/Error/Running). The InsertAuditRun proc stores p_end_status as VARCHAR2 verbatim, so the persisted literals genuinely differ in spelling/case. The proposed fix (add a String legacyStatusName field per constant with @Getter generating getLegacyStatusName(), then bind that with the null guard) reproduces exactly .NET's output and compiles cleanly — RunStatus already has @Getter, so no manual accessor is needed; the new two-arg constructor RunStatus(String,int) is consistent with existing codes 1-4. Severity LOW is correct: data-fidelity/reporting only, no control-flow impact.

### [#84] Hardcodes format 'QXP', ignoring the cursor's format column that .NET reads  — ⚠️ FIX ADJUSTED
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/GetDocumentByIdDaoImpl.java:73`
- **Area:** daos-runtime (infra/dao/impl)
- **Scenario:** getDocumentById maps a row; the Get_Document_ByID cursor returns d.format, but Java forces setFormat("QXP").
- **Java is (verified):** GetDocumentByIdDaoImpl.java:73 `doc.setFormat("QXP");` then :77 `String fileName = String.format("%s_%d.%s", doc.getPrefix(), doc.getId(), doc.getFormat());` and :80 `doc.setFilePoolPath(fileName);`. The cursor mapper (:69-82) reads id_document, nom, contenu, id_langue but never reads the `format` column.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Document.cs:155-159; QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:107-115` — Proxy_Document.cs:155-158 `__document = new Document(__odr["id_document"], __odr["nom"], __odr["format"], __odr["contenu"]);`. Document.cs:112 `_format = format;` :114 `_type = GetDocumentType(format);` :115 `_fileName = string.Format(FileNamePrefixePattern, _prefix, this.ID, format);` where FileNamePrefixePattern (:46) = "{0}_{1}.{2}". Note: format is used RAW (no .ToUpper) in _fileName; .ToUpper only inside GetDocumentType (:152).
- **⚠️ Corrected fix (use THIS, not the original):** In mapFromResultSet, read the cursor's format column and use it RAW (do not uppercase), matching .NET which builds _fileName from the unmodified format value. Fall back to "QXP" only when null/blank to preserve current behavior:

  String fmt = rs.getString("format");
  doc.setFormat((fmt != null && !fmt.isBlank()) ? fmt : "QXP");
