Keep prefix/fileName/filePoolPath derivation as-is (lines 74,77-80) so fileName becomes D_<id>.<rawformat>, mirroring .NET's string.Format("{0}_{1}.{2}", "D", id, format). Do NOT call .toUpperCase() on the stored format — .NET's Document._fileName (Document.cs:115) uses the raw DB value, so uppercasing would produce D_<id>.PDF when .NET produces D_<id>.pdf, re-introducing a fileName/pool-key divergence. The cursor already returns d.format (ora.txt:8968), so no SQL change is needed. The GetDocumentType mapping is N/A: DocumentDomain has no type field. Note the DAO builds fileName manually (line 77) and intentionally bypasses DocumentDomain.generateFileNames() which would lowercase the extension; that manual %s path correctly preserves raw case and should be kept.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java hardcodes "QXP" (line 73) and never reads the cursor's format column; .NET (Proxy_Document.cs:157) passes __odr["format"] into the Document ctor which propagates it into _format, _type, and _fileName. Cursor selects d.format (ora.txt:8968), so the column is available. The bug is real but latent: the sole caller (CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy:235) always passes idLastQxp (a QXP doc), so current impact is zero — LOW confirmed. The proposed fix is right to read the column but wrong to .toUpperCase(): .NET uses the raw format in _fileName (Document.cs:115, no ToUpper), so uppercasing would diverge fileName/pool key for non-uppercase DB values. Adjusted fix drops the uppercasing.

### [#86] When a store flag is set but the data list is empty, the QXP_DATA_STORAGE cleanup DELETE is skipped  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/EndRunBusiness.java:134-160`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Run where Store_Type includes SQL (or DOCUMENT) but sqlDataNamesValues/docDataNamesValues is empty (e.g. a re-run that previously stored rows but now produces none).
- **Java is (verified):** EndRunBusiness.java:138 `if (storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.SQL) && !run.getSqlDataNamesValues().isEmpty()) {`; :150 `if (storeType.hasFlag(StoreDataType.DOCUMENT) && !run.getDocDataNamesValues().isEmpty()) {`; InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.java:33 `if (names == null || names.length == 0) return;`
- **.NET is (verified):** `Proxy_Store.cs:90-102; ora.txt:3095-3103; Proxy_Run.cs:336-340` — Proxy_Store.cs:90-94 `if ((run.Properties.Store_Type & Store_Data_Type.SQL) == Store_Data_Type.SQL) { ... this.Insert_Data(run.Properties.ID_Suivi, run.ID, run.Fin_Generation, Store_Data_Type.SQL, __datas, false); }` (no emptiness check). ora.txt:3096 `DELETE FROM QXP_DATA_STORAGE WHERE ID_SUIVI = p_id_suivi AND DATA_TYPE = p_store_type AND ID_RUN = p_id_run;` runs BEFORE :3103 `IF (p_names(1) is not null) THEN`. Comment :3098-3101 documents eos.framework binding an empty collection as a 1-element {null} array.
- **Fix:** To restore .NET parity, ensure the proc (and thus its unconditional cleanup DELETE) is invoked whenever the store flag is set, even with an empty list. Two coordinated changes: (1) In EndRunBusiness.insertDataStorage remove the `&& !...isEmpty()` clauses so the call happens on the flag alone (still build arrays from the possibly-empty list). (2) In InsertDataStorageDaoImpl.insertData, replace the `if (names == null || names.length == 0) return;` short-circuit with logic that, on empty/null input, binds a single-element {null} array for p_names/p_values/p_descriptifs/p_infos (e.g. `new String[]{null}`), mirroring the eos.framework's empty-collection-as-{null} behavior documented in ora.txt:3098-3102. The proc will then run the DELETE (line 3096) and skip the insert because p_names(1) is null (line 3103). Alternatively, if changing array binding is undesirable, add a separate DAO method that calls the proc with the {null} sentinel specifically for the empty case. Keep it gated on run status != Error and on the flag, matching Proxy_Run.End_Run.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java adds two extra emptiness gates (EndRunBusiness lines 138/150 and DAO line 33) absent in .NET; .NET's Proxy_Store calls Insert_Data whenever the flag is set, and the proc unconditionally executes the cleanup DELETE before the p_names(1) null guard. So on a non-error run with the SQL/DOCUMENT flag set but an empty data list, .NET purges stale rows for that suivi/run/type while Java does not — a genuine divergence. The proposed fix (remove the !isEmpty() clauses; in the DAO bind a {null} single-element array for the four varchar arrays on empty/null input) correctly reproduces the eos.framework sentinel: the proc runs DELETE then skips insert because p_names(1) is null. Other arrays' lengths are irrelevant since the FOR loop is skipped. Severity LOW is right: the DELETE is a defensive idempotency net only reached when the same ID_RUN is reprocessed with a now-empty list.

### [#87] Audit END_STATUS string casing differs from .NET (UPPER_SNAKE vs PascalCase)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/AuditDaoImpl.java:63`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Every audit row. Java writes run.getStatus().name() -> 'GENERATED'/'ERROR'/'RUNNING'; .NET writes Run_Status.ToString() -> 'Generated'/'Error'/'Running'.
- **Java is (verified):** AuditDaoImpl.java:63 `.addValue("p_end_status", run.getStatus() != null ? run.getStatus().name() : null)`; RunStatus.java:12-15 `TO_GENERATE(1), GENERATED(2), ERROR(3), RUNNING(4);` -> .name() yields UPPER_SNAKE member names.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Audit.cs:102; BusinessObject/Run/Audit/Audit.cs:117; Enums/Run_Status.cs:15-27` — Proxy_Audit.cs:102 `.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_end_status", audit.EndStatus.ToString())`; Audit.cs:117 `public Run_Status EndStatus`; Run_Status.cs:15-27 `ToGenerate = 1, Generated = 2, Error = 3, Running = 4,` -> enum.ToString() yields PascalCase.
- **Fix:** Map RunStatus to the .NET enum-name string before binding p_end_status, so existing audit data and any external consumers stay consistent. E.g. add a method to RunStatus returning the PascalCase label (TO_GENERATE->"ToGenerate", GENERATED->"Generated", ERROR->"Error", RUNNING->"Running") and use `run.getStatus() != null ? run.getStatus().getNetStatusLabel() : null` at AuditDaoImpl.java:63. Confirm with stakeholders whether downstream reports rely on the exact .NET casing before changing; if no consumer depends on it, this can be deferred.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. Java AuditDaoImpl.java:63 persists run.getStatus().name() (GENERATED/ERROR/RUNNING/TO_GENERATE) into QXP_AUDIT_RUN.END_STATUS, while .NET Proxy_Audit.cs:102 persists Run_Status.ToString() (Generated/Error/Running/ToGenerate) since EndStatus is the Run_Status enum (Audit.cs:117, set from _run.Status at Audit.cs:57). The divergence is real and hit on every audit row; no mapping layer exists. The proposed fix (add a getNetStatusLabel() returning PascalCase labels and bind that) correctly reproduces .NET ToString() output, so it makes Java match .NET. Implementer must actually add the new method to RunStatus (it does not yet exist) — the call site snippet is otherwise valid. Severity LOW is correct: free-text VARCHAR2 audit column, no in-engine string consumer; only external reports filtering on historical .NET casing are affected. The separate Duration-unit divergence noted in the finding is out of scope for this fix.

### [#88] Audit message content differs from .NET buildAuditMessage  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/EndRunBusiness.java:104-111`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Every audit row's MESSAGE column.
- **Java is (verified):** EndRunBusiness.java:104-111: `private String buildAuditMessage(Run run) { List<RunError> errors = run.getErrors(); if (errors.isEmpty()) { return "Run " + run.getId() + " " + run.getStatus(); } return "Run " + run.getId() + " " + run.getStatus() + " - " + errors.size() + " error(s): " + errors.get(0).getMessage(); }`
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Run/Audit/Audit.cs:64-78; Errors.cs:116-138` — Audit.cs:68-77: `int __nbtask = 0; foreach (Task_Base __t in _run.Tasks.Values) { if(__t.Todo) __nbtask++; } __message.AppendFormat("Nb Tasks: {0}\nNb Errors: {1}", __nbtask, _run.Errors.Count); __message.Append(_run.Errors.ToString());`  ||  Errors.cs:118-137: foreach err -> Bloquante:"\nErreur Bloquante: "+msg / Critique:"\nErreur Critique: "+msg / else:"\nErreur : "+msg
- **Fix:** If audit-message parity matters for downstream log/forensic comparison with the legacy system, reproduce the .NET format in buildAuditMessage: count run.getTasks() entries that are "todo", then build "Nb Tasks: " + nbTodo + "\nNb Errors: " + errors.size(), then append per error a "\nErreur Bloquante: "/"\nErreur Critique: "/"\nErreur : " prefix (mapped from RunError category to Error_Type) plus the message — mirroring Errors.ToString(). Keep the DAO-side truncation to the column width. If free-text parity is not required, leave as-is and add a one-line comment noting the intentional message-format change versus .NET Audit.buildAuditMessage so future reviewers do not re-flag it.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified from source. Java (EndRunBusiness.java:104-111) emits "Run {id} {STATUS}" / "...- {n} error(s): {firstMsg}". .NET (Audit.cs:75-76 + Errors.cs:116-138) emits "Nb Tasks: {nbTodo}\nNb Errors: {count}" then EVERY error as "\nErreur Bloquante:/Critique:/ : {msg}". Real content divergence: Java drops the todo-task count and includes only the first error message. Fix direction is correct and feasible — all dependencies exist: Run.getTasks() is Map<Integer,TaskBase>, TaskBase.isTodo() (TaskBase.java:22), RunError.getCategory() codes match Error_Type exactly (1=Unspecified→"Erreur : ",2=Critique→"Erreur Critique: ",3=Bloquante→"Erreur Bloquante: "). Concrete parity impl: int nbTodo=0; for(TaskBase t:run.getTasks().values()) if(t.isTodo()) nbTodo++; StringBuilder sb=new StringBuilder("Nb Tasks: "+nbTodo+"\nNb Errors: "+errors.size()); for(RunError e:errors){String p=e.getCategory()==RunError.BLOQUANTE?"\nErreur Bloquante: ":e.getCategory()==RunError.CRITIQUE?"\nErreur Critique: ":"\nErreur : "; sb.append(p).append(e.getMessage());} return sb.toString(). Severity LOW confirmed: value flows only into the write-only free-text p_message audit column (Proxy_Audit/AuditDao), never SELECTed or parsed by either codebase; all structured audit columns preserved. Fix's offered alternative (leave + comment) is also legitimate given free-text nature.

### [#89] NullPointerException if dateEcheance is null when inserting a generated document  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:61`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Successful run whose runProperties.dateEcheance is null (LocalDate, not loaded/optional) and a final QXP or PDF exists to insert.
- **Java is (verified):** InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:61 `params.put("p_date_document", java.sql.Date.valueOf(dateEcheance));` (unguarded; p_date_document declared Types.DATE at line 46). RunProperties.java:30 `private LocalDate dateEcheance;` (no default, nullable). EndRunBusiness.java:99 success path passes `run.getRunProperties().getDateEcheance()`. Sibling GetDocumentDaoImpl.java:67 guards: `dateEcheance != null ? java.sql.Date.valueOf(dateEcheance) : null`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Document.cs:234-239,267; Run_Properties.cs:217; Proxy_Run.cs:120` — Proxy_Document.cs:238 `, DateTime date_echeance` and :267 `.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_date_document", date_echeance)`. Run_Properties.cs:217 `public DateTime Date_Echeance`. Proxy_Run.cs:120 `__run_Properties.Date_Echeance = __odr["date_echeance"];` — DateTime is a non-nullable value type, so no null can reach Insert_Document.
- **Fix:** Mirror the sibling DAOs: replace `params.put("p_date_document", java.sql.Date.valueOf(dateEcheance));` at InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:61 with a null-safe form, e.g. `params.put("p_date_document", dateEcheance != null ? java.sql.Date.valueOf(dateEcheance) : null);`. The p_date_document parameter is already declared as Types.DATE (line 46), so a null maps cleanly. This matches GetDocumentDaoImpl.java:67's guard and keeps consistent null handling across all dateEcheance consumers without changing behavior for the normal (populated) case.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified by reopening all sources. Java: unguarded java.sql.Date.valueOf(dateEcheance) at line 61 NPEs when the nullable LocalDate field is null; the success branch in EndRunBusiness reaches it. .NET: Date_Echeance is a non-nullable DateTime, so Insert_Document(DateTime date_echeance) can never receive null. The Java mapper introduced null-tolerance .NET never had — a port-specific NPE. The proposed fix mirrors GetDocumentDaoImpl.java:67's existing guard, compiles cleanly (p_date_document already Types.DATE), and leaves the normal populated case unchanged. Minor caveat: .NET would default to DateTime.MinValue (not null) if unset, but date_echeance is effectively always populated for runs reaching finalization (UNIQUE constraint / doc identity), so behavior is identical in practice. SOUND; LOW severity correct.

### [#90] Insert_Document uses procedure-column metadata access (unlike AuditDao) — fragile RETURN/param binding for an Oracle FUNCTION  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:36-53, 69-70`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Every generated-document insert. SimpleJdbcCall.withFunctionName('Insert_Document') with declared SqlOutParameter('RETURN') but WITHOUT withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess(), while AuditDaoImpl deliberately disables metadata access.
- **Java is (verified):** InsertDocumentDaoImpl.java:36-39 `new SimpleJdbcCall(dataSource).withCatalogName("QXP_PK_RUN").withFunctionName("Insert_Document").declareParameters(` — NO `.withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess()`. Line 40 `new SqlOutParameter("RETURN", Types.NUMERIC)` + 12 SqlParameters (41-52). Line 70 `Number idDoc = (Number) result.get("RETURN");` line 75 `return idDoc != null ? idDoc.intValue() : Integer.MIN_VALUE;`. Contrast AuditDaoImpl.java:35 `.withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess()`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Document.cs:234-295; ora.txt:9213` — Proxy_Document.cs:258 `new QXPDataOracle.OraParameter("p_id_doc", OracleDbType.Int32, ParameterDirection.ReturnValue)` then 262-273 explicit AddParameterToSqlParamList for all 12 IN params; line 281 `new SQLRequest(CommandType.StoredProcedure, __sql, __oraParameters)`; line 287 `__id = __id_doc.ReturnValue;`. ora.txt:9213 `FUNCTION Insert_Document (...) RETURN NUMBER`.
- **Fix:** Add .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() to the SimpleJdbcCall in InsertDocumentDaoImpl (after .withFunctionName("Insert_Document")), so the call binds solely from the explicit declareParameters list — matching AuditDaoImpl and the .NET explicit-parameter approach, making the RETURN/param resolution deterministic across drivers. The existing declareParameters list already covers RETURN plus all 12 IN params, so no further binding changes are needed; keep the result.get("RETURN") null->MIN_VALUE guard as-is.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple verified. Java omits withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() on the Insert_Document FUNCTION call while sibling AuditDaoImpl uses it. .NET (Proxy_Document.cs:258-273) binds every parameter explicitly with no metadata/DeriveParameters: explicit ReturnValue p_id_doc + 12 named IN params via CommandType.StoredProcedure. ora.txt confirms FUNCTION RETURN NUMBER with the same 12 params. Fix (add .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() after withFunctionName) makes Spring bind solely from the already-complete declareParameters list (RETURN as SqlOutParameter first + 12 in order), matching .NET explicit binding and the sibling DAO; compiles, standard pattern. Finding's cited .NET lines 234-295 match exactly (method decl spans 234-239). LOW confirmed: explicit declareParameters present and Spring reconciles by name even with metadata on, so null->MIN_VALUE corruption of ID_DOC_QXP/PDF/DOC is driver/environment-conditional hardening, not a guaranteed failure.

### [#91] End_Run/Update_Status_Run/Insert_Run_Errors rely on JDBC metadata access (no withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `infra/dao/impl/EndRunDaoImpl.java:32-45, 65-74, 91-98`
- **Area:** end-persist (EndRunBusiness + EndRun/InsertDocument/Audit/InsertDataStorage DAOs)
- **Scenario:** Every finalization call. These three SimpleJdbcCalls do not disable procedure-column metadata access.
- **Java is (verified):** EndRunDaoImpl.java:32-35 `new SimpleJdbcCall(dataSource).withCatalogName("QXP_PK_RUN").withProcedureName("End_Run").declareParameters(...)` — no withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess(); same at :65-68 (Update_Status_Run) and :91-94 (Insert_Run_Errors). Contrast AuditDaoImpl.java:32-35 which has `.withProcedureName("InsertAuditRun").withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess().declareParameters(...)`.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Proxy/Proxy_Run.cs:292-323; ora.txt:9302-9314,9350-9362,9447-9454` — Proxy_Run.cs:292-296 `__oraParameters.AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_id_run", __run.ID).AddParameterToSqlParamList("p_run_status", (int)__run.Status)...`; :306 `__sql = string.Concat(PACKAGE, "End_Run")`; :323 `new QXPDataOracle.SQLRequest(CommandType.StoredProcedure, __sql, __oraParameters)`. .NET binds every parameter explicitly by name, never deriving from server metadata.
- **Fix:** Add .withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() to all three SimpleJdbcCall builders in EndRunDaoImpl (between .withProcedureName(...) and .declareParameters(...) at lines ~34, ~67, ~93), matching AuditDaoImpl.java:35 and the other 13 DAOs. This makes parameter binding fully deterministic from the explicit declareParameters declarations and consistent with the rest of the DAO layer. Note: the special-type params in Insert_Run_Errors (OracleTypes.ARRAY with named SQL types QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY/NUMBERARRAY) are already fully declared, so disabling metadata access is safe there too. Optionally also extract these into constructor-initialized fields like AuditDaoImpl/RunStartUpdateDaoImpl do, rather than rebuilding per call, but that is a separate refactor.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified all three claims. Java EndRunDaoImpl omits withoutProcedureColumnMetaDataAccess() on all three SimpleJdbcCalls; 14 of 17 DAOs (incl. structurally identical AuditDaoImpl in the same end-persist flow) include it — only EndRun/InsertDocument/InsertDataStorage omit it. ora.txt confirms the declareParameters exactly match the procedure signatures in name/order/type, including the named ARRAY types QXP_PK_COMMON.VARCHARARRAY/NUMBERARRAY, so disabling metadata access is safe. .NET (Proxy_Run.End_Run) uses ODP.NET fully-explicit named binding with CommandType.StoredProcedure and no metadata derivation; the fix makes Spring rely solely on the explicit declarations, matching .NET's deterministic binding and the rest of the DAO layer. Insertion points (after withProcedureName, before declareParameters) are valid and won't break compilation. Conditional hardening only triggered under specific driver/schema-privilege conditions; params fully named/typed — LOW is correct.

### [#92] changeDocument does not recompute fileFullPath; the absolute path becomes stale after a gabarit swap  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/DocumentDomain.java:222-227 (changeDocument)`
- **Area:** enums-modes
- **Scenario:** After a process step, QxpsCallerBusiness.updateGabaritAfterStep calls gabarit.changeDocument(newName, newPoolPath, newData). fileName and filePoolPath are updated but fileFullPath still points at the previous gabarit name.
- **Java is (verified):** DocumentDomain.java:222-227: public void changeDocument(String newFileName, String newFilePoolPath, byte[] newData) { this.fileName = newFileName; this.filePoolPath = newFilePoolPath; this.data = newData; purgeXmlAndProject(); } — fileFullPath (declared line 49) is never updated.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:172-188` — public void Change_Document(string newName) { _fileName = newName; _filePoolPath = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPath(_fileName); _fileFullPath = QXPS_File_Manager.GetPoolPathAbsolute(_fileName); _xml = null; _project = null; _data = QXPInteropQuarkServer.QXPS_Helper.GetFileData(_filePoolPath); ... }
- **Fix:** In DocumentDomain.changeDocument, also refresh the absolute path so all three location fields stay consistent (matching .NET's _fileFullPath = GetPoolPathAbsolute). Since the domain stays I/O-free, have the caller pass the precomputed absolute path: add a newFileFullPath parameter and set this.fileFullPath = newFileFullPath. Then in QxpsCallerBusiness.updateGabaritAfterStep compute it via run.getRunProperties().getPoolPathAbsolute(newGabaritName, documentPoolBasePath) (same helper already used in GetGabaritBusiness.preparePaths) and pass it in. Alternatively, if a parameter change is undesirable, set this.fileFullPath = null in changeDocument so a stale value can never be read. Given it is latent, this is optional hardening for .NET parity rather than an urgent fix.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed. .NET Change_Document sets all three location fields including _fileFullPath = GetPoolPathAbsolute(_fileName) (Document.cs:176); Java changeDocument updates only fileName/filePoolPath/data and purges XML/project, leaving fileFullPath stale (DocumentDomain.java:222-227, field at :49). The proposed fix (add newFileFullPath param; compute in updateGabaritAfterStep via run.getRunProperties().getPoolPathAbsolute(newGabaritName, poolBasePath) and set this.fileFullPath) faithfully reproduces .NET: the helper exists (RunProperties.java:86) and is already used identically at GetGabaritBusiness.java:92; poolBasePath is available in QxpsCallerBusiness (used at :109); newGabaritName equals the fileName passed to changeDocument, matching .NET passing _fileName. Compiles and matches behavior. Severity LOW is correct: latent — the only caller mutates run.getGabarit(); render reads getFilePoolPath() (updated), and DocumentTaskProcessStrategy's getFileFullPath() reads are on distinct task sub-documents, never the swapped gabarit, so no live read of the stale value today.

### [#93] Mode_Degrade size check is missing the QXP-type guard present in .NET Evaluate_Mode_Degrade (DocumentDomain has no equivalent method)  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `domain/Run.java:159 (mode-degrade size check)`
- **Area:** enums-modes
- **Scenario:** A gabarit whose format is not QXP but whose bytes exceed sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft would be put into mode-degrade.
- **Java is (verified):** Run.java:159 `if (this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) {` — no QXP/format guard; DocumentDomain.java has only getData(42)/getFormat(44)/getRatioSizeBox(248), no evaluateModeDegrade method.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/BusinessObject/Document/Document.cs:228-239; Run_Base.cs:915-926; Document.cs:163` — Document.cs:231 `if (this.Type == Document_Type.QXP && QXPFrk.Validation.IsSet(this.Data))` then :233 `return (_data.Length > SizeLimitBeforeFailSoft);` else :237 `return false;`. Type==QXP derives from format via Document.cs:163 `case "QXP": __type = Document_Type.QXP;`. Run_Base.cs:921 delegates `this.Gabarit.Mode_Degrade`.
- **Fix:** Add the QXP-format guard to mirror .NET Evaluate_Mode_Degrade, preferably encapsulated on DocumentDomain to match .NET ownership: add `public boolean evaluateModeDegrade(long sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft) { return "QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(format) && data != null && data.length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft; }` (the `data != null && length > 0` mirrors .NET IsSet(this.Data) and removes the latent NPE on getData()). Then change Run.java:159 to `if (this.gabarit.evaluateModeDegrade(sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft)) { ... }`. If kept inline in Run, at minimum: `"QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(this.gabarit.getFormat()) && this.gabarit.getData() != null && this.gabarit.getData().length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft`.
- **Reconciler note:** Triple confirmed from source. Java Run.java:159 puts ANY oversized gabarit into mode-degrade; .NET only does so for QXP-type documents with non-empty Data. Real divergence: a non-QXP (e.g. PDF) gabarit exceeding the limit is wrongly degraded in Java, plus a latent NPE on getData(). The proposed fix encapsulates evaluateModeDegrade on DocumentDomain with `"QXP".equalsIgnoreCase(format) && data != null && data.length > sizeLimitBeforeFailSoft`, mirroring .NET Type==QXP + IsSet(Data) and matching the already-ported getRatioSizeBox pattern (DocumentDomain.java:249). This makes Java behave identically. LOW severity is correct since gabarits are normally QXP, making the path rarely hit in practice.

### [#94] Misleading RunError severity comment: '1=Bloquante, 2=Critique, 3=Warning' contradicts the correct mapping  — ✅ SOUND
- **File (Java):** `business/ProcessSqlBusiness.java:36`
- **Area:** enums-modes
- **Scenario:** Maintainer reads the comment and assumes 1=Bloquante/3=Warning when constructing RunError categories.
- **Java is (verified):** ProcessSqlBusiness.java:36 "/** RunError categories: 1=Bloquante, 2=Critique, 3=Warning. */"; line 37 "private static final int CRITIQUE = 2;". RunError.java:22-24 "UNSPECIFIED = 1; CRITIQUE = 2; BLOQUANTE = 3;" with javadoc noting numeric value is load-bearing/persisted.
- **.NET is (verified):** `QXP.Engine.Core/Enums/Error_Type.cs:16,20,24` — "Unspecified = 1," (l.16); "Critique = 2," (l.20); "Bloquante = 3," (l.24). No "Warning" member exists.
- **Fix:** Correct the comment at ProcessSqlBusiness.java:36 to match the enum, e.g. "/** RunError categories (see Error_Type / RunError): 1=Unspecified (Non spécifiée), 2=Critique, 3=Bloquante. */". Optionally, drop the duplicated local CRITIQUE constant and reference RunError.CRITIQUE directly to remove the parallel definition entirely.
- **Reconciler note:** Verified the triple. Java comment at line 36 mislabels code 1 as Bloquante (should be Unspecified) and code 3 as Warning (not even an enum member; should be Bloquante). .NET Error_Type authoritatively maps 1=Unspecified, 2=Critique, 3=Bloquante, mirrored correctly by RunError.java. The constant actually used (CRITIQUE=2) is correct, so runtime/persistence behavior is unaffected — purely a misleading comment. The proposed fix rewrites the comment to match the enum (and optionally drops the duplicated local CRITIQUE in favor of RunError.CRITIQUE); it is comment-only, cannot break compilation, and accurately reflects .NET. LOW severity is correct (cosmetic, no behavioral impact). SOUND.

## WITHDRAWN (verified NOT real — do not fix)

### [#3] addRunBlocs reads the child's TEMPLATE gabarit instead of the generated final QXP  (was CRITICAL)
- **File:** `service/task/impl/CompartimentTaskProcessStrategy.java:322-323, 332`
- **Why withdrawn:** Triple verified at source. The finding's premise that gabarit=original template (no dynamic data) is false in this port: the gabarit DocumentDomain is mutated in place to the post-modification SaveAs output (updateGabaritAfterStep:264), which is exactly what render reads back. getProject takes a server-side document NAME (getXPressDOM); the DF_ finalQxp has filePoolPath==null and is never pooled under its DF_ name, so the proposed fix yields QxpProject.EMPTY and breaks every generate-mode child. .NET reaches the same rendered content via Final_QXP.FilePoolPath (DF_<id>.QXP) which exists server-side there. Java's current line 332 produces the equivalent rendered structure. Java already matches .NET behavior; the proposed fix is wrong and regressing. WITHDRAWN.
- **Skeptic:** Verified in source. Java mutates the single gabarit in-place: QxpsCallerBusiness.updateGabaritAfterStep:264 calls gabarit.changeDocument(name,poolPath,data) after every modify step, so gabarit.getFilePoolPath() points at the final modified server doc; render(:241) literal-fetches that same doc into finalQxp. So getProject(gabarit.getFilePoolPath()) parses the final structure WITH dynamic data — equivalent to .NET Final_QXP.QXPProject. .NET works because Document ctor sets _filePoolPath (Document.cs:118) AND QXPProject triggers QXPS_File_Manager.Get_Project(doc,addFile=true):213 which uploads DF_ bytes before getXPressDOM. Java's finalQxp has filePoolPath==null (generateFileNames:275-279 sets only fileName) and getProject never uploads. The proposed fix getProject(finalQxp.getFilePoolPath()) passes null → EMPTY → EmptyRunChildProject for every child. Current code is correct; leave as-is.

### [#85] Unknown-task-type warning logs the coerced enum (SYSTEM) instead of the raw code, hiding the real unmapped value  (was LOW)
- **File:** `mapper/TaskMapper.java:60`
- **Why withdrawn:** Triple verified from source. Java fromCode collapses code 0 and all unmapped codes to SYSTEM (TaskTypeEnum.java:28); the default branch logs taskType which is always literally SYSTEM (TaskMapper.java:60), never the raw ID_TYPE_TACHE. .NET Get_Task (Proxy_Task.cs:226-260) has the same 5 cases, NO default and NO logging, returning null silently. Java's functional behavior (return null, task skipped) is faithful to .NET. The misleading warning is a Java-only added line with no .NET counterpart, so there is no parity divergence — WITHDRAWN as a defect. The proposed fix is a valid optional diagnostic improvement but does not constitute matching .NET behavior (which logs nothing), so fixCorrect=false. Severity LOW confirmed: troubleshooting-only, zero functional impact.
